# 31. The meaning spine: design decisions and open choices

The design note of the consistency spine (`ochr/core/lean/Ochr/Meaning/`, lean_lib `OchrMeaning`): the Lean definitions and lemma statements of docs/18's main theorem against the real checker types (`Value`, `Term`, `MState`), for a swarm of provers to fill. Kept short and current; decisions in the order they were made. Questions of docs/18 are cited by name with their number in brackets, since the numbers have moved before.

**The current status is the section "WHERE THINGS STAND" below (2026-11-21, late night: the proof line `spine` at d6d83487, the function columns on `spine-lead14` and `spine-model5`), and the plan is docs/18 on main (64bc732c): a fundamental lemma of the model over the strict run, which is proved (D105), with its seam to the simulation ruled and built (D106); the simulation, in a form that held unchanged (D107), proved end to end with no assumed fact on two narrow columns and on every term of a small fragment (D112, D114 to D122; `frag_run_free`), and for the first function, `I`, assuming two facts about the checker read and judged true (D123 to D126); and the existence of a data model, whose statement was repaired and has two instances (D110). The paragraph that follows is the status of 2026-11-09, kept as the record of how the milestones were laid out.**

Status (2026-11-09, after the implementers' first reports): milestone 1 (the meaning of a state) done and reviewed by the lead; D8 revised and D10 (the per-future run) added after Lean-checked counterexamples from m2-proofs and data-generic (see the implementer ledger at the end of this section); milestone 2 (the reference run) built: `run` covers every term form the checker runs, the knot with `Value.mean` is tied (`codeMeaning`, `meanV_eq_mean` stated), and the sealed-substitution and congruence lemmas are stated (`Lemmas.lean`); what remains of it is the generic `DataModel` and the program meaning, plus the proofs of its three stated lemmas; milestone 4 (the assembly) is stated in `Consistency.lean` (`accepted_resolves`, `defn_member`, `false_means_empty`, `falseConst_ty`, `consistency_rel`, `exists_dataModel`, `consistencyBounded_assembled`; all `sorry`); milestone 3 (the step-lemma skeleton) is restructured (D17): 73 statements centralised in `Steps/Statements.lean` as fuel-indexed `Prop`s, bundled by `Steps/IH.lean`'s `StepsIH`, each area file proving `StepsIH fuel → stmt (fuel+1)`, `Steps/Knot.lean` closing the induction on fuel; every statement `sorry`; it started with `Steps/Borrow.lean` (the shape of a step lemma, and the eight borrow-primitive statements), `Steps/Subst.lean` (substitution of a loan or a refinement, re-normalisation, `refine`: 8 statements) and `Steps/Eq.lean` (`mkEqM`, `conv`, `convPi`, `convFn`, `rewriteGoal`, types future-free: 6 statements); milestone 4 not started.

## Milestone 1: the meaning of a state

### D1. Typed elements, not ZFC's untyped tuples (`Sem.lean`)

docs/18's model is ZFC: a constructor value means a tagged tuple, a function a set of pairs, all in one `V_κ`. Lean has no analogue of a small universe closed under its own function space: an inductive type cannot hold functions on itself (Cantor, or simply positivity), and a universe of first-order trees whose leaves are elements of arbitrary small types is one Lean level too big to be its own leaf type. Both were tried on paper before writing (`Sem.lean`'s header records the argument). The one encoding that keeps `Π` small is the typed one of `Ochr/Model/Slice.lean`: a type means a Lean type holding only its good elements, and a function value means a Lean function on the carriers of its domain and codomain.

So the semantic domain is:

- `TyM`, the meaning of a type value: a proposition (`prop P`), a carrier at Lean level 0, 1 or 2 (`ty0`, `ty1`, `ty2`), a universe `univ l` (`⟦sort l⟧`, kept symbolic so its elements, type values, are recognised by their level), or `err`.
- `Sem`, the meaning of a value, *tagged by its carrier*: `el0 (A : Type) (a : A)`, `el1`, `el2`; `ty T` (a type used as a value); `proof` (`•`); `moved` (`⊥`); `ctor ty c fs` (see D3); `err`.
- Membership `s ∈ₛ T` is the tag's equality with `T`'s carrier (`Sem.mem`): `el0 A a ∈ₛ ty0 B` iff `A = B`; `proof ∈ₛ prop P` iff `P`; `ty T ∈ₛ univ l` iff `T.level = l`.

Consequences, each a departure from docs/18 the provers must know:

- **Convertible types must have equal Lean carriers.** Conversion soundness for types says `⟦T⟧ = ⟦T'⟧` as `TyM`, i.e. Lean type equality of carriers. It is provable where the plan's (V1)–(V9) say conversion holds: the meaning is a function of the components (Π by `funext` + congruence, `And(True, P) ≡ P` by `propext`, a type function's call by the stabilisation lemma of `Slice.lean` §4). It is *not* decidable, and the semantics uses it classically (`Sem.as` decodes an element by comparing tags with `Classical.propDecidable`), so everything in `Ochr.Meaning` is `noncomputable`.
- **Constructor values' recorded parameters are read, and missing ones are read off the position's type** (D8 below), since the carrier of `D(ā)` depends on `ā`.
- **A borrow type means its content type** (`⟦&A⟧ = ⟦A⟧`): a place holding `borrow_ℓ w` means `⟦w⟧` and its declared type is `&T`, so membership needs the content's carrier. The model therefore validates `Eq(Type₀, &A, A)`. That is harmless only if the checker never *refutes* it, which (WF8) in `WF.lean` pins down: `&` occurs only at the top of a stored type, an abstract value's type or the goal (RULES §1, D48 (2)), never inside a value or deeper in a type, so no reachable state holds the type value `Eq(Type₀, &A, A)` at all. The step lemma for `evalCore (.ref A)` (which rejects a nested `&`) and for `evalCtor`/`tind` are where (WF8) is preserved.

Rejected alternatives: (i) a `fn` constructor storing the domain and codomain as `TyM`s with the function on their carriers: needs `El : TyM → Type` inside the constructor of `Sem`, induction–recursion; (ii) a universe of codes with `El`: the same, or a syntactic universe, which is not the set model; (iii) Mathlib's `ZFSet`: faithful to ZFC and extensional, but a new dependency on a project that imports Lean core only, and without inaccessibles `Type₀` has no set to mean. (iii) is the one to revisit if type-equality casts make the step lemmas unbearable.

### D2. Levels: bounded at `Type₁` (universes in Lean [Q1], `Bounded.lean`)

Lean's universes cannot be indexed by a number, so the model fixes the levels a program may write. The statement to mechanise is `Ochr.Consistency.consistencyBounded`: `consistency` with the extra hypothesis `LevelsBelow 2 q`, no `sort l` with `l > 2` written in `q` (`Prop`, `Type₀`, `Type₁`). Types the checker *computes* reach one level higher (`Type₂`, the type of `Type₁` and of `Π(A : Type₁). Type₁`), which `TyM.univ 3` supports as a universe whose elements are only type values; nothing quantifies over it, so it needs no carrier. Carriers: `ty0 : Type` for `Type₀`, `ty1 : Type 1` for `Type₁` (`Prop` and `Type₀` themselves, Π over them), `ty2 : Type 2` for `Type₂`-sorted Π-types that a parameter can have without `Type₂` being written (`f : Π(A : Type₁). Type₁`). `Sem : Type 3`.

Every example in the suite satisfies the bound (`Prelude.decls` by `#guard`; no example writes `Type₂`), and `Type₁` is used (`Functions.lean`: `Pow (X : Type) : Type₁ := Π(a : X). Prop`), so the bound cannot be 1. Raising it is one constructor in each of `TyM`, `Sem` and the carrier functions; the uniform `consistency` is not a target of the mechanisation.

### D3. `⊥` is `moved`, a definite value; partly moved data is an untyped tuple (D84)

Under D84, `⊥` means "definitely moved", the same at the generic call and at every instance; `eq(⊥, ⊥)` is `True` and `eq(⊥, v)` is `False`. In the model `Sem.moved` is a constructor equal only to itself (`Sanity.moved_definite`), and `Eq`'s meaning is equality in `Sem`.

A constructor value some field of which was moved out (`Pair(⊥, 5)`, which every match that moves a field out leaves) lies in no carrier, so it cannot be `el0`; it means `ctor ty c fs`, an untyped tuple, and a whole, well-typed constructor value is `el0` of its carrier (`DataModel.ctorM`; since D25 an ill-typed one is a tuple too, not `err`). Equality then agrees with the checker's injectivity and disjointness rules in every case: two partly moved tuples are equal iff their fields are; a whole and a partly moved value are never equal (`eq(⊥, v) = False`).

The order `⊑` (`Sem.le`): `moved` below everything, `ctor` componentwise, equality otherwise. Under D84 it is **not** an information order on observations (`moved` is definite); it is the proof device the main theorem needs: the checker's state after an early end (`Pick`) has the holder `⊥` where the reference keeps the borrow, and at a whole leaf `⊑` is equality (`Sem.le_eq_of_whole`). The lead's note that the information order "remains only for borrows ended early and similar over-approximations that are never observed" is recorded as an obligation on the step lemmas: no observation reads a position an early end forgot (borrow holders are never owners, so `Obs.footprint` never lists them).

Membership for a possibly partly moved binding is `DataModel.pmem`: `moved`, or some whole member with parts moved out (`MovedOf`). As first defined, through `Sem.le`, it was false of every partly moved tuple (D24).

### D4. The data model is an interface (`Data.lean`); the construction is milestone 2's

User inductive types need a generic small carrier per declaration and parameter tuple, with dependent fields (telescopes), Fording equations, type-function fields and nesting (`Rose(A) := Node(v : A, kids : List(Rose(A)))` is in the suite). The spine fixes the interface the step lemmas use, `DataModel`: `car`, `pack`, `tag`, `fields`, `rank`, and the least-fixed-point laws (`tag_pack`, `fields_pack`, `pack_fields`: every element is a constructor applied to its fields; `pack_isSome`: packing succeeds iff each field is a member of its field type at the earlier fields; `rank_lt`: recursive fields have smaller rank, for guarded recursion). `fieldTy`, the meaning of a field's declared type at parameters and earlier fields, is part of the interface because the laws are stated with it; the assembly must show it agrees with the reference run's reading of the declaration.

Planned construction (milestone 2, the model beyond the slices [Q2]): the stage construction of `Slice.lean` §4 on carriers, `stage (k+1) ps := Σ c, telescope of field carriers with recursive occurrences at stage k`, by recursion on `k` *as a function of the parameters*, so that non-uniform parameters (D71) cost nothing, and the carrier as the colimit (`Σ k, stage k ps` modulo the stage embeddings, a `Quot`). Nesting needs the nested earlier inductive's stages to be re-run at the new parameter; whether to translate nesting to mutual recursion (as Lean's kernel does) or to interleave the stages is the one open choice. `Prop`-sorted declarations are the truncation `Nonempty (car d ps)` of the same fixed point, so `False`, `True` and `And` need no separate clause.

The library's `Pair`, `False`, `True`, `And` are modelled by hand (`libModel`; its laws are left to provers). `Nat` and `Unit` are not declarations and mean `Nat` and `Unit` directly.

### data-generic: choices (`DataGeneric.lean`)

The generic construction of D4, branch `spine-data-generic`. The choices it made, and the obligations they leave the assembly:

- **The input is a signature of codes** (`Sig`), one per declaration in program order: the constructors' arities, the sort, which parameters are nestable, and for each field a code of its declared type (`PCode`) as a function of the parameters and the earlier fields. A code is `ext T` (a closed small type: everything mentioning neither the declaration nor a nestable parameter, so equations, type-function fields, `Nat`, and earlier inductives at fixed arguments), `par j` (nestable parameter `j`), `self fx tys` (an occurrence of the declaration: fixed parameters `fx`, and a type code `tys j` at each position, used at nestable positions, so non-uniform nested recursion like `NL(Pair(A, A))` is covered), or `nest e fx tys` (an occurrence of the earlier declaration `e`, whose type codes may contain the declaration). The assembly reads codes off the field-type terms and must show that a code read at the colimit (`interp`) is the reference run's reading of the term. That needs docs/18 (I1)–(I6) and three well-formedness facts: type codes only at nestable positions; `ext (tyOf s)` at positions taking a fixed parameter `s`; and the field-type term reading only earlier *closed* fields.
- **Masking.** A field's code sees the earlier fields through `mask`: a closed field's meaning, `err` for a recursive, nested or parameter-typed one. This is what makes the telescope independent of the stage, because a recursive field's meaning differs between stage `k` and the colimit. It is sound because no accepted field type reads such a field. Reading one would need the declaration, or a nestable parameter, written outside a whole field type, which (I1), (I2) and nestability forbid (`SelfEq`, `Loop` are rejected).
- **Nesting is read through the earlier declaration's own carrier, not translated to mutual recursion.** The run reads `List(Rose(A))` as `dm.tyM List [ty (dm.tyM Rose [A])]`, so the field's type must be `List`'s carrier at `Rose`'s carrier. A mutual translation gives an auxiliary type that is only isomorphic to it. Identifying the two would mean defining `List`'s carrier at a `Rose`-carrier parameter by recovering `A` from the carrier, which is D8's `paramsOf` problem again (carriers do not determine parameters). So stage `k + 1` of `Rose` holds `List`'s carrier at `Rose`'s stage `k`, and the colimit argument needs `List` to be functorial in its nestable parameters and finitary: an element over the colimit comes from some stage. That is the interface `NModel` (map, raw meaning relative to the leaves, a predicate on leaves, `lift`, `directed`). The construction *produces* one for every declaration (`nmodel`), so the program's declarations are modelled in order (`progModel`, `progEnv`), and `progEnv_car` says that a later `nest i` reads declaration `i`'s own carrier.
- **Leaf environments and blanking.** A carrier is built at parameters `ps` and a leaf environment `Z : Nat → TyM` (the nestable parameters' types). Codes see `ps` with the nestable positions blanked to `err`, so the carrier depends on a nestable parameter only through its `par` leaves, and is functorial in it with no hypothesis on the codes. The data model takes `Z = envOf ps`.
- **The colimit's equality is raw meaning, relative to a raw meaning of the leaves.** Members of different stages are identified when their raw meanings (`sraw`: constructor and fields' raw meanings, `Sem.ctor` trees) agree, read with the canonical leaf raw. A nested field's raw meaning reads its leaves through the outer declaration's raw meaning, so `NModel.raw` takes the leaf raw as an argument. The master lemma `sraw_rel` (equal raws under related leaf raws stay equal under any related pair) makes this well defined on the quotient and coherent under nesting.
- **`Prop`-sorted declarations** are the same fixed point. An occurrence of one stores `PLift (Nonempty _)`, so a field's meaning is `•` and `pack (fields x) = x` holds. The leaves of the hidden witness are reached existentially (`allLB`), which is what `lift` and `directed` need for a proposition nested in one with a recursive constructor.
- **Field types are cut to `small`** (`Type₀` carriers and propositions; anything else becomes `err`, which has no members), so that `pack` succeeds exactly on members (`gPack_isSome`). This agrees with the run wherever (I6) holds. libModel does the same with its parameters.
- **The rank is the first stage** (`crank`). The rank law is proved on the core carrier (`crank_self`): a field whose code is `self fx tys`, read at that occurrence, has a smaller rank. The interface's `rank_lt` uses the declared-occurrence form the lead adopted (the carrier-equality form is false for `Pair(Pair(Unit,Unit),Unit)`). `recArgs` returns the occurrence's parameters (`occFrom`: the fixed parameters, with the type codes' meanings at the nestable positions) only when the interface carrier there is literally the core carrier the field lives in. That guard is classical, and `gRecArgs_wf` is the bridge: for a well-formed occurrence (fixed parameters blank at the nestable positions, type codes `ext (tyOf s)` at the others) the guard holds. Nested paths (`Rose` → `List` → `Rose`) are not covered by `rank_lt`; that is the semantic [Rec]'s documented gap.
- **`fieldData`, `paramData` and `paramTy` are passed through from the signature.** They are the assembly's readings of the declared types (the structured hints of D8 revised), unused by the construction. `pack_congr` is not part of the interface (dropped with D8's structured hints).
- **Decision (spine, 2026-11-10): parameters carry their own structured hint.** `FieldsFaithful`'s data part is unprovable at a parameter-typed field (`Pair`'s `fst : A` at `A := List(Nat)`): the run's hint carries `("List", [⟦Nat⟧])` from the parameter's structured type, while `fieldData d ps` sees only the carrier, and recovering a declaration from a carrier is D8's problem (false for two declarations of equal carrier). So the parameter hint is to be recorded, not recovered: a parameter's `TyS` keeps its `data` (`Option (String × List TyS)`, D8(c)), and `fieldData` at a `par j` field returns parameter `j`'s data. The run already threads structured hints for fields; the parameters get the same treatment. To do in the assembly; not yet wired.
- **Assembled** as `genericModel (Γ : Prog)`, where `Prog := List (IndDecl × Sig)` is in program order. It covers a declaration that is the program's declaration of its name, with the signature's arities and sort. Declaration `i` is `declDM` at `progEnv` (the models before it). `#print axioms genericModel`: propext, Quot.sound, Classical.choice.

### D5. Code-dependent meanings go through `CodeMeaning` (`Value.lean`)

Globals, closures, Π-closures, sealed programs and deferred D82 types mean "what running code means", which is milestone 2's reference run (the meaning of code, part of [Q2]). `Value.mean` takes a `CodeMeaning` (`global`, `sealed`, `clo`, `pi`, `paramTy`), so the first-order meaning is fixed and checked now, and the reference run will define `Value.mean` and the run in one mutual structural recursion over `Term`/`Value` and prove it agrees with `Value.mean C` at its own `C` (the knot-tying lemma). The run never re-runs a callee's body (it applies the callee's meaning), so it is structural; a recursive `fix` means its guarded fixpoint by well-founded recursion on `DataModel.rank`.

### D6. The meaning of a state and its shape (`State.lean`)

`MState.mean ρ st : Mean` is `(positions, future ↦ position ↦ Sem, shape)`, as docs/27 Def. 2: a lent place means its final content `φ ℓ`, a holder its current content, `⊥` means `moved`. The shape (which position holds the borrow of which label) is a partial map thanks to (WF1). `Mean.semEnd r l` is [End] on meanings and is below (`semEnd_below`, proved); `Mean.forget` is a drop or a capture by move (proved below); `Mean.dependsOn m p l` is what the reference run's [Access] ends.

The reference run's [Access] will end exactly the labels a place's meaning depends on, where the checker ends every label whose loan *occurs* in the content; the step lemma is that the checker's extra ends are below and commute (by WF3's acyclicity).

**The relevance table** (which `MState` fields `⟦Ω⟧` reads) is `State.lean`'s module docstring, with `MState.Core` the relevant projection and `mean_core` the theorem that the rest is irrelevant: read are `env` (values, frame structure; stored types for membership), `inds`, `absTy`, `goal`, `neutrals` and `etaRefs` (conditions on `ρ`), and `globals` through `CodeMeaning.global`. Irrelevant: counters and bounds (`nextLoan`, `nextAbs`, `nextRecUid`, `depth`), caches and logs (`classCache`, `constDecls`, `cfg`, `locs`), and inputs of decisions (`refs`, `recStack`, `convStack`, the declared-type reader's flags, the run-mode flags, `placeLog`). `placeLog` was flagged and resolved: on branch `d84` (both 6400a1d3 and the redesign in progress) the drop at the join and a block's captures are decided statically by `Decide.jdMoves`, from syntax and declared types, and the arms' log is kept only for an internal assertion that never fires. So the log is irrelevant to the meaning and feeds no decision; the reference run's join reads `jdMoves` (to confirm against `origin/d84` when it is written).

### D7. Well-formedness and membership (`WF.lean`)

(WF1)–(WF4) and (WF7) are stated on `MState` as the plan has them, and (WF8), `&` only at the top of a type (D1); (WF5) is `ArgsLoanFree env ws`, a property of a call's argument array, not of a state; (WF6) is split: its naming half (every `σ` in Ω, Δ or the goal has a type in Δ; labels and `σ`s are below their counters) is `WF6`, its typing half is membership. `TypedFuture`: each live borrow's final content lies in its holder's declared content type. `TypedAssignment`: `ρ σ ∈ₛ ⟦Δ(σ)⟧_ρ`, plus the path condition for `neutrals` (`ρ σ = ⟦n⟧_ρ`) and `etaRefs`. `Membership`: under every typed future, every position with a stored type holds a `pmem` of it; Δ's types and the goal are types. `Good ρ st` is the hypothesis of the main theorem. Stored types are read under the constant future `err`, since no stored type holds a live loan (docs/27 Lemma 5; to be a WF clause or a lemma of the skeleton).

### D8. Constructor values without recorded parameters are read at the type of their position (`Value.lean`, `Data.lean`)

`ps = []` constructor values are reachable: `Machine.evalCtor`'s untyped path (around line 2331) builds `.ind ty c h evs ws` with `evs` the parameters *written* in the source; inferred parameters are filled only on the typed path, and untyped runs (sealed-program re-normalisation, callee bodies, D85's runtime) put such values into states. The checker reads their type from the position (D49 (4), `indValType?` when `ps.isEmpty`). A WF clause "constructor values record their parameters" would therefore be false (the lead's review, 2026-11-09).

Chosen: the lead's option (a). `Value.mean ρ φ hint v` reads a value *at a type*, and only a constructor value with `ps = []` of a parameterised type uses the hint: `DataModel.paramsOf d hint` picks, classically, parameters whose carrier is the hint's (`paramsOf_car`: `car d ps = A`), so the element is tagged with the position's carrier and membership holds. The hint is the binding's stored type (`Env.hintAt`, equal to `MState.posTyM` where there is one, `hintAt_posTyM`), a field's type at the earlier fields (`DataModel.fieldTy`, threaded through `Value.meanFields`), a parameter's type (`DataModel.paramTy`, through `Value.meanParams`), or an equation's type; a temporary's hint is `err`, and the step lemmas for in-flight values supply the type the context requires. Everything else ignores the hint, so docs/18's V2 holds in the sense that a constructor value's meaning is determined by its type, recorded or not. Not chosen: (b) inferring parameters from the fields (undetermined for `Nil`); (c) a checker change recording inferred parameters in untyped runs, which would be a separate, verdict-neutral lane and would let the hint go away; worth doing later for simplicity, not needed for soundness.

A subtlety to carry into milestone 2: two parameter lists with the same carrier must pack the same fields to `HEq` elements (a `pack_congr` law on `DataModel`, to add when the generic construction is written), so that the choice in `paramsOf` is immaterial.

### D10. The run is per-future (`Run.lean`; m2-proofs' counterexamples)

m2-proofs refuted the first substitution lemmas in Lean: the run read every position under the constant future and never recorded its own labels in `shape`, so a hole or inert loan read `err` and a borrow the run made could never be ended. Now `run K ρ φ hint t : SState → Option (Res × SState)` with `Res := Sem × TyS` is the run **at the future `φ`**: every read of a position evaluates its `SVal` at `φ` (a free label means `φ l`; a match on a free hole picks its arm at `φ`), the run's own labels are recorded in `shape` at the position that comes to hold the borrow (`pushBindShaped` for a `let`; a result's label travels in `TyS.label`), ended by `access`/`endLabel` (= `Mean.semEnd`) before they are read, and a `let`'s drop ends the label its binding holds. The meaning of a configuration `(Ω, t)` is `fun φ => run K ρ φ hint t (Ω's sstate)`, result and state read at `φ`; that is the reference Mean every step lemma compares with. A sealed program's run starts its label counter above its loans (`emptyState n`). `meanV`'s `.tPi` clause wraps in `.ty`; capture types are read at the ambient `φ` (types are future-free at reachable states, so `meanV_congr` uses the `hasLoan` form of its hypothesis, which looks inside `rd`). `CodeMeaning.global : String → Sem × TyS` (a global's Π-shape), and `piShapeOf` computes a closure's or Π-closure's shape from its term, used by `.val`, `codeMeaning.paramTy` and `Steps/Call.calleeRes`. `Decisions.joinMoves` takes the entry-moved set (`movedPlaces`), as d84's `jdMoves` does. (Superseded by D18: `codeMeaning K ρ φ` ignored its assignment and future arguments, on the belief that `Value.mean` is read only at the state's own; it is not, and `codeMeaning K` now passes both through.)

### D11. The join drop follows `Machine.dropAtJoin`; Prop constructors mean `⋆` (mono-lemmas, prog-decisions)

mono-lemmas showed `joinDrop_below` false at field places (a field set to `moved` makes the value a `ctor`, never below an `el0`) and listed four differences from d84's `dropAtJoin`. `joinDrop` now accesses the place first, leaves copy places (by the declared reading), proofs and places already `⊥` alone, ends a borrow held at a dropped root, and sets the place to `moved`; the match step lemma compares the checker's post-state with the run's (both after their drops), so it needs no "below its input" claim. `Sem.updAt`'s overlapping patterns mean it has no equation lemmas (unfold with `Sem.updAt.eq_def`); a prover who needs them should split the `Nat` case into its own function. prog-decisions found that a constructor of a `Prop`-sorted inductive went through `ctorM` where the checker gives `⋆` (D42): `run`'s `.ctor` and `meanV`/`Value.mean`'s `.ind` now mean `.proof` for `d.sort = 0`, the fields run as statements. m2-proofs' counterexample file is archived verbatim as `Ochr/Meaning/Regress/CE_82394c1a.lean` (not built; each example is to become a positive check against the current run).

Open: a bridge from `WF3 st.env` to mono-lemmas' semantic `SWF3 (st.sstate …)` needs a future-locality law on the run (a sealed program's, closure's or Π-closure's meaning reads the future only at the loans it contains) — `meanV_congr` in its `hasLoan` form is that law for values; the run-level form (`run φ t s = run φ' t s` when `φ`, `φ'` agree on `t`'s loans and the state's live and free labels) is to be stated in `Lemmas.lean`. prog-decisions reports a checker bug, not the spine's: `Decide.sortOf` reads a Π-term's domain through `declSort → declOf false`, so `Π(f : Π(x : Nat). Prop). Nat` gets declared sort 1 but computed sort 2, and `def T : Type₁ := Π(f : Π(x : Nat). Prop). Nat` is rejected with the INTERNAL [D55] message; pinned by `DecisionsSanity.piSort_nested` on their branch.

### D12. Closures at their declared sort; proof calls are `⋆` (prog-decisions' review)

The `.fix` clause built `el0 (piCar0 …)` whatever the Π's sort, so a proof-valued function and a polymorphic function (`id (A : Type) (x : A) : A`, in `Type₁`) meant elements outside their types' meanings. Now the clause reads the sort (`Decisions.piSort`, D55): at `Prop` the closure means `⋆` and its type is the proposition `piProp`; at `Type₀` it is `el0 (piCar0 …)`; at `Type₁`/`Type₂` its type is `ty1 (piCar1 …)`/`ty2 (piCar2 …)` but its *value* is `err` until a level-1/2 `mkTele` (parameters decoded at `el1`/`el2`, type arguments by `dec1`) is written: an open gap, visible rather than silent. A call whose class is "returns proofs" (`Decisions.flags`'s second component) yields `⋆` without applying anything, typed by the codomain at the arguments, as `Machine.callFn` does at class 2 (P5). **Closed by prog-decisions' `Apply1.lean` (landed eec3d961, wired in the following commit):** one level-generic construction (`Lvl` packaging el/enc/dec/ty and the `Sem` tag at each level; `piCarL`, `applyTeleL`, `mkTeleL`, `closureAt`, `piTyAt`, `applyFnL`) with β-laws (`applyTeleL_mkTeleL`, `applyFnL_closureL`) and round trips; `run`'s `.fix` builds the closure at its declared sort through `closureAt`, `.pi` gives `piTyAt`, and `callOf` dispatches on the callee's tag (`el0/el1/el2`). Levels 1 and 2 carry final contents and `PiOut` like level 0 (type-level functions do borrow: `Swap (V : Type) (a b : &V)`), so Pi.lean's `piCar1`/`piCar2` and Apply.lean's level-0 `applyTele`/`mkTele` are now unused by `run` (kept; `piCarL_lvl0` relates them). Remaining gap: `Prop` and `Type₀` as *elements* of `Type₁` (`.ty (.univ 0/1)`) do not decode at level 2 (`TyM.dec2`), so `F (A : Type₁) : Type₁` applied to `Type₀` is undefined until the level-2 universe carrier gets codes for them.

### D13. Labels follow borrows; the substitution lemmas' hypotheses (m2-proofs' round 2)

m2-proofs refuted the restated lemmas again (Lean-checked, `CE2.lean`): (A) a borrow whose result is discarded (`&y;`) or ascribed, or written by `assign`, or moved out of its binding (F), lost track of its label, so a later read saw `φ l` for the run's own label. Fixed in `run`: a discarded labelled result ends its label at its content (`.seq`); `.ascribe` keeps the label; `.assign` ends the old content's borrow at a root and makes the place the holder of a written borrow; a move of a whole position holding a borrow clears the position's shape entry and carries the label in the result's type, so whoever binds it (`pushBindShaped`, `.assign`) becomes the holder, and a call's borrow arguments carry their labels (`callOf` fixes their futures). (B) `run_substLoan` needs the state not to read `l` and `l` below its counter (`StateFreeOf`). (C) the decisions must not see the substitution (`DecInvariant K.D l w`; the real `Decide` has it for data fills, which is what [End] and [Close] substitute). (E) `w` is not code (`NotCode`): an embedded closure carries a shape an embedded loan does not. (D) a sealed program's counter `t.loans.foldl max 0 + 1` changes under substitution; rather than pick a stable counter, `run_counter_indep` states that the run's result is independent of the starting counter above the term's and state's labels (fresh labels are names), a lemma needed anyway. `CE2.lean` is to be added to `Regress/` by m2-proofs (the statements that D13 makes true flipped into positive checks).

### D14. A label always has a holder; reads without access use the closed future (m2-proofs' round 3)

Per-site ends (D13) could not be complete: `S(&y)` consumes the borrow's result before `.seq` sees it, so its label leaked (m2-proofs, `CE3.lean`, Lean-checked). Uniform fix, m2-proofs' proposal: (1) `borrowPlace` parks the new borrow in a fresh temporary of the top frame and records `shape temp = l` at once (`SState.pushTempShaped`); binding (`pushBindShaped`), a root `assign` and a call's borrow arguments take the holder over (`SState.takeLabel` forgets the parking temporary), a move out of a holder parks the borrow again, a returned borrow is parked too; so from creation to its end a label has exactly one holder, `access` ends it before any dependent read whoever consumed the result, and the `.fix` frame pop ends what is left. The per-site ends of D13 are removed. (2) Reads that skip [Access] (`movedPlaces`, the guards of `setContent` and `joinDrop`) read at `SState.closedFuture s φ`: `φ` with every live label fixed to its holder's current content, iterated as `access` iterates, so they depend on `φ` only off the live labels. (3) `run_counter_indep` compares the result's meaning only (`(·.1.1)`): a returned borrow's label and the state's labels are the counter. The induction invariant for the four lemmas becomes "every position factors through `L ∪ live(s)`, and every read is at a future agreeing with `φ` off `live(s)`". Mono.lean's `joinDrop_below` proof was adjusted by one discriminant (the closed future), with mono-lemmas informed.

### D15. Borrow parameters have cells; the returned borrow survives the pop (m2-proofs' D14 regression report)

Under D14 the `.fix` clause's frame pop ended every label the body made, the returned borrow's included, so a borrow-returning closure's backward function became constant and the closure meant `err`; and returning a borrow parameter itself gave no label. Now a borrow parameter is set up as the generic call is ([Def]): a fresh label `lᵢ`, a *cell* reading `lᵢ`'s final content (a temporary of the frame, so de Bruijn indices are unchanged), and the parameter as the holder of the content (D14); the pop ends the body's labels and the parameters' except the returned borrow's (`R.label`), whose holder is the caller's; a borrow parameter's final content is its cell's value. Returning the parameter returns its label, and the backward function reads the cell at the future fixed at that label (`mkBackAt`).

### D16. On main's D84: `jdMoves`, `typeState`, and `Id` under [Obs-ref] (2026-11-10)

`spine` is rebased onto main 7ae8cf00 (D84 revised merged at ebb398b8). `DecisionsInst.checkerDecisions st fuel` no longer takes `joinMoves` as a parameter: it is `Decide.jdMoves fuel [] [] t entry` as `Machine.staticJoin` calls it, and the new field `Decisions.rePointed` is `Decide.typeState`'s `repointed` component as `idType` reads it. The run's `Id` follows [Obs-ref]: `observation φ reps ws r s` first writes, through each re-pointed borrow binding of the side (`reps`, static), the `i`-th of the values `ws`, then ends every live borrow and reads the result and every position; since a fresh abstract value under every assignment is every value, the `.id` clause means `∀ ws, observation … t = observation … u`, so `RePoint`'s two sides differ (they write to different owners) while `SameTarget`'s agree. [Id-typestate]'s well-formedness and no-ending clause are checks and need no run clause. `Steps/Decisions` gains `joinMoves_aligned` (against `jdMoves`) and `rePointed_aligned` (against `typeState`). Mono.lean's observation lemmas are stated for no re-pointed bindings (`observation φ [] []`). Still to state: the step lemma for `idType` under [Obs-ref] compares the checker's fresh `σ_w`s with `ws` under the extended assignment (`observe_step`'s `∃ wsS`).

### D17. Milestone 3 restructured: fuel-indexed statements, one induction hypothesis, one knot (the pilot prover's findings, 2026-11-10)

The pilot prover (`spine-prover-borrow`, Scratch.lean, against 3c323dcc) found every Borrow statement false or unprovable alone: (1) the step lemmas depend on each other through the checker's mutual recursion on fuel (`endBorrow → substEnv → substV → nfSealed → eval`), so none is provable in isolation; (2) four kernel-checked counterexamples: a returned borrow is in flight, not in Ω, so `Good st'` fails WF2 (`borrowPlace`, and `readPlace`, which moves a borrow out of Ω); an assigned value had no hypothesis (`assignPlace` with `loan_5`); a dropped or popped position compared the checker's out-of-range default `()` with the run's `err` (`dropTopBind`, `popFrame`); (3) nothing tied `K.C` to the knot (D18 removes the knot hypothesis altogether); `accessPath` alone ends only the path.

Restructure. `Steps/Statements.lean` holds every statement as `def X_stmt (K ρ φ) (fuel) : Prop := ∀ …` (73, with the helpers `Runs`, `SM'`, `Exact`, `propOf`, `calleeRes`, `argsRes`, `Extends`, `refObs`, `hintM`); `Steps/IH.lean` bundles them: `structure StepsIH K ρ φ fuel` with one field per statement (it also carried `knot : K.C = codeMeaning K ρ φ`, removed by D18); each area file proves `theorem X (fuel) (ih : ∀ ρ φ, StepsIH K ρ φ fuel) : X_stmt K ρ φ (fuel + 1)` (the hypothesis at every assignment and future, D18) (provers partition by file, import order is irrelevant); `Steps/Knot.lean`'s `stepsIH : ∀ fuel, StepsIH K ρ φ fuel` is the induction (base: fuel-indexed statements are vacuous at fuel 0, the rest proved outright in `stepsIH_zero`; step: the area theorems). Statement fixes: a value a step returns is in flight and counts as a temporary (`MState.withTemp`) for `Good` and `Below` (readPlace, borrowPlace); `assignPlace` assumes `Good (st.withTemp v)`; `Env.mean` is bounds-checked, so a dropped position means `err` on both sides; [Access] is the checker's composite `accessPath; accessInside` against `SState.access` (`access_step`); every statement assumes `K.Δ = deltaOf K ρ φ st` (now `DeltaOk K ρ st`, D18). Two further defects found while converting: `endBorrow`, `substV`, `substEnv`, `refine`, `renormAll`, `nfSealed` were stated *exact*, but re-normalisation may run a known callee's body, which is only *below* the callee's meaning (an early end inside it), so they are now `⊑`/`Below`; and a sealed program's call through an abstract function had no Π-shape in the run, so every closed-off higher-order call meant `err`: `RunCtx.Δ` now carries Δ with shapes (`deltaOf` from the checker's `absTy`), read by the `.val (.abs σ)` clause; it is fixed per checker state since the reference run creates no unknowns.

### Sanity theorems (`Sanity.lean`), all without `sorry`

On literal `MState`s with a `Nat` binding: [Borrow] is exact (`borrow_exact`); [End] is `semEnd` at the state's positions (`end_is_semEnd`) and below (`end_below`); a `#guard` runs the checker's `endBorrow` on the pre-state and checks that it yields the post-state the theorem is about; `⊥` is definite (`moved_definite`); `Pick`'s early end is an end and nothing else (`pick_early_end`: after reading `b`, `a` and `b` mean their fills at the hole fixed to `r`'s current content, under every future, and `r` is forgotten), with the sealed programs' meanings taken from `CodeMeaning`.

### What could not be defined in milestone 1

- The generic data carrier (D4) and the code meanings (D5): parametrised, built in milestone 2.
- `libModel`'s operations and laws are `sorry` (routine).

(ii) A lender at an *untyped* position (a temporary, an untyped binding) reads the content [End] writes back at hint `err`, so for a hint-dependent content `endBorrow_step` is false against `semEnd`; the state is `Good` as `Good` stood. Added (WF9): a loan outside a sealed program sits at a typed position (true of the checker by the reads' [Access] and because every lender is a typed binding; provers keep it, a step that cannot is a checker finding). (WF9) excludes the pilot's `Ωp` (`not_wf9_Ωp`).

(iii) `closeCall_step_stmt` as the pilot stated it was refuted on its free parameters (`Regress/CloseCallStmt.lean`, `old_false_of_run`): the callee's type `fT` was quantified freely (`fT := .zero` gives `callOf` no shape), and `kind`/`res`/`fills`/`tys` had nothing tying them to the callee, though the checker trusts typing for them (`res := .unit` at a `Nat` codomain returns `()`). Restated: the callee is read through the run (`run (.val fv)`), and `hkind`/`hres`/`hfills`/`htys` assume the agreement typing provides. This is substEnv_loan_step's premise for calling substV_loan_step per position. `allPos_nodup` (the environment's positions are distinct) and `substEnv_loan_spec` (proved): a successful `substEnv (loan ℓ) w false`, with `substV` leaving the environment as it finds it, writes at every position of `allPos` an output of `substV` on the position's original value and nothing else. `endBorrow_spec` (proved, untyped run outside an observation, content hole-free): [End]'s run is the holder found (`findBorrow`), its content taken (`takeBorrow`), the holder set to the rest (`⊥` for a whole borrow), then the substitution walk — so `st'.env` is the fold of `substV (loan ℓ) c` outputs over the positions of the emptied environment. The typed run adds `repackCheck`, an observation the type-state check; both only reject (extra hypotheses for that form). `endBorrow_below` (proved): given the substitution step at each value (`hsubst`: `substV`'s output means, at every future and hint, below the value read at the future fixed at ℓ to the content's meaning at the holder's hint — `substV_loan_step` with the filler read at the holder's hint, which hint-freeness makes the same as at `err`) and that `substV` keeps a value's shape (`hshape`), the state after [End] is `Below` `semEnd` at the holder. So endBorrow_step's Below conjunct is reduced in Lean to m2-proofs' substitution lemma plus `hshape` (a lemma on substV/nfSealed: a re-run sealed program is never a borrow value) and the typed/observing hypotheses; `Good st'` remains.

**Lead's decisions on the open probes (2026-11-13, recorded by the lead):**
- (P1) `&f` on a function value is ACCEPTED and is in the suite: `def BF (f : Π(x : Nat). Nat) : Unit := (let r = &f; ())` is accepted on main 72d7491b (checked against a flipped control), and BorrowTypes' `SetSucc`, `SetAdder`, `SetIf` and `CallThrough` write and call closures through `&(Π…)` parameters. So `NotCode` cannot be a WF clause. Replace it: the run's loan clause reads `loan_ℓ` with the Π-shape of its position's declared type (by WF9 every loan outside a sealed program sits at a typed position), and the substitution lemmas assume "the filler's TyS shape equals the shape of its loan position's declared type", which typing gives. m2-proofs' counterexample E was exactly this shape mismatch.
- (P2) A sealed content with a J tail is reachable: borrowing a place that holds a stuck J's result, `let w = J(Nat, n, m, Vec, h, v); let r = &w` with n and m unknown. Make it hint-free by reading J's transported term at the motive applied to its second endpoint, P(b), taken from its own text, which is how [T-J-stuck] closes it off. Treat `inplace` the same way, at whatever hint the checker's re-run uses.
- (P3, `hshape`) nfSealed never returns a borrow VALUE, by construction: [Close]'s &T row returns `borrow_k ⌈…; let r = C; *r⌉`, with the borrow OUTSIDE the sealed text and the text ending in the content read `*r`; a stuck block's &T row is the same; WF1 forbids a borrow inside a sealed program; and [Seal]'s head guard stops a re-run closing off again. State it as a lemma ("every sealed text's tail is a read or a call with a borrow-free codomain"), or a WF clause if needed, and discharge `hshape` from it. A sealed form whose tail can return a borrow would be a checker finding.

### WHERE THINGS STAND (2026-11-21, late night; `spine` at d6d83487, `spine-lead14` at 34ac0730, `spine-model5` on top of both; read this first)

Nat`, at the checker's own table), and its body step holds in the unchanged form, applied at the concrete start (`body_outW`; assumes the checker facts `BodyWInv`, `InvNumA`). **Finding, ruling asked (D141):** `OutGood` asks a borrow parameter's final to be a member at every future, which is false for any function returning its borrow parameter (`K`: the final is the returned borrow's final content); the closure lemma at a returned borrow, `MemBelow` and the consistency chain's stage obligations inherit it; recommended: ask it at futures that give the returned label a member. D137 (B) is not dropped: `Regress/BlockReadings.A3` is its test (KeepTP closes no block). `K`'s body step, `Q`'s step and `J2` remain.

**Update, eighteenth lead (`spine-lead18`, 2026-11-21; D137 to D139).** The first block the checker closes off (column `Q`, `S(match n {…})`) and the first borrow parameters (`W (r : &Nat) : Nat := clone(*r)`, `K (r : &Nat) : &Nat := r`) are pinned by guards and their checker facts stated (`BlockQInv`, `FixRunQ`, `BodyWInv`, `BodyKInv`). The form held for both as far as they went; each met a MODEL fault, kernel-checked, ruled (A) in both: the model read every closed-off block that takes a place as `err` (its `.clo` clause asked `CapsOk` of all of `rd`, where the checker keeps the block's parameters' readings; D137: FIXED, `CapsOk` reads the captures only, and `mean_vQ` proves the block means the reference's result); no body state the model builds for a borrow parameter is related to the checker's (its cell is not recorded as the label's site; D138: ruled, NOT YET BUILT). The reference's direct run of a non-tail match and the block's code as a closure are proved to give the same number (`runMatchQ`, `callOf_A`), so `Q`'s step goes through once (1) is ruled. `J2`'s call: its inversion is a disjunction (ruled), its reference run and the value agreement are proved in both branches (D139); `Pre`/`Rel` after the call remain.

**The plan is two halves, and a model for every program** (D86; docs/18 on main, 64bc732c, is the plan as it now is): (I) the fundamental lemma of the model, about the strict reference run alone; (II) the simulation: the checker's successful run makes the strict run succeed and is related to it; and a data model that exists for every accepted program. Membership is a corollary of (I) and (II). Entries D87 to D126 record what was built and found since D86. What this section calls "proved" was rebuilt with `#print axioms` (the three standard axioms or fewer): by the orchestrator, the model at 847f05ab, the two columns and the facts about the checker alone at 8e75e22d, and the commits leading to the fragment theorem; by the machine lane, `frag_run_free` at d6d83487; by model5 on `spine-model5`, `fund_all`, `column_C_free`, `column_B_free`, `frag_run_free`, `column_I_ff` and `column_I_def` after D126's stage 1.

**There is one reference run, the strict one** (`Run.lean`'s `runT`, D94), on the data layer with records as functions of paths (D92), recursive functions by weakened stages (D89), strict type formers (D97), the typed read (D99), open membership, the invalidation of dependent fields, strict reads and the test at writes to a field (D100, D104, D105), typed futures at whole members with a test at every end of a borrow (D106), and a `Prop`-sorted declaration's data code in no universe (D110). It follows the checker at the points the columns exposed: a non-recursive function's own-name slot holds stage 0, a member, where it held `err`, so the strict run failed on every accepted non-recursive function that writes a field (D113); each label has one holder, as a clause of `Strict.SGood` (D117); a `let` binding's reading is taken before its right-hand side runs (D118); expected-type hints are handed as the TYPED checker hands them, at every site, with no `typed` flag (D119; probes in `Strict/WitnessHint.lean`). Still owed on the model's side: `.succ` still hands its argument a hint where the typed checker hands none (missed by D119; harmless for every term whose run reads no hint, the fragment's included); its `Id` (below); runs in which an accepted function is formed as a closure and called, as witnesses for each strict test (started: `Strict/WitnessFix.lean` on `spine-model4`, the `.fix` clause reduced to its body's run); and the frame lemmas (below). The core lemmas are proved on it: `meanV_substLoan`, `run_substLoan`, `meanV_congr`, `run_congr`, `meanV_congrA`, `run_congrA`, `meanV_substAbs`, `run_substAbs`, `run_hintIndep`; and the relation's three (`Rel.start`, `sim_seq`, `Rel.private_copy`).

**(I) is PROVED** (D105, D106), and still with no assumption after D113 and D117 to D119. `Strict.fund_all` holds for every clause of the strict run, the strict ends included, and nothing under `Meaning/Strict/` uses `sorry`. Its context `FundCtx` has two fields (every global a good result with no label; no declaration of sort 2) and an instance (`Witness.fundCtx_Kw`). It is applied to concrete runs: four that write at a field path (`Witness.w_move`, `w_refill`, `w_joinDrop`, `w_borrowIndex`); one that moves a part out through a live borrow, refills it and reads the lender (`w_endOk`), and the same without the refill, whose strict run fails at the end (`run_endBad`); and a `let` that moves a fresh borrow from its temporary into a binding (`Strict/WitnessLet.lean`, D117). NOT covered by it: a call run in place and a closure body's frame. Since D126 a closure's body runs in the checker's frames (`SState.under`: the creation state under a fresh bottom frame, `self` and the parameters on its top frame), so for a global the body's start state is the checker's read as a reference state, and no bridge between frame shapes is assumed. The frame property was false as stated (`run_frame_false`, D113: in place the strict test after a write at a field reads the caller's frames) and is restated over good states, unproved; the in-place call's pop is strict (`runBodyRef` succeeds only where `popEnds` pass `EndsOk`), and `endInto` is not an end. `FrameRel`'s statement-level findings (D113) are not yet restated.

**The seam between (I) and (II) is ruled and built** (D106, ruling (A)). A typed future gives each live borrow a whole member of its record's type, and ending a borrow is a strict point, tested at each kind of end site. What (II) owes because of it: each end test, discharged by the checker's rule that no borrow ends over moved-out content (main's D98, D53), with [Repack] where the content is of a dependent type; met at every end the two columns make. The fact the relation lacked (for a live label, the record's type is its holder's stored type) is PROVED from `SGood` (`Steps/ColumnWrite.heldTy_eq_holderTy`), which every step in the form assumes; the three old-form `sorry`s that wait only on it (`DropOneAsm.dropOne_exists`, `MovedPlace.joinDropOne_noop`, `EqJAsm.J_step_asm`) can use it once they take `SGood`.

**(II): the form held, two columns and a fragment are closed with no assumed fact, and the first function is closed under two facts about the checker.** The form of a step (`Steps/Form.lean`: `Pre`, `Ext`, `Out`, `Sim`, `eval_sim`, the composition `sim_seqS`; D107) did not change across both columns and the fragment. What changed around it: "each label has one holder" was added to `Rel` (D114) and moved into `Strict.SGood.uniq` (D117, D120); the fragment has its own invariants on checker states, `FSt`, and `FStT` with a value in flight (D122).
- *The two columns, with no assumed fact* (both in the default build). `column_C_free` (`Steps/Machine/ColumnCFree.lean`) for `def C : Nat := let x = S(Z); x`, and `column_B_free` (`ColumnBFree.lean`) for `def B : Nat := (let x = S(Z); let y = &x; *y := Z; x)`, a borrow taken, written through, and ended by reading its lender. Each says: for every data model, assignment and future, every successful typed run of the checker's `eval` on the body from the constant-body start state makes the strict run of the body succeed with the expected number, a member of `Nat`, at type `Nat`. They do not say that the checker's run exists (a `#guard`, not a kernel proof), anything about another program, or anything beyond the strict run's success.
- *The fragment* (`Z`, `S(u)`, a variable read, `let`, `u; w`, `&x`, `x := u`, `*y := u`, nested freely, at `Nat`, `Unit` and `&Nat`). `Steps/FragSim.frag_sim`: `eval_sim` for every term of the fragment at every fuel, by induction on its typing `FTy` with the sub-terms' steps as hypotheses, including several live borrows, a read that ends exactly the borrows reaching the variable, and a `let` whose drop ends the borrow it bound. `Steps/FragTop.frag_run`, `frag_run_ff`: for every closed well-typed fragment term, from the start state, the checker's successful typed run makes the strict run succeed with a member of the term's type; `B`'s and `C`'s bodies are fragment terms. Its 18 facts about the checker (`FragOpen`) are all proved by the machine lane (machine5 to machine7, `FragRun` last, restated with the label counter above every label in use): `Steps/Machine/FragFree.frag_run_free` holds with NO assumed fact (standard axioms, d6d83487).
- *The first function, `I`* (`def I (n : Nat) : Nat := S(#0)`, the core check of the surface's `succFn`, D124; on `spine-lead14` and `spine-model5`, not yet on `spine`). The relation reads the function's own marked binding at the context's type (D123, `Binding.refTy`); the body's start bundles `Pre`, `Rel`, `SGood` at `stI` hold with no checker fact; the body's simulation (`ParBody.body_simI`) and its reference run (`ColumnI.body_runI`) assume `VarNumAInv` and `InvNumA`. With D126's stage 1 the reference's body state for `I` is the checker's start state (`ColumnIRun.start_I`): `column_I_ff` (the reference's `.fix` run of `I` from the empty state succeeds and the closure is a member of `Π(n : Nat). Nat`, from the checker's run of the body) ASSUMES 2 facts about the checker, `VarNumAInv` and `InvNumA`, read and judged true, unproved; `column_I_def` (from `checkDef defI`'s acceptance) assumes `FixRunI` too (stated, `#guard`ed).
- *`P`* (`match n { Z => Z, S m => m }`, the first split; D131, `spine-lead16`): the split's step in the tail form (`TailSim`, a wrapper around the unchanged `Out`) from every related reference state, and `column_P_ff`, assuming 1 fact about the checker (`SplitPInv`); `column_P_def` assumes `FixRunP` too; no model fact.
- *No general step beyond the fragment and `I`'s body is proved in the form*: an unknown read and `S` over it are (`I`); splits (`P`), matches and joins, calls, `Id`, data and dependent values are untouched. The 81 statements of `Steps/Statements.lean` stay in the form of D80; they are no longer to be moved in one pass: the fragment is widened term former by term former (`Steps/LEAD-HANDOFF.md`, lead12's "Next").
- *How facts about the checker are handled.* Each step's facts about the checker alone (what a successful run on a term former looks like; that `Inv` survives a given state change) are stated by the lead at the step's exact entry conditions, sanity-run by `#guard`, then read against the checker's code by the machine lane before it proves them. About half were false as first written (D114 to D116, machine2 to machine4 in `Steps/Machine/HANDOFF.md`): a tail position; a reading handed down; a lazily typed binding; the place lent; the stored type of the place written; the old content a write overwrites; when a decision is read; a decision table left free. Hence the rule below.

**The first old step taken to the bottom, the drop at the join, is assembled over open leaves** (D93), in the old form and not on the fragment's line. `joinDrop_step` rests on `stepField_dat`, `joinDropOne_noop`, `dropOne_exists`, `ctx_botWrite`, `recordedNeutral_ok`, `etaFresh_sim`, `Rel.kept`, `DecTab.back` and the `grows_*` facts. `stepField_dat` (`Steps/PlaceTyping.lean`) is false as stated for an arbitrary `T` (the restatement is known); the other two reopened leaves wait only on the holder-type fact, now proved from `SGood`.

**The model's `Id` is behind the checker's** (D109). Since main's D113 the checker's `Id` also compares what each re-pointed borrow binding points to; the model's `observation` puts the fresh value in the binding's place and never reads the content it replaced, so `idType_step_stmt` is false (main's `Hide`), and the step `key` inside `idType_step_asm` is a `sorry`. Queued for the model lane, not built.

**The existence of the data model** (D110; `DataModel/Exists/HANDOFF.md`, docs/34 §18). The statement is repaired, and its restated faithfulness bundles have two kernel-checked instances in the default build: the library (`PreludeModel.lean`), and a second, larger program with a list, a type nested through it, a field typed by a type function of an earlier field, and two `Prop`-sorted declarations, one nested at a `Prop` parameter (`Step2.lean`); each rests on one native assertion, that the checker's declarations are the ones the instance is about. "Two members of one small type with the same small reading are equal" is proved (`Piece3.memTy_small_inj`). A probe (`Probes.DMXBoundary`) shows that a field type only ever calls globals returning a proposition or a type of `Type₀`. The general statements are written and attacked (`General.lean`, outside the default build): three of five were false or vacuous as first written and are restated. The general proof is not started; its order: frame locality (`runT_frameLocal`), `globals_typed`, `knot_exists`, `mono_prop`/`truth_exists`, then pieces 6, 7 and the assembly.

**The facts about the checker alone** (D88, D111, D116). Proved (standard axioms), with no model in scope: a successful run only extends the tables, for the whole machine (`machineGrows`, all 118 functions of the mutual block, no gap); decisions agree across fuels (`decide_fuel_agree'`), except the block's captures; the decision table at a run's end holds at earlier states (`DecTab.back`); the inversion facts and state-level invariant lemmas the two columns use (`InvSteps`, `VarNumInv`, `BorrowNumInv`, `AssignDerefInv` restated, `VarEndInv`, `SeqInv`, `LetInv`, `SuccInv`, `DropNoopInv`), with `PlaceLocal` proved of the real table (`placeLocal_ff`); the fragment's 18 (`frag_run_free`). Open: `VarNumAInv`, `InvNumA` (column `I`; read against the checker and judged true as stated), `FixRunI` (stated by model5). Deferred as whole-machine inductions, to be scheduled when a step needs them: that every private computation keeps the invariant's counters in step (`Quiet` extended); and that the mode is kept by the borrow-ending machinery.

**The checker this line builds against is main's through its D116** (D115: no value the checker holds lives outside its state while anything runs; D116: `clone` of a proof place is that proof; merged into the proof line by the machine lane, and into `spine-lead14`, D124). Decided and not yet landed on main: D117 (the frame in which a dependent field's type is computed binds what a loan stands for, not the loan), D118 (a declaration that mentions an undeclared name is rejected wherever the name sits) and D119 (docs/31 D125 (B′): the split of an unknown at the end of a body drops at the join), which another agent is landing; column `P` waits for it.

**The top-level chain** (`Consistency.lean`): `consistencyBounded_assembled`, `consistency_rel`, `defn_member`, `step_member_fix`, `step_member_const` are assembled over open leaves. Of the leaves only `false_means_empty` is proved. Open: `accepted_resolves` (with `resolves_of_accepted`, `resolvesTo_unique`), `falseConst_ty`, `exists_dataModel` (with `covered_prefix`), `const_split`, `const_value`, `fix_split`, `fix_sortRule`, `fix_canon`, `labelsInhabited_of_rel`, `recDecreases`, and the decision table's `decide_fuel_agree` (proved in its own form as `decide_fuel_agree'`) and `chainDecisions_blockCaps` (`DecisionsFF.lean`). `step_member_const` and `fix_split` still rest on the [Return] statement D81 found false, and are to be re-assembled through `return_member`.

**Not started:** D123's stage 2 (a closure value's capture of the function being checked at the context's type; D126's cells are built); a column for a function with a borrow parameter; the frame property (`FrameProp.run_frame`, restated by D113 and, for the closure's two frames, by D126; `run_frame_obs`) and the two frame lemmas (I) lacks; the model's `Id` after main's D113 (D109); the general proof of existence (D110); conversion on code and the function rules' premises (D101); the knot for a closure formed inside a run (D83's row 11; D94's ruling 6 makes the strict run ask it of every `fix`).

**The lanes, by what they do** (2026-11-21, night):
- *The simulation* (its lead; the twelfth's work is all on `spine`): widen the fragment where the plan needs it next (a match on a number, a call), reusing the case lemmas, each new fact stated at its step's exact entry conditions with `#guard`s.
- *The facts about the checker alone* (the machine lane): review and prove the fragment's 18 facts, `FragRun` last, `FragDec` with the decisions; and re-close `machineGrows` on the checker after D115 and D116 (regenerated, as `Steps/Machine/HANDOFF.md` says).
- *The model* (`spine-model5`): D123's stage 2 (D126's stages 1 and 2 are built); accepted functions as closures as witnesses for each strict test (`Strict/WitnessFix.lean`; column `I` is the first function run through it); its `Id` after main's D113 and D114; the two frame lemmas; `.succ`'s hint.
- *The existence of the data model*: the general proof, in the order above.
- *The checker and its merge into this line*: D117 and D118 on main; the merge of D115 and D116 here, in progress.

**On lanes' branches, not merged here:** `rules-trial` ea3fbb37 (conversion and `Eq`, D101); `spine-slice` 10542690 (the drop's leaves reduced further, D93); `spine-model4` (`Strict/WitnessFix.lean`, started). Merged: `spine-model-dep` (ruling (A), at 14853a79), `spine-exists2` (at 0e728883), the machine lane's and the leads' work through `spine-lead12` (aa410844), and the machine lane's through `spine-machine7` (d6d83487). Not yet on `spine`: `spine-lead14` (D123 to D126's ask; `I`'s body), `spine-model5` (D126 built: the body under a fresh bottom frame, the cells in frame 0; column `I`). `spine-dep` is superseded: its open membership is in `Data.lean` here; only its kernel records of why leaf fields exist (`Dep/Records.lean`) live there.

**Standing rules**, each from a finding below. A lemma is reported proved only when its bundle of hypotheses has an instance checked by the kernel and it has been applied to a concrete run (D98, D104), and a restated bundle stays pinned to its instance by the default build (D110). A form of statement is proved end to end on one narrow column, with an instance of every bundle of hypotheses, before many statements are moved to it (D106, D107). A statement is attacked before any of its pieces is proved (D110). A fact about the checker alone is stated and proved with no model in scope (D88, D101), is stated at its step's exact entry conditions and read against the checker's code before it is proved, and a theorem that assumes facts not yet so read is reported as "assumes N unreviewed facts", never as proved (D114 to D116). Where the checker decides a judgement by computing, what it computes is a relation, proved sound in the model and computed by the machine, in two separate lemmas (D101). A statement change is its own commit. A checker fault stops everything and goes to main with the smallest program.

### Where things stood on 2026-11-20 (fifth lead; superseded by the section above, kept for its detail on the drop of one place and on D29's rebuild)

**Main's D95 and D96 are in** (spine rebased onto main ed4676eb, 2026-11-20): the decision procedures write no cache (D95), and read an unknown only by its reading frozen when it was made (`MState.absDecl`, D96), never by its type. So the decision core (`DecideScope.EnvRel`, `DecCore`) is Σ, the frozen readings, the reading mode and the top frame's readings, with no cache hypothesis and no table of types (D65).

**Main's D90 and D91 are in** (spine rebased onto main a02f5396): when a borrow ends is a decision every run of the checker shares. The model is being rebuilt on it, as designed in **D29** (read it before anything below).

**Built and green, the four core lemmas re-proved** (`meanV_substLoan`, `run_substLoan`, `meanV_congr`, `run_congr`; `#print axioms`: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`):
- the value form `lent_k v` (`Value.gloan`) everywhere, meaning its content; the congruence lemmas read loans only (`Value.hasLoanM`), main's `hasLoan` also counting marks;
- the reference state's sites (`SState.lent`, `Site`), set by [Borrow], re-rooted by [End] (`SState.relend`), given to a borrow-returning call's result as fills ([Lend]); the reference's [Access] reads sites only (`SState.access s p hit`, `dataHit`, `tagHit`), and reads after it are at the closed future;
- the join's drop as the checker's (`accessLentPrefix`, `movedPlace`'s access of a lent parent; `dropAt`, `dropAtJoin`);
- the relation of D29 (`Rel`: skel, fresh, sim with holders both ways, inj, sites): no list of early ends, no loan witness; `Rel.start` (now with no hypothesis), `sim_seq`, `Rel.private_copy` proved on it;
- deleted: `Wit`, `ReachDep`, `accLoans` and their helpers, `Rel.early`, the reference's dependence machinery (`obsDep`, `access_FT`, `wholeOb`, `joinAccess`), the witness's two machine facts (`substV_loan_keeps_stmt`, `closeCall_hole_stmt`), `Regress/EarlyUnpinned`, `WitNonVacuity`, `CondDep`, and the order form `endBorrow_below`;
- restated in the simulation form on the new relation: `endBorrow_step_stmt`, `access_step_stmt`, `joinEntry_stmt` (now list equality), `joinDrop_step_stmt` (one drop list);
- kernel-checked runs repaired for the static access: `Attack/RunEqs` (the Close attack), `Regress/CE4_flipped`, `CE5_flipped`, `Adequacy_flipped.access_other_field` (A1, static), `JoinBorrowRoot.dropAt_borrow_root`; two probes main's D90 now rejects (`AdequacyProbes2.Disjoint`, gap F4; `JoinBorrowRoot.RJ`, gap EK) are `reject def`s.

**Built since:** the reference ends a discarded borrow at `t; u` (`discardEnd`, the checker's `dropValue`) and, at a `let`'s end, the borrows lent at a binding that holds no borrow (`letLenders`, D65's `endLoansOfTopBind`); the core lemmas re-proved, the kernel-checked runs repaired.

**Two of D29's attacks run on the checker's own states** (`Regress/StaticLend.lean`, built): the reborrow that outlives its borrow (D90's G8, at an unknown and a known `n`) and the borrow-returning call at a known argument (Pick at `Z`), each beside the checker's verdicts on the program and a rejected control. On the states the checker holds at a hole placed at the point (its real run), the call's sites are the reference's `lentK`; after [End] the checker's `endBorrow` and the reference's `relend` and `semEnd` leave every label at the same sites and the same holders (the relation's `sites` clause and the holders part of `sim`; the meanings are not computable); and reading the lender ends the reborrow on both sides. All hold. The instance states are kernel-checked (`decide +kernel`, the checker's `endBorrow` included); the checker's reads (well-founded `firstLiveLoanOnPath`) and the generic state (sealed fills that call `Pick`) are evaluated by `#guard`. The third attack, the non-tail match entry, waits for [Entry].

**Data values describe themselves (D32, built 2026-11-20).** A constructor value's meaning is `Sem.dat` (declaration name, parameters' meanings, constructor, fields, and its carrier element when whole and typed); a data type means the code `TyM.data`. The reference reads a value by reading it, never through a type or a carrier, which removed three faults at the root (D31's carrier guess; the join drop after a moved index, `Regress/PartialMoveDep`; the read into an open value, `Regress/OpenWriteDep`) and the clause (WF13) they had needed. A call's result carries its codomain's data (its shape is still lost: the Π half of the call-result redesign). The four core lemmas and `mean_updAt_bot` are re-proved (standard axioms).

**A refinement record is a path condition (fixed 2026-11-20).** `Good` read no part of `MState.refs` ([Split]'s refinements in this world), which `State.lean` classed as an input of decisions only. But the checker reads it back into the state: a program generalised in this world is replaced by its unknown with the refinements since substituted (`canonNeutral`, `recordedNeutral`, `expandRefs`). `Regress/RefsPathCond.lean` (built, standard axioms): a state with `a : Nat ↦ σ₁`, the record `⌈0⌉ := σ₀` and the refinement `σ₀ := S σ₁` met every clause of `Good` at `σ₀ ↦ 0, σ₁ ↦ 7`, and there `canonNeutral ⌈0⌉` runs to `S σ₁`, meaning `8` (`canon_step_false`: `canonNeutral_step_stmt` was false). Fixed: `TypedAssignment.refs` (each record `σ := r` says `ρ σ = ⟦r⟧`, as the generalisation and η records do), `refs` added to `MState.Core`; the state is no longer `Good` at that assignment (`not_good`). It is what `refine` already establishes (`refine_sim'`'s `hσ`), and the movedPlace branch `recorded_sim` needs it.

**The assignment congruence is PROVED (2026-11-21, `AssignCongr.lean`, standard axioms):** a run and a meaning read the assignment and Δ only at the unknowns the term or value holds (`run_congrA`, `meanV_congrA`), which closes `Lemmas.run_abs_congr`. It carries every meaning across an extension at fresh unknowns. Proving `freshAbs_sim` with it found (WF6) too weak (D36, fixed). **Ends create unknowns (D35, probed, decided):** an end of a borrow of a dependent type keeps an unknown nothing names (394 of 24,870 ends from the suite's recorded states; none adds a record); `DeltaOk` now asks `K.Δ` only at referenced unknowns, and `access_ctx`, `dropAtJoin_ctx` take `Good`.

**Clauses of `Good` read off the checker, NOT proved preserved** (every step's `Good` conclusion owes them): (WF11) no stored type or record holds a loan; (WF12) under a live fill only borrows filled at its path (restated a second time, D39: D33's form is false since main's D93); (WF8) restated (D33); (WF6) now bounds every unknown the state names, records and stored types included (D36). The sweep (main's `lake exe sweep`) tests the syntactic clauses on every state the checker records: see D33 for its results and the three clauses it found wrong. "No live fill above a moved part" (`lentPrefix_none_of_noop`, restated without proof parts, D33) is stated, not yet a clause.

**`dropOne_step`: L2, the sites half of L3, the commutation of ends and the place link proved; the other obligations stated** (`Steps/DropOne.lean`, each a named Lean statement, `sorry`'d where open, with what was tried):
- L2 `mean_updAt_bot` (`BotWrite.lean`): PROVED (standard axioms), for a value recording its parameters with no sealed program, along a path it has (`NatAlong`); since D32 nothing is asked of the hint. Open beside it: `mean_updAt_bot_sealed` (a top-hint-free sealed sibling; the same proof with `meanV_hintIndep`, not written).
- L3 (the write of `⊥` keeps the relation and `Good`; its first statement, `botWrite_step`, is deleted, unused): its RELATION half is PROVED (`botWrite_rel`, `Steps/BotWriteRel.lean`, standard axioms: skeleton, fresh, readings by L2, holders both ways, borrows in flight, liveness from (WF1), sites), given what the drop knows at that point (after the access, `RecPlain`, `NatAlong`, not a type value at a root); its sites half was PROVED first (`sitesOf_updAt_bot`, `SitesWrite.lean`: the write removes exactly the sites at or under the path; a mark on the way and every site off the path stay), the rest open (skeleton at a type-valued root, liveness, the fresh clause, `Good`), with `access_clears` (after the checker's access no live borrow has a site at, over or under the place): open, not tried beyond L2.

**Types mean codes (D59, built; A17 beside it).** A type's meaning is a code closed under the type formers (a function type's telescope, a data type's declaration and parameters, universes decoding to the codes of the level below), so no clause can drop a type's structure. The four core lemmas are re-proved on codes (standard axioms), and the model runs the programs that motivated it to the checker's answers (`Regress/CodeRuns.lean`: `UseAdder`, `UseFn`, `UsePred`, `Alias`, `BoxProof`, `let g = id(Nat → Nat, f); g(Z)`). A recursive closure's stages are filled (D60: built strictly, every recursive closure was `err`).

**A data type's code names its declaration, its parameters and its carrier, nothing more (D61, built).** An element's constructor and fields are read through the data model (`DataModel.read`), so membership and encoding take the reading, and recursive and nested declarations have codes. `CodeFaithful` is gone: a packed element is a member of its type for every covered declaration whose parameters are read back from their codes (`ctor_mem_tyM`), non-recursive, recursive and nested alike. Kernel-checked beside the checker's verdicts: `Cons(3, Cons(4, Nil))` and the rose tree `Node(1, Cons(Node(2, Nil), Nil))` built, matched and run by the model's own constructor clause, each a member of its type's code, and `Len` over the list (`Regress/DataCodes.lean`, `Regress/CodeRuns.lean`). The four core lemmas re-proved (standard axioms). `exists_dataModel` stays `sorry` and now owes more: a reading coherent with the model (`DataGeneric.Coherent`), a fixed point of the model and its reading.

The lead holds the relative invariant for nested runs until main's D97 (a sealed program with a live hole never runs) is assessed: `nestedRun_sim` is stated for a program with no hole, and D63 lists every checker site that runs a sealed program with a hole. The untyped runs are kernel-checked and fixed (D64: `eval_step_stmt` and `evalCore_step_stmt` take `hok`). Next: `callOf_inPlace` on concrete calls (`Inc(&y)`, a returned reborrow `Id1(&y)`: the checker's verdicts are asserted in `Regress/ShapeProbes.lean`), then the frame property of `run` and its proof.

**Task C, in progress (D30):** `joinDrop_step` is proved from the drop of one place (`dropOne_step`, open); the statements gained `Runs` from any log, an extended context for steps that create unknowns, the IH at every context, and the drop list's grain. ### Second lead (2026-11-13, from dcdd0a34): the four core lemmas landed; review of m2-proofs' proposals

`spine-m2-proofs` (head dcdd0a34) is landed by fast-forward. `meanV_substLoan`, `run_substLoan`, `meanV_congr` and `run_congr` (`Lemmas.lean`, through `Locality`, `LocalityRun`, `RunPres`, `RunPresInd`, `RunOrd`, `RunCongr`, `SubstDefs`, `RunSubst`) are proved with no `sorry`; `#print axioms` on each gives `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound` (re-checked by this lead). The definition and statement proposals it carried are all ACCEPTED; none is a model fault:

- **P1's declared loan shape** (266fbd18): `RunCtx.Λ : Nat → TyS`, read by the `.val (.loan l)` clause for the loan's Π-shape; `FillShape` (at every hint, `.val w` and `.val (loan ℓ)` give one `TyS`) replaces `NotCode` in the substitution pair. A data loan reads as before.
- **`rePointed` joins `DecInvariant`** (266fbd18): `Id` reads the re-pointed bindings off its first side's text, so the substitution must not change them.
- **The `.fix` pop keeps the returned label only at a `&` codomain** (266fbd18): a labelled result at a data codomain is ended with the rest.
- **A copy, an embedded value and a statement call's result carry no label** (33549adb, 48a197e3): a result's label names only a borrow the run holds for that result.
- **`hK`, `hord`, `hsh`** (dc786e0a, d92a418b): a global carries no label (`∀ n, (K.C.global n).2.label = none`; true of `Program.lean`'s `globalTyS` by construction); `run_congr`/`run_substLoan` assume the starting state's holders label-ordered and its shape entries real positions below the counter. The step lemmas use `meanV_substLoan`, which has no state hypothesis; the empty state satisfies both (`rinv_emptyState`).
- **PROPOSAL 8** (dcdd0a34): a sealed program runs from `emptyState 0`, as a closure's code already ran from `stateOf 0`.

Obligations these leave, each owned by the step statement that will use the lemma (none is discharged yet; `substV_loan_step_stmt` and `substEnv_loan_step_stmt` do not yet assume them):
- `hD : DecInvariant K.D ℓ w` at the checker's decisions for a data fill (decide-scope's lane);
- `hc : FillShape K ρ φ ℓ w`, from `K.Λ ℓ` being the Π-shape of the loan's position's declared type and typing; no statement ties `K.Λ` to the state yet (the same per-state question as `K.Δ = deltaOf …`);
- `hK`, a hypothesis on the program meaning.

**The counter concern (recorded, as the team lead asked).** The CHECKER re-runs a sealed program (`nfSealed`: `env := #[{}]`, the state's other fields kept) and runs a callee's body at its global, never-reused label counter (D37); the model runs both from the counter 0. So `nfSealed_step`, `closeCall_step` and `runBody_step` get from `evalCore_step` the run from `stateOf st.nextLoan _` and must compare it with the run from `stateOf 0 _`. That is `run_counter_indep`, the renaming lemma, which is therefore KEPT, and restated in the form those steps need: any two counters, a frame of future-free bindings, no condition on the term's loans (the old form required the loans below both counters, which the counter 0 never satisfies), compared on the result's meaning and shape. It is open (`sorry`), believed true (a fresh label never reaches a meaning: `run_congr` at `L :=` the term's loans; a loan is read as a constant of the ambient future, never through the state), and its proof is a simulation up to the shift of the labels, of the size of part (E). Not new with PROPOSAL 8: closures' code always ran from `stateOf 0`.

### D18. No knot hypothesis; the induction hypothesis at every assignment and future (second lead, 2026-11-13)

**Finding (Lean-checked, `Regress/Knot_406891db.lean`; a definition fault).** `codeMeaning K ρ φ₀` ignored the assignment and future it was passed, and `StepsIH` assumed `K.C = codeMeaning K ρ φ₀`. So `MState.mean K.dm K.C K.Γ ρ st` read every sealed program, closure and Π-closure at `φ₀` whatever future it was asked at: `Value.mean K.dm K.C K.Γ ρ φ hint ⌈loan_ℓ⌉ = φ₀ ℓ` for every `φ`. But `Below` (`∀ φ', ∃ φ`), `Mean.semEnd` (`m.val (φ.upd ℓ _)`) and `Membership` (every typed future) read a state at other futures, and every step that creates unknowns hands on an assignment `ρ'` that is not the knot's. Consequences: `endBorrow_step_stmt`'s `Below` conjunct had no witness on a lender holding a fill (`x ↦ ⌈loan_ℓ⌉`, `r ↦ borrow_ℓ 5`, `φ₀ ℓ = 7`: the checker gives `x` the meaning 5, `semEnd` reads 7 at every future); `endBorrow_below`'s premise `hsubst` (every future, in `Value.mean K.C` form) could not be discharged from `substV_loan_step` (one future, in `meanV` form); and the hypothesis at `fuel` could not be used at an extended assignment at all.

**Fix.** (1) `codeMeaning K : CodeMeaning` reads each code-dependent form by the run at the assignment and future it is passed (`paramTy`, a deferred type, under the constant future, as every stored type is). (2) No knot hypothesis: the statements read a state through `K.M = codeMeaning K` (`MState.mean K.dm K.M K.Γ ρ st`, `Good K.dm K.M K.Γ ρ st`), and `K.C` supplies the globals only. So a run context can change (its Δ, below) without re-tying anything. (3) An area file's hypothesis is at every assignment and future: `theorem X (fuel) (ih : ∀ ρ φ, StepsIH K ρ φ fuel) : X_stmt K ρ φ (fuel + 1)`; `Steps/Knot.stepsIH : ∀ fuel ρ φ, StepsIH K ρ φ fuel` takes no hypothesis. (4) `deltaOf K ρ st` no longer takes a future (stored types are read under the constant one), and the statements assume `DeltaOk K ρ st`: `K.Δ` is `deltaOf` at the unknowns the state has and free beyond them. `Run.mean_sealed_at_future` is the positive check.

### D19. The reference run against the checker, clause by clause: five accepted programs it fails on (second lead, 2026-11-13)

The reference run had never been compared with the checker on a real program: every lemma about it (congruence, substitution, the invariant) holds of whatever `run` is, and the step lemmas, where the comparison is made, are all unproved.
