Reading `Run.lean`'s clauses against `Machine.evalAt`/`evalCore`/`evalCall` found five small programs the checker accepts on which the run of 1d94e4eb fails (`none`), so the closure means `err` and `defn_member` ("an accepted definition means an element of its type") is false of each. `Regress/AdequacyProbes.lean` (built) asserts the five acceptances, each beside a flipped control the checker rejects; `Regress/Adequacy_1d94e4eb.lean` (unbuilt record) kernel-checks the first two failures. All five are **definition faults of the reference run**; none is a checker bug.

| | accepted program | the checker | the run of 1d94e4eb | fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | `let h : True = (let y = x; refl); x` | runs an erased term on a private copy (`evalAt`: `inStatement`), whatever its form | asked `Decisions.flags` only of calls; a proof-valued `let`, `;` or `match` ran in place and moved `x` (`stmt_let_moves`, Lean) | every clause runs under `flags`: a statement on a copy, its result carrying no label |
| R2 | `Twice (f : Π(n : Nat). Nat) (n : Nat) : Nat := f(f(n))` | reads a call's head in place (D53 (e), `evalCall`: `inPlace`) | read the head by `run f`, a move for a function variable (`call_twice_fails`, Lean) | a place head is `readPlace … true` |
| R3 | `CopyParam (u : Unit) : Unit × Unit := (u, u)` | binds a parameter at its Π-type's declared reading (`runBodyCore`: `paramDecls`, `refineDecl`) | bound parameters and `self` at the reading `other`, never a copy type | the `.fix` clause binds each parameter at the reading of its domain term (a new `Decisions` field) |
| R4 | `let h : True = LemTwo(x, x); x` | runs each argument of a statement call on its own copy, a borrow-typed one in place (`stmtArgIf`, `Decide.stmtArgInPlace`) | ran the arguments in sequence on the call's one copy: the second `x` found `moved` | `runArgs`/`runFields` take the statement flag; a new `Decisions` field for `stmtArgInPlace` |
| R5 | `let r = &y; y := clone(*r); y` | evaluates the right-hand side, then accesses the place (`assignPlace`) | accessed the place first, ending `r`, then failed reading `*r` | `.assign` runs `u` first (at the place's stored type, read without access) |

Smaller differences found on the same read, not yet probed: `inplace(p)` is a read in place in the checker and `run hint u` (a move) in the run (the run's clause should be `peek`'s); `clone`/`peek` of a term that is not a place copy every read inside it in the checker (`withCopying`) and run it as written in the run; the proof primitives (`symm`, `trans`, `cong`, `rewrite`) return their proposition as type in the checker and `.plain .err` in the run, so `evalCore_step_stmt`'s type conjunct is false of them (a statement fault: the conjunct should not speak of proofs); a `J` at convertible endpoints goes through `joinDrop` in the checker and not in the run (the checker is then below, not a failure); `SM.onCopy` restores the label counter where the checker's private copies keep it (D37), so after a statement that borrowed, the run's next fresh label is not the checker's (matters once steps are composed, below).

**Status (same day, after the batch).** R1, R2, R4 and R5 are FIXED in `Run.lean`, and `inplace(p)` is now a read in place. The four core lemmas were repaired and are again proved with no `sorry` (`#print axioms`: the standard three), as are `run_hintIndep` and the Close attack. What changed:
- `SM.stmtIf b x`: when `b`, `x` runs on a copy that is discarded and its result names no label. Every clause of `run` that can change the state or return a label (place, borrow, assign, `let`, `;`, both matches, a constructor, `fix`, a call, `S`, `&A`, `J`, `clone`/`peek`/`inplace`, an ascription) runs under it at `Decisions.flags` of its own term. The clauses with no effect and no label are their own statement form and are unchanged.
- `SM.headOf f rd rn`: a place is read in place (`rd`), anything else by its run (`rn`); a call's head and the operand of `clone`/`peek`/`inplace` go through it.
- `runArgs` and `runFields` take the statement flag: each argument of a statement call, and each field of a proof constructor, runs on its own copy unless its declared type is a borrow type (`Decisions.declOf … matches .ref _`, which is `Decide.stmtArgInPlace`). No new `Decisions` field was needed.
- `.assign p u` runs `u` first, at the place's type read on a discarded copy, then accesses `p`. The right-hand side's label stays live or unread across that access (`contentOf_lou`).
- The repair of the proofs was one pattern per induction: `SM.stmtIf_some` in `run_rinv` (a statement leaves the state and returns no label, `rinv_pure`), `SM.stmtIf_congr` in `run_congrE`, the `flags` conjunct of `DecInvariant` in `run_substSame`, `stmtIf_resVS_congr` in `run_hintIndep`.
- `Regress/Adequacy_flipped.lean` (built) holds the positive checks: `head_in_place`, `stmt_keeps`. `CE4_flipped`, `CE5_flipped` and the Close attack now assume, or show by `rfl` for their literal contexts, that the decisions classify nothing as a statement.

**R3 is FIXED too (same day).** `Decisions` gains `typeDecl` (the reading of a type term, `Decide.typeDecl`), and `Decisions.fixDecls sc ds c` folds it over a function's domain terms as `Decide.telescope` does (`Decide.paramDecls`). The `.fix` clause binds each parameter at its reading and `self` at `.pi cd`. So a parameter of a copy type is copied (`CopyParam`), and a call through `self` or through a parameter that returns proofs or types is classified as one (before, a call of a hypothesis `h : Π(n : Nat). Eq(…)` or of a recursive lemma through `self` was run as a data call on `⋆` and failed). `DecInvariant` gains the `typeDecl` conjunct, `typeDecl_aligned_stmt` joins the skeleton, and `DecisionsSanity` checks by `rfl` that the checker's decisions read a `Unit` parameter as a copy type and a parameter `Π(n : Nat). Eq(…)` as `.pi .prop`. The four core lemmas are still proved with no `sorry`.

**R6, found while fixing R3, FIXED (same day): a function parameter could not be called** (`Regress/NoShape_R6.lean`, kernel-checked against f013653b, unbuilt record). `PiParam` carries a parameter's type as a `TyM` and nothing else, so the `.fix` clause bound a function parameter at a type with no Π-shape, and `callOf` had no shape to apply it along: the body of every function that calls a function parameter (`Twice` again, `HO`, any `map`) had no reference run. For the same reason `extendBy` bound the earlier parameters at `.plain .err` when a later domain or the codomain was read, so a dependent type that calls an earlier parameter (`(P : Π(n : Nat). Prop) (h : P(Z))`) meant `err`. The fix is D21 as planned: `PBind`, `runBinds` in place of `runDoms` in the mutual block, `extendBy s bs acc` binding the earlier parameters at their types, shapes and readings, and the `.fix` clause's frame binding each parameter at its binder's type. `PiParam`, `Pi.lean` and `Apply1.lean` are unchanged. The four core lemmas are again proved with no `sorry`. `Regress/Adequacy_flipped.lean` has the positive checks (`pi_param_has_shape`, `runBinds_pi`). Residual, as D21 says: a parameter whose type is a Π-type only after computing has no shape, and a call that returns a function has none (`PiOut`).

The smaller differences listed above are also open. And the adequacy of every clause is still only read, not proved: the step lemmas are where it is proved. A cheaper guard than waiting for them would be one kernel-checked run per form on a small real program, in the style of `Attack/CloseCallFwd.lean`; the five probes are the first candidates.

### D21. R6: a parameter is bound at its full type (second lead, 2026-11-13; done as planned)

Keeps `PiParam` as it is (no nested inductive), so `Pi.lean` and `Apply1.lean` do not change. In `Run.lean`:

- `PBind := (List Sem → TyS) × DeclInfo`: a parameter as a frame binds it, its type with its Π-shape as a function of the earlier arguments, and its declared reading.
- `extendBy s bs acc` binds argument `i` at `bs[i].1 (acc.take i)` and reading `bs[i].2` (it bound every argument at `.plain .err` and `other`).
- `runBinds s dcs pre : List Term → List PBind` replaces `runDoms` in the mutual block: each domain term is run in `extendBy s pre acc`, the forming state extended by the earlier parameters *at their types*, and gives `⟨v.asTy, R.shape, none, none⟩` (the run of a Π-term already returns the Π's shape in its result type). `dcs` are the telescope's readings (`Decisions.fixDecls`).
- `psOf bs ds : List PiParam` is derived outside the block: `isRef` from the domain term, `dom acc := (b.1 acc).m`.
- The `.pi` and `.fix` clauses and `piShapeOf` use `psOf (runBinds …)` and read the codomain in `extendBy s bs acc`; the `.fix` clause's frame binds parameter `i` at `bs[i].1 acc` (with its label for a borrow parameter) and `bs[i].2`.
- Not covered: a parameter whose type is a Π-type only after computing (a type alias, a type function's call), which has no shape here as it has no class in the checker without D77's reading; and a call that returns a function (`PiOut` carries no shape: the old gap (ii)).

Lemmas to restate: `runDoms_congrE`, `runDoms_substSame`, `rinv_extendBy`, `piShapeOf_*`, `fixOut_*`, `run_pi_def`, `run_fix_def`, and the fix helpers of `RunCongr` (`fixStep` takes `bs`).

### D22. Prerequisite (a), shape agreement: REFUTED as a blanket claim; the relation now carries the shape (third lead, 2026-11-19)

**The claim** (D20 item 1 (b)): a binding's Π-shape in the reference state, which its run made, is the shape the checker's stored type reads as (`Value.tyS`, through `tyShape`), because "a Π-type's shape does not depend on where it is read". `Rel` left the shape out of its skeleton on that belief.

**Finding 1, a fault of the statement.** `Rel` without the shape admits a reference state whose bindings have lost their shapes (the skeleton compared the type's meaning, data and reading only), and from such a state every call through a variable fails (`callOf` at `shape = none`). So `evalCore_step_stmt` and `eval_step_stmt`, stated from ANY related state, were false of every accepted call through a variable. Fixed: `bindSkel` includes the Π-shape, and the type conjunct of the two statements is `StrAgree` (the result type's meaning, shape and data are the checker's type's), not the meaning alone. On the way: `eval_step_stmt`'s type conjunct was false of every proof term (the run types an erased proof `err`, the checker returns its proposition); it now excludes proofs.

**Finding 2, three faults of the model** (kernel-checked halves in `Regress/ShapeAgree.lean`, the checker's verdicts in `Regress/ShapeProbes.lean`, each beside a rejected control; all built). The structure of a value (a function's Π-shape, a data type's parameters) is carried beside its meaning, in the type the run returns, and it is lost or misplaced in three places, so the agreement is false there in both directions:

| | accepted program | the checker | the reference run | kernel-checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A11, A18 | `let a = Adder(n); a(Z)`; `(f : Fn(Nat))` with `Fn(A) := Π(x : A). A`, then `f(n)` | stores the call's codomain at the arguments, or the type the call computed: a Π-type value, which has a shape | a call's result never has a shape or a data component, whatever the callee | `call_noShape`, `tyShape_tPi`, `call_shape_ne` |
| A19 | `let T = Π(n : Nat). Nat; let g : T = f; g(n)` | binds `T : Type`; reads the alias's value at the annotation | binds `T` at the type its run returned, a universe carrying the Π's shape; the checker's binding read as a reference state had none | `pi_shape`, `tyShape_sort` |
| A17 | `let b = MkPBox(h); n` with `PBox(A : Prop) := MkPBox(a : A)` | solves the parameter from the type of the proof `h` | does not run a proof and types it `err`, which solves nothing: the constructor fails | `erased_proof_untyped`, `unify_untyped` |

A18 is new and is not exotic: `UseFn` is the predicate idiom (`Pred(A) := Π(x : A). Prop`, then a parameter `P : Pred(Nat)` applied). The second lead recorded it as "no class in the checker without D77's reading"; the checker accepts it.

**What is proved.** `ShapeAgree.tyStr_futureFree`: a type value with no loan reads as the same type with the same shape and data at every future (from `tyShape_congrE`), so the checker's reading at the constant future is the reading at the run's. So of "where it is read", the future is settled, given (WF11) below.

**What is stated, open.** `piShape_step_stmt` (in the skeleton, `Steps/Types.lean`): for `capture` on a Π-term or `fix`, from any related reference state, the shape the run reads off the ORIGINAL term in the forming state is `tyShape` of the captured value. It rests on a capture lemma (a term's run reads the state only at its free variables, after [Access]) and on the decisions reading the same readings through the renaming. Known false where a captured PROOF variable is inlined as `(⋆ : T)` (A17 again).

**Fixed in the model:** A19's reading side. `Value.strS`: a binding that holds a type value carries the structure of the type it HOLDS, and `MState.sstate` binds at it, so a checker binding `T : Type` holding a Π-type has the shape the reference's own binding has. Not in the run's mutual block, so the four core lemmas are untouched. Residual of A19: a closure's captured alias (`capsTys` reads the recorded type only) and an assignment to a type variable (the reference's binding keeps the old structure).

**Not fixed: A11, A12, A17, A18.** They have one root, and one redesign closes A11 and A18: a function's type must give the structure of its RESULT as a function of the arguments. Design (not built): a W-type `Str := none | mk shape data (res : List Sem → Str)` and a field `res` on `TyS`; `callOf` types its result by `fv.2.res args`; the `.pi` and `.fix` clauses, `mkBind` and the annotation clauses fill it from the codomain term's run (as `fixCod` already does for the body's hint), and from the BODY's run where the codomain is a universe (a type function's result is a type, whose structure only its body knows: A18). No nested inductive, and `TyS.shape` keeps its type, so the proofs change where a `TyS` is built by `⟨_, _, _, _⟩` (56 sites) and at `callOf`. A12 (a field's structure) needs the declarations' field types run, which is not structural on the term: a separate design. A17 needs the run to type a proof it does not run (a place by its binding, a call by its codomain at the arguments).

Until then the simulation statements are false exactly on programs that use a call's result or a field as a function or as the hint of a bare constructor, and on a constructor whose parameter a proof field solves.

### D23. Prerequisite (b), no label issued inside a private copy survives it: a statement fault found, an invariant added, the positive form PROVED (third lead, 2026-11-19)

**Finding, a fault of the statement** (`Regress/CopyLabels.lean`, built). `Rel.fresh` asked that EVERY label below the checker's counter be renamed below the reference's. The checker keeps its counter across a private copy (D37, `restoreKeep`); the reference restores its own, and does not run a proof at all. So after any statement that borrows, the checker's counter is ahead, and from a state whose counter is 0 no renaming exists: on the type `Eq(Nat, (let r = &y; Z), Z)` the checker ends with counter 1 and Ω restored (`checkerCounts`, a `#guard`), the reference's run of an `Eq` leaves its state (`eq_state`, kernel-checked), and no renaming sends every label below 1 below 0 (`old_fresh_false`, kernel-checked). So the two migrated `eval` statements were false on it. Fixed: `fresh` ranges over the labels that OCCUR in the checker's state (`MState.Occurs`: a loan or a borrow of that label inside a value of Ω).

**What survives a private copy** (`restoreKeep`, read; `restoreKeep_spec`, proved): Ω, the goal and the declarations are the snapshot's; the counters, the generalisation and η records, and the types of the unknowns created inside are the copy's. So a label issued inside can survive only in a record, in such a type, or in the value the statement returns.

**The invariant that excludes it: (WF11), now a clause of `WF`.** No stored or deferred type, no type of an unknown, not the goal, and no generalisation or η record holds a loan anywhere. Read off the checker: a neutral is generalised only in typed mode, after `accessNeutralHead` has ended every live loan inside it, and in a typed run every loan is live ((WF2)); an evaluated argument is held in a temporary while the next is run, so a borrow in flight is found by [Access]. It was implicit before (`TypedAssignment.neutral` and `Value.tyS` read at the constant future "because types are hole-free") and stated only as `types_holeFree_stmt`, in a form that `Good` did not imply (a consequence of `Good`, for LIVE loans in stored types). `types_holeFree_stmt` is now (WF11) and is PROVED (a projection). (WF11) is a hypothesis-carried invariant: each step that concludes `Good st'` owes it, and none is proved yet. Not kernel-checked against the checker; no accepted program was found that breaks it.

**Proved** (`Steps/Sim.lean`, standard axioms): `private_copy_no_label` (with the snapshot's labels below its counter, no label at or above it occurs in the restored state) and **`Rel.private_copy`**: if Ω is the snapshot's, the restored state is simulated by the SAME reference state under the SAME renaming. That is the checker's side of the step of an erased term, and it absorbs the audit's A15. `CopyLabels.new_fresh_holds` instantiates it on the program above.

**Open:** a nested run that is not on a `Good` state (a sealed program's re-run binds its holes as inert loans, D20 item 3) can store a type that holds one; (WF11) is false there as `Good` is.

### D24. The drop at the join, attacked from a related state: three faults, all fixed (third lead, 2026-11-19)

The leaf D20 item 1 (e) named riskiest: the join reads which places are moved when the match starts, and a borrow variable the checker ended early is `⊥` for the checker and not for the reference. That difference turned out harmless (below). Three other things were not.

**1. A partly moved constructor value was a member of no type; so `Good` failed after every move of a field** (`Regress/PartlyMoved.lean`, built; a fault of the model's definition). Membership of a partly moved value, `pmem`, was `s = moved ∨ ∃ t whole, s ⊑ t ∧ t ∈ T` with `⊑` the order `Sem.le`. A constructor value with a moved field means an untyped tuple, a whole one an element of its carrier, and `Sem.le` compares a tuple only with a tuple: nothing whole and typed lay above a partly moved tuple, and `pmem` was false of every one (`old_pmem_false`, kernel-checked). A match binds no variables: a pattern variable is a place inside the scrutinee, so `match xs { Cons(h, t) => t }` leaves `xs = Cons(h, ⊥)`. So `Membership`, hence `Good`, which every statement assumes and concludes, failed at the state after every such read. Fixed: `DataModel.MovedOf` ("is this member with parts moved out": a tuple lies below a packed element of its carrier with the same tag and fields above its fields), and `DataModel.pmem` through it; `pmem_partly` is the positive form.

**2. The join's entry set was at the wrong grain; the checker proved an `Id` the model made false** (`Regress/JoinProbes.lean`, built; a fault of the model, recorded as a gap when `movedPlaces` was written). The checker reads the places moved at entry from the values (`botPlaces`: every place with a `⊥` inside a value); the reference's `movedPlaces` listed whole variables only. With `p.0` moved before a match, one arm that refills it and one that does not: from the entry `[p.0]` the join is `[p.0]` and the checker drops `p.0` after the arm taken; from the entry `[]` it is `[]` (two `#guard`s on `Decide.jdMoves`). Accepted: `Refill`, and `IdRefill`, where the match is one side of an `Id` whose other side leaves `p.0` moved, proved by `refl`; rejected: `RefillRead`, which reads `p.0` after the match. In the reference the first side left `p` whole and the second partly moved, and `Id` compares every position: the proposition was false. Fixed: `movedPlaces` is `Sem.movedIn` (the checker's `botPlaces`, on meanings) of each variable's meaning, through a borrow variable's content as `*r…`, read at the future that gives every live label `err` so that a lent part contributes nothing, as a loan contributes nothing for the checker. `botPlaces_partly`, `movedIn_partly` (kernel-checked): the two sets of a partly moved pair are the same place. `movedPlaces_congr` re-proved; the four core lemmas are untouched.

**3. `Rel` compared meanings by `⊑`; it is now equality** (a fault of the statement). All the slack between the two runs is the borrows the checker ended early, which `Rel` names and ends before comparing. With `⊑` the relation also admitted a reference state holding a value where the checker's holds `⊥` inside a partly moved tuple (`JoinProbes.slack_le`); the entry sets then differ (`slack_entry`), so the join differs, and `Id`, which compares every position after each side, is true for the checker and false for the reference from such a state. The statements quantify over every related state, so they were false of a match and of `Id`. `SimEq`'s two value clauses are equalities, and the value conjunct of the migrated statements is `=`: a value that is bound must satisfy the relation at its binding. RISK taken: if a re-normalised value can be strictly below its sealed program's meaning (the second lead stated `nfSealed`, `substV` and the calls with `⊑` to be safe), equality is false there; no such value is known, and results are whole, where `⊑` is equality.

**The early-ended borrow variable.** `Decide.jdMoves` from an entry set with one more borrow variable `r` returns the same set with `r` added: a move of a borrow variable covers only itself (`jdCovers` stops at a `deref`), and `jdGrain` and K1(g) do not move it. The checker's drop of `r` does nothing (`dropAtJoin` at `⊥`). So the two drop sets differ by variables on which the checker's drop is a no-op. This is read off `Decide.lean`, and stated: `joinEntry_stmt` (the reference's entry set is the checker's but for such variables) and `joinDrop_step_stmt` (the drops, given drop sets that differ only so). Both `sorry`.

**Migrated to the simulation form:** `evalMatch_step_stmt`, `evalMatchInd_step_stmt`, `evalMatchByType_step_stmt`, `closeBlock_step_stmt`, through `SimConcl` (the conclusion every step shares). `evalMatchInd` and `evalMatchByType` now run at the checker's expected type; their hint was free.

### D25. [Access] from a related state: one model fault fixed, one statement fault OPEN (third lead, 2026-11-19)

**1. Reading one field ended the borrow of another: the audit's A1 was not fixed for packed data** (`Regress/SiblingAccess.lean`, built; a fault of the model). [Access] ends a label when some future, changed at that label alone to some value, changes what is observed. Stage 4 of the batch made the observation the content at the place's path, and checked it on a position that means the tuple `P(ψ l, c)` at every future. The run's own meanings are `ctorM d ps c [a, ψ l]`, and `ctorM` meant `err` when its whole fields did not pack. At a future giving `l` a whole value outside the field's type (`•` at a `Nat` field) the value was `err`, no field of `err` can be read, and the observation of the OTHER field was `err` there: it depended on `l` (`old_sibling_dep`, kernel-checked). So the reference's read of `p.0` ended the borrow of `p.1`, and on the accepted `Disjoint` its result was not the checker's. Fixed: an ill-typed constructor value means the tuple of its fields, never `err` (`ctorM`); `DataModel.fieldsOf_ctorM` (proved): a constructor value's tag and fields are read off its meaning whatever its fields hold. Nothing else changed: no proof depended on that branch. Residual: a field's TYPE is `fieldTy` at the earlier fields, so a read of a later field of a dependent pair still depends on an earlier lent one, which is the audit's A13 ([Open]).

**2. Repaired by D27 (the loan witness); was OPEN: `Rel` does not pin the reference state at other values of an early-ended label** (`Regress/EarlyUnpinned.lean`, built; a fault of the statement's quantifier). `Rel` compares the checker's state with the reference state after the early-ended labels `E` are ended, so it reads the reference's meanings only where each label of `E` has its holder's content. [Access] reads every future. A position meaning "`ψ k` where `ψ l = err`, else `c`" agrees with the constant `c` once `l` is ended, and depends on `k`: a read of it ends `k` in the reference where the checker, holding the constant, ends nothing, and the reference is then ahead, which `Rel` cannot express. So a step statement quantified over every related state is false at such a state. It is not a reachable one (a value that reads a label only when another has a given content needs a sealed program with two holes, which (WF5) excludes), so the run is not at fault; the statement is. Fix candidates, none taken:
- `Rel` says the reference state is the reading of some checker-form state of which the checker's is the early-ended version. Regularity is then inherited from `Good`; the cost is that each step must produce that state, which is the "monotone in the state" lemma on the checker's side.
- [Access] decides dependence at the closed future only (every other label at its holder's content). What it reads is then what `Rel` pins, but "after the access the content reads no live label" (`access_FT`) is lost and reads must be at the closed future.
- The reference ends what the checker ends where the checker ends deliberately (a discarded borrow, the borrowers of a dying binding, a capture), leaving in `E` only labels no position reads; `Rel` then asks that of `E`. It does not cover a neutral's head ([Access] for a match ends every loan in a neutral, tag or not).

### D26. `Id`'s observation, attacked from a related state: one model fault fixed, three statement faults fixed (third lead, 2026-11-19)

Read: `Machine.idType`, `observe`, `obsResult`, `Obs.footprint`, against the run's `.id` clause and `observation`.

**1. `Id` ranged over every written value, typed or not** (`Regress/IdUnitWrite.lean`, built; a fault of the model). [Obs-ref]: where both sides re-point a borrow binding, the checker writes one fresh unknown of the BORROWED TYPE through it after each side, so that where it points is observed. The reference's `Id` asked the observations to be equal for every `w : Sem`. They differ at a type with one element: `IdUnit` (accepted, by `refl`; `IdNat`, the same at `Nat`, is rejected) re-points `y : &Unit` at `a` on one side and at `b` on the other; a `Unit` written is `()`, and `a`, `b` are `()` after either side; but `⊥` written through `y` is in `a` after one side and in `b` after the other. On the two end states: `id_all_false` (the `∀` over every written value is false) and `id_unit_true` (equal at `()`), kernel-checked through the run's own `observation`. Fixed: the `.id` clause quantifies over `obsTyped` values (one per re-pointed binding, a member of the type the binding is declared at). The four core lemmas still build unchanged.

**2. The statements of `Id` and `Eq` said nothing** (a fault of the statements). Both ended `∃ r s', run … = some (r, s') ∧ ⟦P⟧ true ↔ ⟦r⟧ true`, and `∧` binds tighter than `↔`: any `r` that is not the run's result satisfies it (`IdUnitWrite.old_stmt_vacuous`, kernel-checked). Restated, in the simulation form.

**3. Footprint coverage lacked [Id-typestate]** (a fault of the statement). The footprint lists what the sides write or borrow, not what they move; a place one side moves and the other does not is outside it and differs. The statement now assumes the two sides' type-states agree, as `idType` checks before it observes.

**4. The fresh written values and the assignment.** The checker's `Id` is a conjunction that mentions the fresh unknowns written through the re-pointed bindings; its meaning under one assignment is the instance at the values they are assigned, where the reference's is the `∀`. `idType_step_stmt` therefore says: under SOME assignment of the unknowns created (a member at which the two sides differ, where there is one) the two are true together, and if the reference's is true the checker's is true under EVERY typed assignment. So a binding `h : Id(…)` keeps `Rel.skel` for the assignment the step hands on.

**Checked by reading, no fault found:** the result and the owners against "every position that was there when the side started" (a borrow holder and a temporary are `⊥` after every borrow ends, on both sides; the footprint covers the rest, given the type-state); the order in which the borrows end (label order; a meaning is a function of the future, so a reborrow's end reaches its lender whichever ends first); a borrow ended over a moved-out part (`observing`: the same in the reference); [Obs-ref]'s write through `*y` against a write at `y`'s position.

**Migrated to the simulation form:** `observe_step_stmt` (with the written values, at the hint `A`), `footprint_coverage_stmt`, `idType_step_stmt`, `eq_step_stmt`. They conclude `st'.env = st.env`, not that the core is unchanged: the counters and records of a private copy survive it (D23).

### D27. The relation repaired: a loan witness, not a preimage (fourth lead, 2026-11-19)

D25 item 2 was decided as candidate 1: `Rel` says the reference state is the reading of some checker-form state satisfying the invariant, of which the checker's state is the early-ended version. How tightly the checker's state is tied to that preimage was left open. Neither tie works, and what [Access] needs does not need a preimage.

**1. A semantic tie is not enough, and the lemma it rested on is false** (`Regress/CondDep.lean`, built; the meaning-level half is kernel-checked, the rest is argued). The semantic tie keeps today's equality after the early ends and adds "the reference state reads a `Good` checker-form state". It was to rest on the lemma "in a state satisfying the invariant, whether a position depends on a label does not depend on the other labels' contents". That lemma is false at `Good` states. A stuck call of an unknown `σ_f : Π(x : &Nat, y : &Pair(Nat, Nat)). &Pair(Nat, Nat)` returns a borrow `k`, and the fills of `x` and `y` hold the hole `k`. When `k`'s holder holds `(loan_j, loan_m)` (two reborrows of its fields) and `k` ends, `x`'s fill means `(back (ψ j, ψ m)).1`. An assignment gives `σ_f` any member of its type, so any backward function whose joint result is injective. With `back (a, b) = (if a = 0 then b else c, (a, if a = 0 then c else b))` (`back_injective`), `x` depends on `m` exactly when `j` holds `0` (`fx_dep_m`, `fx_indep_m`), at typed futures. Now relate this to a checker state that ended `j` early (holder `S(n)`) and holds the constant `c` at `x`. Equality after the end holds (`fx_after_end`) and the preimage is `Good`. Yet the reference's [Access] of `x` ends `m` and the checker's ends nothing. So a step stated from every such state is false.

**2. A syntactic tie is false after a split.** The candidate: the checker's state is the preimage with `E` ended, its values or at least its loans. Take `Pick(σn, &x, &y)` closed off at an unknown `σn`, so both fills hold the hole `k`. A split on `σn` refines and re-normalises the checker's fills: in the arm the assignment selects, `y`'s fill becomes `y₀` and loses `loan_k`. The reference never splits, so a preimage it reads keeps the unrefined fill and its loan. Refining the preimage in lockstep needs the checker's re-normalisation to succeed on the preimage, which nothing guarantees. Temporaries also differ: the reference parks borrows, the checker carries values in flight.

**3. The repair: the loan witness** (`Steps/Statements.lean`: `Wit`, `ReachDep`, `accLoans`; a conjunct of `Rel.early`). Every label the reference's [Access] of a place would end, other than an early-ended one, is (renamed) a label the checker's [Access] of the same place ends. On the reference's side this is semantic (`ReachDep`: the observation depends on it at some future while it is live, or after ending labels it depends on, as `SState.access` iterates). On the checker's side it is syntactic (`accLoans`: the live loans on the path, each followed into its holder's content along the rest of the path, then the live loans inside the content reached, as `accessPath` and `accessInside` iterate). Then "the reference ends only what `E` or the checker ends" is immediate, which is what makes the relation hold after an access. It excludes `EarlyUnpinned`'s `f₁` (it depends on `k`, and the checker's constant holds no loan of `k`). In 1 above, at a reachable state the checker's `x` holds the fill with `(S(n), loan_m)` substituted, whose loan `m` its access ends. In 2, the reference's `y` does not depend on `k` under the assignment. No preimage is needed for [Access]. The witness reads only the checker's Ω, so `Rel.private_copy` is unchanged (proved).

**Checked both ways** (`Regress/WitNonVacuity.lean`, built, kernel-checked):
- `not_related`: the unpinned state is no longer related, under any renaming and any list of early ends. The checker holds the constant `Z` at `x` and the borrow of label `1`, and has ended label `0`; the reference's `x` means `EarlyUnpinned.f₁ 0 1 c`. Label `1` is held by the checker, so it is not early-ended (the shape clause), and the reference's access of `x` would end it, where the checker's `x` holds no loan.
- `wit_example1`: the witness holds at the reachable form of example 1, with `E = [0]`: the checker's `x` is the fill with its content substituted, still holding the loan of label `1`, whose borrow it holds; the reference's `x` means `CondDep.fx 0 1 c`. The reference can end only labels `0` and `1` (`reachDep_FT`: what an access reaches is among the labels the positions read), and the checker's access of `x` ends `1`.
- Checking the second found a fault of the clause as first written: it ranged over every place, the places into the sealed fill included, whose meaning the reference follows and the checker cannot reach. `Wit` now ranges over the places whose path reaches content in the checker's state (`pathReaches`: through each live loan into its holder's content, as `accessPath` follows it). A path into a neutral or a `⊥` is one the checker's read fails at, or one a split or an η-refinement first rewrites, which is a step of its own.

**Where it is false: open dependent values (A13).** A later field's type, and the hint of a sealed field, read the earlier fields. When an earlier field is lent, the reference's observation of the later field depends on the earlier field's label, and the checker's access of the later field does not end it. So `Rel.start` keeps the witness at `E = []` as its hypothesis (`hw`), and `Rel.start_simple` discharges it (`wit_start`, proved from `meanV_congr`) at a state whose every value is loan-free or a bare loan. Every place a run starts is of that shape, and is where `Rel.start` is to be used: the generic state of a definition's check (each parameter an unknown, a proof `⋆`, a borrow of an unknown, each borrowed cell a bare loan); the empty state a sealed program re-runs from (no value); a callee's frame (its parameters bound to arguments that are loan-free or borrows of loan-free content, (WF5), asserted where a call closes off, and captures that hold no loan). Today `Rel.start` is used only by `Regress/CopyLabels.lean`, at a one-binding state holding `Z` (`wit_of_const`). The general form, the witness at a state satisfying the invariant whose dependent constructor values have no lent field that a later field's type or hint reads, is the path-local congruence of the meaning and is left to task D, which owns A13.

**What each step owes: the witness's preservation, through two machine facts, now stated in the skeleton** (`Steps/Statements.lean`, area theorems `sorry`):
- (a) `substV_loan_keeps_stmt`: substituting a loan keeps every other loan. Attacked first, since a re-run that computes could drop a loan from an arm it no longer takes. Every sealed program is made by `closeCall`, whose only loan is its hole, the value written through the returned borrow (`*r := loan_k`) after the stuck head call, which cannot read it; what an [End] substitutes for the hole lands in the same place. So substituting a loan never makes a stuck program compute (read off the code). The one value a substitution makes recompute is an `Eq` type value holding a loan (`mkEqM`: `Eq(A, (loan_k, loan_m), (6, x))` at `k := 5` is `False`, dropping `m`, which the reference still reads at `k = 6`); no reachable state holds one, since type values are built by erased runs, whose reads end the loans first. It is the statement's hypothesis `htypes`, not a proved invariant. The re-normalisation that does drop loans is a refinement's, the worst case (`Regress/RenormHoles.lean`, built): `PickP(n, &a, &b)` stuck on `n`, then a split on `n`; in the arm `n = Z`, `b`'s fill computes to `b`'s old content and drops the hole. `DropArm` is accepted (`*r := (7, 7)` reaches `a`, `Id` by `refl` on `a.0`) and `DropArmWrong`, claiming `b.0` is `7` in that arm, is rejected. The witness survives because the reference reads the same arm under the same assignment: with `n = Z`, `b` means `PickP`'s backward function read at `b`, its old content, which reads no label (argued; the reference's side is not kernel-checked).
- (b) `closeCall_hole_stmt`: a call closed off with a borrow result `k` leaves, wherever a borrow argument's loan was, a fill holding the loan of `k` (read off `closeCall`). Where the callee's body runs instead, the reborrow it returns sits in the lent place's final content: that half belongs to `runBody_step_stmt`'s relation after the call.

The hardest access leaf, checked by reading: a read of a place whose content holds loans of early-ended labels. The reference's iterated access follows each such label into its holder's content (`ReachDep.via`); the checker substituted that content when it ended the label, so the content's loans are in the place's content, and the witness at the same place asks for exactly those.

### D28. The first step proof, the drop at the join: findings so far (fourth lead, 2026-11-19; in progress)

**1. The reference's drop of a borrow variable ended what it lends; the checker's does not** (`Regress/JoinBorrowRoot.lean`, built; a fault of the model, FIXED). `Machine.dropAtJoin` drops a place holding a borrow by ending that borrow and nothing else. `Run.joinDrop` accessed every dropped place first, observing a borrow variable's whole content, so it also ended every reborrow of a part of it. On the accepted `RJ`, `s` reborrows `(*r).0`, the join drops `r`, then `*s := 7` and `Id(Nat, clone(w), 7)` by `refl` (`w` is `x.0` read after). The reference had ended `s` at the join, so the write was lost and the `Id` was false in the model. `RJRead` (the arm taken reads `r` whole) and `RJGen` (unknown scrutinee) are rejected. Kernel-checked: the old access depends on a label its root reads (`old_access_depends`); the fix `Run.joinAccess` accesses nothing at a root holding a borrow (`joinAccess_borrow_root`), as `assignAt` already did. The proofs of `RunPres`, `RunEnv`, `RunOrd`, `RunCongr` and `Mono` that unfold `joinDrop` were adapted (a case split on `joinAccess_cases`). The four core lemmas still build on the standard axioms.

**2. The step statements fixed the run's budget to the structural fuel; the induction hypothesis did not apply to sub-calls** (a fault of the statements, FIXED). Every statement read `Runs (f fuel …) st fuel …`: the budget the machine's `Fuel` state starts with was the structural fuel. Inside a step at `fuel + 1`, a sub-call such as `endBorrow fuel l` runs with whatever budget is left, which only `evalCore`'s `tick` spends, so it can be `fuel + 1`: the hypothesis at `fuel` spoke only of runs with budget exactly `fuel`. Every hypothesis of the form `(h : Runs … st fuel …)` (70 of them, and `funType?`'s existential) now quantifies its budget (`(bh : Nat) (h : Runs … st bh …)`). Conclusions and the two top-level `checkDef`/`checkInd` statements keep theirs.

**3. A CHECKER FAULT: an early end makes the generic call's drop at the join skip a move an instance makes** (confirmed by the orchestrator on main's checker; being fixed on main as D90; the proof of the drop at the join waits for it). The program, with the `Nats` prelude:

```
def PickP (n : Nat) (x : &(Nat × Nat)) (y : &(Nat × Nat)) : &(Nat × Nat) := ( match n { Z => x, S _ => y } )
def EJ (n : Nat) (a : Nat × Nat) (b : Nat × Nat) : Nat × Nat := (
  let r = PickP(n, &a, &b);
  let a1 = a;
  let k = S(Z);
  match k { Z => (let z = (*r).0; Z), S m => Z };
  let bb = b;
  bb
)
```

Verdicts: `EJ` (generic `n`) is ACCEPTED. `EJ1`, the same body with `S(Z)` written for `n`, is REJECTED with "[D53] a borrow ends while its content is partly moved out ((⊥, σ5))", and so is the closed call `EJRun := EJ(1, (2, 3), (4, 5))` ("((⊥, 5))"); `EJ(0, …)` is accepted. What happens:
1. At the generic call `PickP` is stuck on `n` and closed off: `r` holds the returned borrow `k`, and the fills of both `a` and `b` hold the hole `k`.
2. `let a1 = a` reads `a`; its fill holds the hole, so [Access] ends `k`: `r` becomes `⊥`. Under an assignment with `n ≠ 0` nothing `a` means depends on `k`, so this end is early.
3. The match on the known `S(Z)` runs the arm `S`; its other arm moves `(*r).0`, so the join drops `(*r).0`.
4. `movedPlace` re-roots `(*r).0` to the longest prefix that exists, `r`, which is `⊥`, and `dropAtJoin` of a `⊥` does nothing: `b` stays whole.
5. At the instance `n = 1`, `PickP` returns a borrow of `b`, `r` is live, and the same drop moves `b.0` out through `r`; reading `b` afterwards (and ending `r`) fails.

The reference run follows the instance: it applies `PickP`'s meaning, keeps `k` live (it ends only what an observation depends on), drops `(*r).0` through `r`'s content, and fails when `b` is read under any assignment with `n ≠ 0`. So an accepted function goes wrong on a closed call (Target B), and part (a) of the theorem is false on an accepted program. No closed proof of `False` was found: every instance tried errs and is rejected rather than computing differently. The stuck-block form is fail-safe: with an unknown scrutinee (`EJGen`) the block's capture reads `*r` and the program is rejected ("no such place *r: its path does not exist in ⊥").

**4. The root cause, as a statement about the checker.** On the generic path `⊥` at a borrow variable can mean "ended early", a fact no instance need share (at an instance the borrow may be live), while [Join] reads `⊥` as "moved", which by D84 every path shares. Two readers do so. `staticJoin`'s entry set (`botPlaces`) counts the early-ended variable as moved; on its own this is harmless (D24: a move of a borrow variable covers only itself, and the drop of a `⊥` is a no-op on the checker's side, an end on the reference's). The exposure is the other reader, `movedPlace`'s re-rooting to the longest prefix that exists followed by `dropAtJoin`'s skip at `⊥`: a place reached THROUGH an early-ended borrow variable is dropped as the dead variable, a no-op, where an instance moves the lender's part. Agreed with the orchestrator's reading, with that refinement. Other readers of `⊥`-as-moved, as far as I know:
- `J` at convertible endpoints runs its transported term and then the same `joinDrop`: the same exposure (not probed).
- A stuck block's captures (`blockCaptures`) re-root through `movedPlace` too, but then read the captured place, and reading through a `⊥` is an error: fail-safe (`EJGen` rejected).
- [Id-typestate] reads moved places from syntax (`Decide.typeState`), not from `⊥`; a side that moves through an early-ended borrow variable fails at `content`: fail-safe.
- `repackAt` skips a `⊥` place ("left to the machine's own error"). At [Return] the places checked are borrow parameters, whose lenders are in the caller's frame, so their borrows cannot end early inside the body; I know no exposure there, but it reads `⊥` the same way.

**5. The plan of the proof of the drop at the join** (to resume once D90 is on main and spine is rebased onto it). Relation changes first, then lemmas, in this order:
- R1 (relation): the shape clause of `SimEq` two-sided on bindings. Today it says only that a borrow the checker holds, the reference holds (renamed). The reference's decisions read its own shape (`joinAccess` and `assignAt` skip the access at a root holding a borrow; `movedPlaces` reads a holder's content through `*r`), so a related state with an extra holder in the reference makes them differ. After the early ends, the reference holds a borrow at a binding only where the checker holds one, renamed.
- R2 (relation): semantic acyclicity of the reference state (`SState.SWF3`, or the run's own `Inv2`: a holder's content reads only labels above its own). Needed for ends in different orders to agree (`endLabel_comm_of_SWF3`): the reference ends in label order, the checker in its own, and `E` is a list. `Rel.start_simple` gives it at start states (holders' contents are loan-free there).
- R3 (relation): early-ended holders' contents have no moved part, in the reference. `movedPlaces` reads an early-ended holder through `*r`, where the checker's entry has the bare `⊥` variable; `joinEntry_stmt` holds only if that read finds nothing. True at reachable states: the checker's [End] requires a whole content outside an observation, and nothing touches an early-ended holder afterwards (after D90, a move through one is an error or a real move).
- S1 (statement, approved): `joinDrop_step_stmt` with the drop lists aligned (`DropAlign V drops (K.D.joinMoves (scopeOf s) entry mt)`): the reference's list is the checker's with elements inserted or deleted, each an early-ended borrow variable of `V` (`⊥` in the checker, an early-ended holder or `moved` in the reference), the order otherwise the same; the variables of `V` stay so through the drops.
- S2 (statements): `endBorrow_step_stmt` and `access_step_stmt` in the simulation form (from `Rel`, concluding `Rel` of the checker's state after and `s.endLabel (π l)`, respectively `s.access pos (obsAt … wholeOb)`, and `Good`), the induction-hypothesis fields the drop uses; then `refine_step_stmt`, `generalizeNeutral_step_stmt` and `substEnv_abs_step_stmt` likewise, with an extended assignment, for `movedPlace`'s other branches.
- L1 (lemma): ending an early-ended label in the reference keeps `Rel`, with that label out of `E` (R2 for the commutation; the witness because `ReachDep` after the end is `ReachDep` before through that label).
- L2 (lemma): a value's meaning commutes with writing `⊥` at a path: `meanV hint (v.updAt (fun _ => ⊥) steps) = Sem.updAt T (fun _ => moved) steps (meanV hint v)` where the path exists. It FAILS where a later field of a constructor on the path is read at a hint or a type that reads the written field (a dependent constructor whose later field is a sealed program, or whose later field's type mentions the written one: the audit's A13); so it is stated for constructors whose later fields do not read the written field, and task D owns the rest.
- L3 (lemma): the checker's `setPlace q ⊥` against the reference's `setAt pos (updAt moved steps)` keeps `Rel` (L2 for the equality; the witness because the new meaning is a function of the old, so it reads no new label, again A13 aside) and `Good` (membership by `MovedOf`, a part moved out of a member; the well-formedness clauses all survive removing a part; the assignment owes less).
- L4 (lemma): a proposition-typed value in Ω is `⋆`, so the checker's syntactic test `some .proof` and the reference's semantic test `.proof` agree (the checker makes every proof `⋆`; to be read off and stated as a clause or a lemma).
- L5 (lemma, the main case): one drop where the place exists in the checker's state (`movedPlace` returns it unchanged): `⊥` or `⋆` (both no-ops; the reference's access ends only early-ended labels, since the witness's `accLoans` of `⊥` and `⋆` is empty, then L1); a borrow (the [End] statement against the reference's `joinAccess`, the identity there, and its end of the label); copy data (the aligned decisions `copy_aligned`, `placeDecl_aligned`: both skip); other data (the [Access] statement, then L3).
- L6 (lemma): the list induction, from L5 and the extra-variable cases (a checker drop of a `⊥` variable is a no-op; a reference drop of an early-ended holder is its end, L1). This closes `joinDrop_step` for drops whose places exist.
- L7 (lemma): `movedPlace`'s other branches: through an unknown of a one-constructor type (η-refinement, `refine` in the simulation form, the assignment extended by the fields of `ρ σ`; the reference follows `ρ σ` directly), through a sealed program of such a type (generalisation, then η), through a lent place ([Access]), through anything else (the longest prefix that exists; after D90 never a dead borrow variable).
- L8 (lemma, for `joinEntry`): `botPlaces v q = Sem.movedIn (meanV hint v ψ) q` at a future giving every live label `err`, by induction on the value (`⊥`; `S`; a borrow at the root only, (WF1); a constructor value, `ctorM`'s tuple naming its fields as `botPlacesFields` does when a field is moved, its packed element when whole), with "a sealed program, a closure, an unknown and a global mean nothing with a moved part" (the run's results are whole or it fails; to prove about the run), then R1 and R3 for the early-ended variables.
The riskiest are L2/L3 at dependent constructors (A13: the step may have to exclude them until D) and L8's lemma about sealed programs' meanings.

### D29. Static lending in the reference run; the relation without early ends (fifth lead, 2026-11-19)

Main's D90 and D91 (`ochr/core/DECISIONS.md`) made when a borrow ends a decision every run of the checker shares: a place lent to a returned borrow holds a fill over it ([Lend]: a sealed program over the hole, or the new value form `lent_k v`, `Value.gloan`), a non-tail match and a computing `J` first access what the stuck block would capture ([Entry]), a stuck block does that before its arms are checked (D91), and `movedPlace` ends the loans of a fill before generalising it. So the generic call and every instance have the same borrows live, lending the same places, at every point (D90's alignment property, A1 to A4). Until now the reference run ended a borrow by what a place's meaning DEPENDS on, the checker by its loans, and the relation carried the gap: a list `E` of borrows the checker ended early, the loan witness `Wit` (D27) to pin the reference state where an early-ended label has another value, and a plan full of lemmas about both (D25, D27, D28). This entry makes the reference read lending the static way the checker does, and drops all of that.

**1. What `lent_k v` means.** The meaning of `v`; the mark has no meaning of its own (`Value.mean (.gloan _ w) = Value.mean w`). Where `v` holds the hole (`lent_k loan_k`, or the content with `loan_k` lower down) the hole is read from the future like any loan, so the place means its final content where `k` points into it and its current content elsewhere, which is the function of the future a sealed fill over `k` means at that instance. A mark says which borrow may point at a place; it is read by [Access] and [End], never by a meaning, `Eq` or conversion (D90: they never meet one).

**2. What the reference state carries, and its rules.** `SState.lent : Nat → List Site`, with `Site = (position, path, fill?)`: for each label, the places it is lent at. A plain borrow (`&p`) has one site, not a fill; a returned borrow has fills. The reference's [Access] reads only these sites, never a meaning.
- *[Borrow]* `&p` at `(pos, ψ)`: the fresh label's sites are `[(pos, ψ, plain)]`.
- *[Access]* of `(pos, ψ)`: end, in label order and to a fixpoint, every live label with a site `(pos, ψ', _)` whose path is a prefix of `ψ` or extends it (at, over or under the place; paths are compared by field, `.proj i` and `.field g` naming the same field). For a match (the tag only: `accessPath` and `accessNeutralHead`) only sites at a prefix of `ψ`, the place itself included. Before an assignment, at a root holding a borrow written whole, nothing (D67). This is `firstLiveLoanOnPath` (a loan, a mark, a sealed program on the path) with `accessInside` (the loans, marks and holes inside), read off sites. The read after it is at the state's closed future (every live label fixed to its holder's content), as a read that skips [Access] already was (D14).
- *[End ℓ]*: on meanings as before (`semEnd`). On sites, `lendV (.ended ℓ to)` with the substitution: each site `(r, *::π, f)` of any label, `r` being ℓ's holder, is replaced, for each site `(q, ψ, f')` of ℓ, by `(q, ψ, fill)` if `f'` is a fill (the whole place passes to the labels of ℓ's content, as a mark or a sealed fill's holes do) and by `(q, ψ ++ π, f)` if it is plain (a loan is replaced by the content, so a reborrow's loan lands at its own path); ℓ keeps no site. This is D90's transfer: a reborrow of `*ℓ` that outlives ℓ keeps every place ℓ was lent.
- *[Lend]* (rule 1): a call whose declared result is a borrow gives the fresh result label the union of its borrow arguments' sites, each a fill, and consumes the arguments, whether the checker runs the call or closes it off. A call with a data result consumes them.
- *[Entry]* (rule 2): a non-tail match, and `J`'s transported term, first access each place the stuck block would capture (a new aligned decision `Decisions.blockCaps`, the checker's `blockCaptures` in the checker's order; D90's obligation 1 is the content of its alignment statement), as data. For a match whose declared type is a borrow, the sites of the borrow variables it would take by move are snapshot before the arm; after the arm and the join, the result label gains a fill at each place it would take by `&` and at each snapshot site.
- *Tail position*: `run` carries a tail flag, as the checker's `checkTail` and its untyped runs' `tailNext` do: set for a function's body, passed to a `let`'s body, a sequence's tail, the arms of a tail match and the one arm of a match on a proof, false everywhere else (`J`'s transported term and a sealed program's re-run included). A tail match has no entry (D90's `G4`).
- *The block*: the reference never forms one (it reads the tag of the scrutinee's meaning). The checker's block (D91: entry, then the arms, then the block call, a rule-1 call when it returns a borrow) ends and lends exactly what the reference's non-tail match clause does.
- *Ends the reference used to leave to the checker* ("below" in the audit), now made, since the two must hold the same live borrows at every point: a discarded borrow at `t; u` ends (`dropValue`); at a `let`'s end a binding holding no borrow ends the live labels with a site at it (D65, `endLoansOfTopBind`); a closure's captured places are accessed, oldest first, before they are moved (`capture`); the join's drop of a place first accesses, whole, the shortest strict prefix of it that holds a site of a live label (`movedPlace`'s access of a lent parent, point 5 f), then the place.

**3. The one-sided invariant.** At a related state, the content of a place at a path reads the future only at non-live labels and at live labels with a site comparable with that path. It is not a clause: it follows from the readings clause (the reference's meaning is the checker's value's at every compared future, and the compared futures leave every live label free) and from a path-local form of `meanV_congr` (a value's meaning at a path reads the future only at the loans, marks and holes at comparable paths), which holds for hint-free values (point 5 e). So after an access the content reads no live label, and the reference's read at the closed future is the checker's read. The core lemmas do not need it: the access consults no meaning, and a read at the closed future is congruent in the future (`closedFuture_read`), so `run_congr` loses its dependence machinery (`obsDep`, `access_FT`) rather than gaining an invariant. Two clauses of the checker's well-formedness go with it, read off D90 and owned by the steps that keep them: (WF12) under a fill only fills (every site strictly under a fill of a live label belongs to a label with a fill at or above it: a call lends places borrowed whole, and anything that later reaches under a fill ends it first), and (WF3) on sites (no label is lent inside its own holder's content, transitively), which makes the ends within one access commute.

**Everything the reference run does reads its state only at futures that agree with the run's off the live labels**: an access reads sites, a read is at the closed future, [End] reads the holder at the same future with the ended label updated, a call's backward functions are read at the arguments' labels. Those are exactly the futures the readings clause compares. So the relation determines the run, and no witness is needed to pin the reference where an early-ended label has another value: there are no early-ended labels, and where a non-live label has another value the run never looks.

**4. The relation** `Rel K ρ φ π st s`:
- *skel*, *fresh*: as before.
- *holders*: a binding holds the borrow `l` in the checker exactly when it holds `π l` in the reference; a borrow a temporary of the checker holds (a value in flight) is held under `π l` by a temporary of the same frame with the same content, and no temporary of the reference holds a label the checker does not hold; `π` is injective on the checker's live labels. So the live labels correspond one to one.
- *sites*: for every live label `l` of the checker, the reference's sites of `π l` are the checker's sites of `l` (`sitesOf`: its loans not inside a fill of `l`, its marks, the sealed programs among whose holes it is), compared as sets.
- *readings*: `SimEq` at every future that agrees with `φ` off the reference's live labels, with no early ends: bindings equal position by position, temporaries by label. A moved part is the meaning `moved` on both sides, so the moved places are the same.

**5. The cases that broke earlier designs.**
- (a) *`Regress/CondDep.lean`* (an injective backward function making the dependence on `m` conditional on `j`'s content). The reference's access no longer asks what a place depends on, so the conditional dependence cannot make it end anything the checker does not. At the reachable form, `k`'s End hands both `j` and `m` a fill at `x` on both sides, and both accesses of `x` end both. The unreachable form (the checker holding the constant `c` at `x`) has no site at `x`; the readings clause, at futures that leave `j` and `m` free, makes the reference's `x` constant, so nothing reads `m` there either.
- (b) *A reborrow outliving the borrow it came from* (D90's `Census2.G8`): `r = Pick(n, &a, &b)` gives `k` fills at `a` and `b`; `s = &(*r).0` is plain at `(r, *.0)`; `r`'s scope ends. [End k] re-roots `s`'s site through `k`'s fills: `s` now has fills at `a` and `b`, on both sides (the checker hands `k`'s marks, or the holes of its sealed fills, to `s`). Reading `b` ends `s`, at the generic call and at `n = 0` alike.
- (c) *A fill re-normalised in a split world* (`Regress/RenormHoles.lean`, D90's `Census3.SplitKeeps`). The checker's [Seal] now keeps a re-run fill a fill over the same holes (`lentTo holes v`), so its sites do not change under a refinement; the reference never re-normalises. D27's refutation of a syntactic tie (a hole dropped by a split) was of the old checker.
- (d) *The join through a borrow* (`EJ`). Reading `a` ends `k` on every path (a fill over `k`), so `r` is `⊥` on both sides when the join drops `(*r).0`; both drop nothing. Where `r` is live, both drop the lender's part through it.
- (e) *An open dependent value* (agreed with the user: inside a body the checker types a later field of an open value by what it holds, not by the earlier field). The reference's field read re-read the earlier field through the borrow: its observation `wholeOb` paired the content with its type, the field's type at the earlier fields' meanings at the future, so reading `x.1` depended on, and ended, the borrow of `x.0`. The fix: the type of a field read is read with the earlier fields at their current contents (the closed future), as the checker's `depFieldType` reads them through the borrow (`ctorFields`); and the access no longer observes at all. A value's own meaning reads its position's type only through a hint, and no value in Ω reads one (WF10; [Close]'s programs are top-hint-free, `HintIndep.lean`). So the path-local congruence holds at open values, and reading `x.1` ends nothing lent from `x.0`, as in the checker. What stays for row 4: the checker types a field whose content does not fit the type its earlier fields give by the content (`valType?`), which touches the binding's type (skel), not lending.
- (f) *The forms of a fill at the join.* At the generic call a dropped field under a sealed fill does not exist, so `movedPlace` accesses the parent whole; at an instance the mark is transparent and only the field is accessed. By (WF12) the two end the same labels, so the reference's one rule (access the shortest site-holding prefix whole) matches both. Under a plain loan the checker accesses the parent whole on every path, ending a reborrow of a sibling field; so does the reference.
No case was found that the design does not survive. Recorded as known and outside this lane: main's `CLg` (D90's last bullet: `clone(x.0)` counted as a move by a one-arm match's join), on which part (a) of the theorem is false.

**6. What survives of D28's plan.** R1 is absorbed in *holders*. R2 survives as (WF3) on sites, for the commutation of ends within one access. R3 is gone (no early-ended holders). S1 is gone: the drop lists are the same list (the same entry set, the same `joinMoves`). S2 survives: `endBorrow_step_stmt` and `access_step_stmt` in the simulation form, then `refine_step_stmt`, `generalizeNeutral_step_stmt` and `substEnv_abs_step_stmt` with an extended assignment. L1 is gone. L2 survives, its A13 exception closed by 5 e for hint-free values. L3 survives without its witness half. L4 survives. L5 and L6 survive without their early-ended cases. L7 survives; its lent-place branch is `movedPlace`'s fill branch, matched by the reference's prefix access (5 f). L8 survives without R1 and R3: with no early ends a borrow variable is `⊥` on both sides or on neither.

**Deleted with the early ends** (a note of what they were): `Rel.early`'s list `E` of borrows the checker ended early, compared after ending them in the reference; the loan witness `Wit` with `ReachDep` (what the reference's semantic access could reach), `accLoans`, `loanOnPath`, `insideLoans`, `holderContent` and `pathReaches` (what the checker's access ends, read off Ω); `Regress/EarlyUnpinned.lean` (an unpinned reference state at an early-ended label's other values), `Regress/WitNonVacuity.lean` (the witness checked both ways) and `Regress/CondDep.lean` (case (a)), whose findings are recorded in D25 and D27.

### D30. The drop at the join, first proof: what the statements lacked (fifth lead, 2026-11-19; in progress)

Task C: the drop at the join proved end to end. The loop is proved from the drop of one place; the drop of one place is not yet proved. Four things the statements lacked, each fixed in the open:

1. **A run nested in a `do` block was not a `Runs`.** `Runs x st fuel a st'` was a run from the EMPTY log; the drop's loop runs each body from the log the earlier bodies left, so no step statement could be used at its nested occurrences. `Runs` is now a run from some starting log (`∃ L`); the top-level run is the case `L = #[]` (`Runs.of_runMachine`). Nothing reads the log, so no statement changes in strength in practice; `Runs.unique` (determinism over one log) went with it, and its one use, `restoreKeep_spec`, is proved directly.
2. **A step that creates unknowns has no context to hand on** (D20 item 7, met for real). `movedPlace` η-refines an unknown, creating field unknowns, and the next drop runs under a context whose `Δ` must give their types (`CtxOk`'s `DeltaOk`). The drop statements now conclude under `{ K with Δ := Δ' }`, `Δ'` agreeing with `K.Δ` on the old unknowns, and the induction hypothesis an area file gets is at every run context (`∀ K' ρ φ, StepsIH K' ρ φ fuel`; the knot is `stepsIH : ∀ fuel K ρ φ`). The reference's drop reads no `Δ` (`joinDropOne_withΔ`, `rfl`).
3. **The drop list's grain** (`GrainOk`). `movedPlace` re-roots a place that does not exist to its longest existing prefix, and η-refines or generalises that prefix only when its type has one constructor; the reference never re-roots. Without D84's grain (`jdGrain`: a field step only through a declared one-constructor inductive) the two drop different places (the checker the prefix whole, the reference the field). The drop statements assume it of their list, and that the list moves no copy (the join moves only what is not a copy); the match statements discharge both from `joinMoves_aligned`.
4. **The scope the list is read in** is the same at every step: the reference's drop keeps every binding's declared reading (`declsOf_joinDropOne`, `scopeOf_joinDropOne`, proved).

**Proved, no `sorry` of their own** (standard axioms): the loop taken apart (`runs_dropLoop_cons`, `runs_dropLoop_nil`), the scope kept, and `joinDrop_step` from `dropOne_step` by induction on the list (`Steps/Match.lean`, `Steps/JoinDropLib.lean`; `#print axioms joinDrop_step` shows `sorryAx` through `dropOne_step` only).

**Open: `dropOne_step`**, the drop of one place, a case analysis on `movedPlace` and `dropAtJoin` against `accessLentPrefix` and `dropAt`. What it rests on: the [Access] and [End] statements in the simulation form (IH fields); `refine_step_stmt` and `generalizeNeutral_step_stmt` restated in the simulation form with an extended context; (WF12) for a mark at a strict prefix (the checker accesses the field, the reference the marked prefix whole: the same labels end); L2/L3 of D28 (writing `⊥` at a path commutes with the meaning, for hint-free values, and keeps the sites of the other labels); and a lemma tying the checker's place (its `varPos`) to the reference's `resolve` through `Rel.skel`.

### D31. The reference reads data by its declaration's name, not by its carrier (fifth lead, 2026-11-19; fixed)

**Finding (kernel-checked, `Regress/DataOfChoice.lean`; a model fault).** Found attacking L2, the drop's write of `⊥` at a path. The reference finds its way inside a constructor value through the value's TYPE MEANING: the tag a match reads (`indTag`), a field read (`Sem.follow`, `Sem.stepField`), a write or drop at a path (`Sem.updAt`) and [Rec]'s rank all decode the value's meaning through `TyM.dataOf`, the first declaration of `Γ` whose carrier is the type's, with parameters chosen classically (`DataModel.paramsOf`). Carriers do not determine declarations: two declarations can have equal carriers, which Lean can neither prove nor refute for two types of the same size, and a data model may make true. Then the reference decodes the value as the other declaration's.

The file's model satisfies every law of `DataModel` and covers both declarations of `B1 := T1 | F1`, `B2 := F2 | T2`, each with the carrier `Bool`, `B1` storing `T1` as `true` and `B2` storing `T2` as `true`. The checker's `T2` means `true` at the type `B2`, which means `ty0 Bool`; the reference's match reads its tag through `B1` and gets constructor `0` (`tag_wrong`), so on `match T2 { F2 => Z, T2 => S(Z) }` it computes `Z` where the checker computes `S(Z)`, and `Id(Nat, PickT2, S(Z))`, which the checker proves by `refl` (asserted beside the rejected `Id(…, Z)`), is false in the model. Field reads, writes and drops read the other declaration's fields and write its name into the result the same way. So every step lemma that finds its way inside data is false at this model, and unprovable at the generic one: a proof would have to refute that an earlier declaration's carrier equals the value's, which Lean cannot do for two types of the same size.

**Fix (done).** Decode by the type's structured data, `TyS.data`: the declaration's name and its parameters' meanings, which a binding's type (`Value.strS`, `Value.tyData`, through `&` too) and a field's hint (`DataModel.fieldData`) already carry (D8). `TyS.dataDecl` finds the declaration by name; `Sem.follow`, `Sem.step`, `Sem.stepField` and `Sem.updAt` take a `TyS` and give a field its `TyS` (`fieldHint`: the hint `Value.meanFields` reads the field at); `indTag`, `obsAt`, `tagOf`, `contentOf`, `setContent`, `borrowPlace` and `dropAt` pass the position's `TyS`. The four core lemmas are again proved with no `sorry` (standard axioms); the change to their proofs was the signature only. `Regress/DataOfChoice.lean` now shows the fixed match reading `T2` as constructor `1` (`tag_right`) and keeps the fact the counterexample rested on (`carrier_names_B1`); the counterexample itself is kept, unbuilt, in `Regress/DataOfChoice_189132fd.lean`. Left as they were: [Rec]'s rank keeps `dataOf` (a parameter's type is a `TyM`; a wrong rank only shortens the recursion's guard, so the run fails where the checker succeeds: an adequacy gap, not a false proposition), and a field read's result type was still `.plain` (its data dropped), which after the fix left a binding of a read field with no declaration to decode it by, so `let y = x.1; y.0` failed in the reference: `contentOf` now gives a field the structured type `Sem.follow` gives it (its declaration's hint, with data), with no label (same day; the core lemmas unchanged).

What the fix rests on. Decoding at the type's data is the value's own decoding when the value's recorded parameters mean the type's data parameters (`fieldsOf_ctorM`); a constructor value recording no parameters reads them from the hint already. That is a typing fact of the checker (it converts a constructor's type to the position's parameter by parameter), to be a clause of `Good` (data agreement) kept by the steps. Two parameter lists of equal carrier within one declaration (`pack_congr`, D8's note) are then never compared. Navigation through a parameter-typed field reads `fieldData`, whose agreement with the run at such fields is D8's open item (parameters carry their own structured hint), now needed for field paths too.

**Same day, the number type too.** `Sem.step` and `Sem.updAt` tested "is this `Nat`?" by the carrier (`T.m = ty0 Nat`), so a declaration a model gives the carrier `Nat` would have been read as a number: the same fault. They now test `TyS.isNat`: the type names no declaration and its carrier is `Nat`; a declaration's values are decoded by its name first. The data agreement is a clause of `Good`, (WF13) (`DataAll` in `WF.lean`), read off the checker and not proved preserved.

### D32. Data values describe themselves (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; decided: (a); BUILT)

**The root.** Two faults in two days share one cause: the model reads a data value with information from somewhere other than the value. D31 guessed the declaration from the carrier; `Regress/PartialMoveDep.lean` (accepted `JK`) computes a field's type from its siblings after one of them is moved, and the reference's drop into the field then does nothing where the checker's writes `⊥`. The checker does neither: a constructor value records its own declaration and parameters (D88, D89), and an open value's field is typed by its content. In Lean's own model (docs/33 entries 3, 4, 14; Carneiro §6.4) a type means a tagged code, and an element of an inductive type is a ZFC tree, which describes itself; only our encoding, where an element is an opaque inhabitant of a Lean carrier (`el0 (car d ps) x`), lost that. Decoding such an element needs `(d, ps)` from outside, and a cast between equal carriers is not provably the identity on fields, which is why every route so far has needed an invariant (WF13) or a guess.

**The options, and what each does.**
- **(a) Self-describing data values.** A constructor value means `dat d ps c fs`: its declaration, its parameters' meanings, its constructor and its fields' meanings, whole or partly moved alike (it replaces both `el0`-packing and the `ctor` tuple). A data type means a code naming `d` and `ps` (the data part of (c)), whose decoding is the carrier `car d ps`; membership is "packs at the code" (`DataModel.pack`), and carriers are met only where a Lean function on carriers is applied (`dec`/`enc` at Π boundaries, which need the code at the codomain, so a call's result must carry its data: the data half of the call-result redesign). Navigation (tag, field read, write, drop) reads only the value. Partial moves: fixed, since a field is decoded by itself, never through a type computed from moved siblings. Deleted: (WF13), `DataAll`, `DataOn`, `WholeAlong`, `dataOn_of_wf13`, the open-dependent-value residual, D31's `dataDecl` navigation, and [Rec]'s `dataOf` (the rank is the packed element's). Cost: `Sem` and `TyM` become mutual (`TyM.data` holds `List Sem`); `ctorM`, `fieldsOf`, `MovedOf`, `pmem`, `Sem.mem` and `Apply`'s `dec`/`enc` change; `DataGeneric` is untouched (carriers are still built, as the codes' decodings). Re-proof: the four core lemmas where they unfold data meanings (`RunCongr`'s constructor and match cases, `RunSubst`, `HintIndep`), `mean_updAt_bot` (simpler: no hint conditions), the toy models in `Mono`, `DataOfChoice`, `SiblingAccess`, `PartlyMoved`; the relation's form is unchanged. Estimate: a few days.
- **(b) A type skeleton per position, written by content.** The reference keeps, beside each position's meaning, the checker's typing by content (declaration and parameters at each constructor), set by writes. Partial moves: fixed. It removes `WholeAlong` and the residual but replaces (WF13) by a new clause of the relation (the reference's skeleton is the checker's values' recorded types), and every state operation carries the skeleton through the core lemmas. It duplicates in the reference what the checker's values already record; it is not Lean's way.
- **(c) Types as codes closed under every former.** `TyM` becomes a family of codes indexed by their decodings (Π's code holds a function from arguments to codes). It closes the faults where a call's result, a computed function type and a field read lose structure (the audit's A11, A12, A18), and `TyS`'s optional fields go. Partial moves: NOT fixed: a field's code is still computed from its siblings, and Lean has no moves. It does not remove (WF13)'s parameter half either: an opaque carrier element still needs its type's parameters to be decoded. Cost: the largest (every `TyM` consumer, `Apply`, `Pi`, `Apply1`).

- **(d) Tighten the checker's join drop** (the team lead's option): `dropAtJoin` performs a move's checks, computing the place's type, so a field whose type reads a moved field cannot be dropped any more than moved. The reading behind it is right for MOVES: every other navigation into such a field computes its type and refuses (`PartialMoveDep`'s `Direct`, `JKRead`, `JGen`: "[Read] n was moved out"; [Repack] computes field types too). Other places that act at a path without computing a type do not decode in the reference: the access (`accessPath`/`accessInside`) is mirrored by sites only (D29); D65's ends at a `let`'s end and a frame's pop act on whole bindings; [End] writes back by fixing the future. So `dropAtJoin` is the one hole for moves, and (d) would reject only drops into a field whose type reads a moved field: Rust has no such types, and the direct forms of that code are already rejected; `JK` would be rejected like its unknown-`k` form, which aligns the two. With (d), `WholeAlong` holds at every navigation the reference must mirror. BUT (d) does not reach OPEN values made by a WRITE: `Regress/OpenWriteDep.lean`, `OWK` (accepted): the index is rewritten whole (`w.0 := S(S(Z))`), then `(w.1).1` is read; the checker types `w.1` by its content (`Pk(S(w0))`), the reference decodes it at the hint from the current earlier fields (`Pk(2)`). The earlier field is whole, so this is not a move case, and (WF13) as restricted to fields after whole earlier fields is FALSE there (now restricted to the top constructor, `WF.lean`; `dataOn_of_wf13` to paths entering one constructor). So under (d), as now, decoding by the position's type is not the value's own decoding at every place the reference navigates; only (a) or (b) cover open values.
- **Call results, under any option that decodes by type.** A data call's result is typed `.plain B` (`callOfL`, `Run.lean` line 426): no data. Since D31, a match or a field read on a `let`-bound call result therefore fails in the reference (`DataOfChoice.indTag_plain`, kernel-checked), where before it decoded by the carrier guess. So the data half of D22's call-result redesign (a call's result takes its data, and shape, from the callee's declared result type) is not a later row under (c), (d) or the current model: it is needed now. Under (a) navigation does not need it, but encoding a Lean function's result as a self-describing value needs the codomain's code, which is the same redesign.

**Recommendation: (a), with the data part of (c) it requires (the call-result redesign's data half included), now; the Π part of (c) with the rest of the call-result redesign.** (d) is a sound tightening that makes a known and an unknown scrutinee agree, but it is not needed under (a), and it does not by itself make decoding by type correct (`OWK`). (a) is the value-side half of Lean's model (tagged codes for types, self-describing trees for elements); (c) is the type-side half, and needs (a) or (WF13) beside it for data. (b) is rejected: it maintains by hand what (a) gets from the value. On which departs from Lean less: Lean's model looks at a value only through its type, and its elements describe themselves (ZFC trees); (a) keeps the second and is what lets the first stay harmless with moves and open values; (b) adds state Lean has no counterpart for. With (a) nothing beyond Lean's theory is needed for moves: a partly moved value is a tree with `⊥` leaves. Lean's theory has no moves, so the one choice (a) adds is that a partly moved or ill-typed (open) constructor value is still a `dat`, outside its type's code but navigable.

**Decision (team lead, 2026-11-20): (a), with the data part of (c), call results' data included.** Option (d) (the join's drop performing a move's checks) was considered and is not needed under (a): it would remove an asymmetry worth remembering, `JK` accepted at a known scrutinee and rejected at an unknown one, but it is not sufficient alone (`OWK`), and the checker is left alone.

**The sweep of `Good`'s read-off clauses, first: not buildable from what the checker records.** The `located` mode records, per term, the term's value and type (`Note.value`), a tail term's goal, a rule fired, and at a hole `?` its goal and the bindings of the top frame and frame 0 (`Note.hole`). It does not record Ω at a term: no temporaries, no lower frames, no unknowns' types, no goal or records, so none of (WF11) (no stored type or record holds a loan), (WF12) (under a live fill only what ending it reaches) or "no live fill above a moved or proof part" can be evaluated on recorded states (liveness alone needs every position). What is missing is one opt-in note: the whole `MState` (or its `env`, `absTy`, `goal`, `neutrals`, `etaRefs`) at each located term, behind a `Config` flag that is off in every verdict-bearing run.
