Proved: `botWrite_rel`, `botWrite_good`, `copyRead_aligned`, `grain_deref_not_proof` (in the restated form). On the lane's branch three of the leaves are reduced further, and recording a fresh η-refinement is proved (`etaRecord_ext`): `ctx_botWrite` to the block's captures after the write (`blockCaps_botWrite`, D90's obligation 1 at the state after, a fact about the checker alone); `recordedNeutral_ok` to `recordFacts_of_good` (records loan-free and top-hint-free; not derivable from `Good`, swept with no violation on 72,076 suite and 739 probe states) and the lookup's key; `etaFresh_sim` to a simulation form of split coverage.

**Findings for the statements.** `split_coverage` as stated gives SOME run at its own budget and SOME constructor, and its assignment says nothing of the fresh fields, which nothing names yet; a form that can be used is proposed (`CovConcl`, `Slice/EtaFresh.lean`). The same gap is in `RefineOk`: `refine_step_stmt` and `substEnv_abs_step_stmt` look false when the refinement names an unknown nothing names (no counterexample built). The block's captures should be a hypothesis of the [Entry] step, not a clause `CtxOk` asks of every state.

**Findings for the checker, each now a decision of main.** An arm no run takes put `*` on a proof into the join's list (`Regress/GrainDerefProof`, `StepKindsTable`): main's D101. The join's dead test for `⋆`: main's D102. A join whose access of a lent prefix ended the borrow of the result in flight, rejected only later by an unrelated rule (`Regress/JoinPrefixBorrow`, thirteen variants): main's D106. The lookup of a recorded neutral by `==`, which ignores recorded parameters (probed: about 36,500 matches over the suite and the probes, every one equal up to binder names): main's D108.

### D94. The strict run is built, and is `Run.lean`'s `runT` (model lane, 2026-11-20 to 21; prototype 29288e0d, port a24fc76f)

**What.** D83's specification, built first as a copy (`runS`) and then ported: there is one reference run, and it is strict. `SM.strictS R x` fails unless the result's value is a member of the result's type. Strict points: `.ascribe` (at the annotation's code; a label on the result must record that type, `labTyOk`); `.succ`; `.ctor` (around `ctorOut`, with written parameters members of their parameter types, `paramsOk`); `.fix` (the closure a member of its function type); `.borrow` (the content at the place's type) and `&A` at `Type₀`; `.assign` (the value against the place's type AT THE WRITE, `placeTy`, read on a copy after the right-hand side ran); `.call` (`BorrowArgsTyped`); `.val` through `strictVal`. Reads and ends are not strict. Failure is `none`; strictness only adds failures.

**Where the build departs from D83, and why.**
- *Every erased proof is `⋆` at `prop True`*, not at the proposition it proves (ruling 1, generalised). D83's (C1) had each proof primitive return its proposition; but `cong`, `symm`, `trans` and `rewrite` take theirs from an `Eq` proof's sides, and `prop (a = b)` forgets `a` and `b`. So `SM.erasedIf` runs the clause on a copy and, where it ran to `⋆`, returns `⋆` at `prop True`; the clause's own type is what the lemma checks at the clause (`erasedS_clause_true`), and relating it to the checker's proposition is the simulation's. `SM.erasedAt` is deleted.
- *Borrow invariance at a call is a strict point D83 did not list* (ruling 2): each argument at a borrow parameter carries a LIVE label whose record's type is the parameter's content type.
- *`moved`, not `err`, where a returned borrow's final content does not decode* (`callOfL`; ruling 3, taken after a first ruling for strict ends was reversed on the lane's evidence): `err` there broke the invariant, and typed futures are at PARTLY MOVED members. The checker's "no end over moved-out content" is the named fact `EndWhole`, which the lemma does not use.
- *An erased match on a proof with other than one arm* runs its arms in order, each on a copy, and is the first that runs to a proof (`SM.anyArm`, 9a3cf614). At the port such a match failed, while the checker accepts `Or` eliminated into a proposition (`Subsingletons.OrComm`). The form the lead preferred (succeed iff some constructor's fields hold and every such arm runs) needs the scrutinee's record, and the run has none: a proof's value is `⋆`, and a `Prop`-sorted type is `prop (Nonempty car)`.
- *`strictVal` fails outright on a constructor value whose meaning is `err`*, as on embedded code, instead of testing it at the hint: otherwise a hint-free tail read the hint (found porting `HintIndep`).
- *Strict `.fix`* (ruling 6): a closure whose body fails somewhere is `err` and fails, so every `fix` formed in a run owes the local knot (D83's row 11).
- `bodyAt` records a borrow parameter's `heldTy` as well as its `heldMem`.

**After the port.** The core lemmas are back on the standard axioms (`RunPresInd`, `RunCongr`, `AssignCongr`, `RunSubst`, `RunSubstAbs`, `HintIndep`, `Lemmas`); `SubstAbs.absFill_shape` gained the hypothesis that the filler is a member of `Δ σ`. No file under `Steps/` changed. Concrete runs flipped by design: `(0 : Unit)` now fails (`OpenFalse.run_ascribe_strict`); erased terms run on a copy; `ReturnedRead`'s lender reads `moved` where the checker's mark does. Kernel-checked in `Strict/Runs.lean`: `zero_unit_none`, `let_zero_unit_none`, `succ_nat_runs`, `refl_runs`, `refl_eq_runs`, `refl_neq_none`, `sort_two_runs`.

### D95. The invariant `SGood` and the fundamental lemma `fund_all` (model lane, 2026-11-21; as first built: every clause, three guesses, and a context D98 found empty; false at a field write, D104; PROVED since, D105)

**The invariant** (`Strict/Good.lean`), a predicate on reference states alone, at every TYPED future (`TypedF`: agreeing with the run's future off the live labels, and giving each live label a partly moved member, `PMem`, of its recorded `heldTy`): each binding's value is `GoodVal` of its stored type; a position holding label `l` reads a `PMem` of `heldTy l`; each live label's record `heldMem` is a member of `heldTy`; a binding holding `l` is stored at `heldTy l`; the label order of `RunPresInd`; `ReadsBelow`; and the read clause `ReadOk`. A result is good (`GoodRes`) when it is a whole member of its type, or `moved`, or a value with no data (a type value or `⋆`) at `err`. The escape at `err` is there because the bounded model has no universe for `Type₁` as a value, and a field of `⋆` is read at `err`; a value with data never comes out at `err`.

**The lemma** (`Strict/Fund.lean`): `fund_all (hc : FundCtx K) : ∀ t, t.valsOk (ValsP K ρ φ) → Fund K ρ φ t`, where `Fund` says that from an `SGood` state every successful run of `t`, at every hint and tail flag, returns a good result whose label records its type and is live or unread, and leaves an `SGood` state. One structural induction over the term, mutual with argument lists and arms, one lemma per clause (`Strict/Simple.lean`, `Clauses.lean`, `Match.lean`, `Clauses2.lean`; the operations in `Ops.lean`, `OpsCall.lean`, `OpsPlace.lean`). The context `FundCtx`: `reads : MemReads`; each global a good result with no label; no data declaration at `Type₁`. The precondition `valsOk` is ruling 5: an embedded unknown is good when the assignment gives it a member of its type, an embedded loan when the future does.

**Three guesses** (`Strict/OpsPlace.lean`, `sorry`): `setContent_field_moved_sgood` (moving out of a field), `borrowPlace_field_sgood` (lending a field), `setContent_field_sgood` (assigning a field). Each may be false as stated: a later dependent field's read type changes while its value does not. `fund_join` reaches them through the join's drop, so `fund_matchInd` shows `sorryAx` too. They are answered by D100.

**Where `SGood` is established** (for the simulation side). From the checker's invariant only at the start of a definition's body and at the empty state, where no place is lent; the borrow parameters' records must be set there. Then carried by the lemma, since every reference state is produced by the strict run. NOT covered by the lemma: the call run in place (`CallRef.runCallRef`: `callFrame`, the body's run, `popCallFrame`, `SState.endInto`) and a closure body's frame (`bodyAt`); those frame lemmas are not written.

**A seam, named by D96:** `SGood` speaks of futures whose live labels end at partly moved members, which is more than the checker's invariant speaks of, and the relation compares at futures giving WHOLE members.

### D96. Part (II) stated against an abstract strict run (sixth lead, 2026-11-20; `Steps/StrictSim.lean`, ea100c09; stated)

Written before D94 existed, against the interface it would supply: `StrictRun` (a run with `runT`'s signature, a predicate `SGood`, and the fundamental lemma as a field), `SimConclS` (D81's `SimConclTT` with the strict run), and `eval_step_stmtS`: the evaluation step in (II)'s form, with `SGood` of the reference state, no hole in the term (`Term.hasHolePrim`), `hok` on the strict run, and the value in flight typed at the type returned, in untyped runs too. `evalMem_of_strict` (proved): the evaluated value's membership, from (II)'s conclusion and (I). `membershipT_of_sgood` (a guess): the bridge from the reference's `SGood` to the checker's typed positions, with the open point that `MembershipT` asks every typed future, including ones the relation does not compare. Not yet instantiated on D94's run, and the 81 statements are not yet in this form.

### D97. The type formers are strict, so a value with a meaning is well typed inside (the orchestrator's decision on the trial's advice; model lane, 2026-11-21; 9911399e, 17591fbe; built)

**Why.** Membership says nothing of the parts of a value that do not decide it (D101's first counterexample: `Eq(Nat, And(True, Nat), Nat)` is a member of `Prop`). Conversion compares part by part, so it needs every part typed. The alternative was to carry the trial's typing predicate as an invariant through every step.

**What.** The run fails, and the value meanings mirror it so that the knot `meanV_eq_mean` still ties: `.eq`/`.id` and `.tEq` unless `EqOk` (the type's meaning has a LEVEL and each side is `moved` or a member); `.tind` and `.tInd` unless `paramsOk`; `.pi` unless `PiOk` (every domain and the codomain have a level at every tuple of arguments that are members of the earlier domains); `.clo` means `err` and `.tPi` means `ty err` unless `CapsOk` (each capture a member of its recorded type). `EqOk` first asked a universe; a level was taken instead, since the bounded model has no universe holding a level-3 type and `Eq(Type₁, X, Y)` would have been excluded. Not mirrored: a deferred parameter type of a Π-closure, a closure's own function type, and a constructor VALUE's recorded parameters (the trial's conversion proof does not use them).

**The payoff** (`Strict/Typed.lean`, proved): `HWT K ρ φ h T v`, the trial's predicate adapted (the meaning read at an explicit hint; the head condition `Fit`; code excepted: captures typed, the code not), and `hwt_of_fit`: a value whose meaning fits `T` is `HWT` at `T`, with ONE side condition, `NoProp` (no constructor value of a `Prop` declaration). Its first version also needed a coherence condition on every type read; D99 removed it. Witnesses: `hwt_witness`, `hwt_witness_side` (the lemma applied at a concrete instance of its whole context), `eq_types_not_hwt` (`Eq(Nat, Nat, Nat)` is `HWT` at no type). `hwt_of_run` composes it with `fund_all` and inherits its state.

**Cost.** The four clauses' cases of the fundamental lemma and of `RunPresInd`; the other core lemmas needed only `open Classical`. One obligation moved to the simulation: the `Eq` step must give the strict `Eq`'s test (`EqJAsm`'s `hEqOk`, a named `sorry`).

### D98. `MemReads` holds in no model, so the fundamental lemma was vacuous (model lane, 2026-11-21; kernel-checked at 9911399e; the record is `Strict/MemReadsCE.lean`)

**Found.** `FundCtx.reads` asked that every whole value a read path reaches in a partly moved member be a member of the type the read gives it. A read typed a field by the declaration it found BY NAME in `Γ`, and the universal signature (D92) gives data codes, with members, to declarations `Γ` does not declare. `Fresh.C(5)`, at a record giving its field a `Nat` leaf, is a member of `Fresh` at that record in every model, and its field read at `err`. So for every data model and every `Γ` declaring nothing named `Fresh`, `MemReads dm Γ` was false (`not_memReads`, in the file as it was at 9911399e and a5c24576; the port replaced it by `fresh_read`), `FundCtx K` had no instance, and `fund_all` and everything stated under it held vacuously.

**The rule it left.** Every bundle of hypotheses gets a concrete instance checked by the kernel, and the lemma is applied to at least one concrete run, before it is reported proved.

**Status.** D99 removes this refutation and leaves another. `MemReads` is still a field of `FundCtx` at 2afd418c. (Deleted since, with `ReadOk`: reads are strict, D105.)

### D99. A read is typed by the type it starts at (route (c)); what survives of the read hypothesis (model lane, 2026-11-21; prototype a5c24576, port 2afd418c; built)

**Considered.** Restricting `MemReads` to coherent types and carrying "every stored and held type is coherent" through `SGood` and every source of types; or typing the read from the value alone. The value's record alone is not enough: a field's key projects the earlier fields to the declaration's index fields, which only the declaration says. So the declaration comes from the TYPE the read starts at.

**What.** `DataModel.readTy d r c i earlier f` (`Data.lean`, with `rawTy`, `OwnCarrier`, `shapeReadTy`) is THE function for the type a read gives a field: the type whose members are exactly the values that fit the field's shape (`fits_readTy`, `readTy_fit`), a tree field at its own record, and none at a junk leaf (a data code over the model's own carrier). `Places.Sem.stepField dm T c i s` takes the declaration from `T`'s data code and the record from the value, and fails where `T` is no data code or is of another declaration than the value. `contentTyS` and `dataTyS` are deleted. `ReadMember.follow_good`: every read of a value good at a type gives a value good at the type the read gives it, with NO hypothesis; applied to D98's value (`fresh_read`, `fresh_member`) and to `[3, 4] : List(Nat)` (`list_tail_read`). `follow_below_member` and the by-name read's coherence lemmas are deleted. `hwt_of_fit` lost its coherence conditions (D97).

**What survives.** `MemReads`' partly moved form is false under the typed read too: for `C(n : Nat, v : X(n))` at a coherent record, declared in `Γ`, `C(⊥, 7)` lies below the member `C(3, 7)` and its `v` reads `7` at `err`, the type keyed by the moved index (`ReadProto.not_memReads_dep`, for every data model). That is not a fault of how a read is typed; it belongs to D100, which makes whole reads strict and deletes `ReadOk` and `MemReads`.

**A statement made false.** `Steps/PlaceTyping.stepField_dat` (a field of a constructor value is read at some type, for an ARBITRARY `T`) is false under route (c) and kept with `sorry` to keep the file building. Through it `joinDropOne_noop`, `dropOne_step_asm` and `joinDrop_step` are open. The restatement: `T` is a data code of the value's own declaration and the field's read type is defined; `step_mean` carries the same. `DepWithin.readV` became an untyped walk of the value, and `follow_fst_indep` (the value read does not read the type) is false now and deleted. `Steps/Slice/UpdAt.updAt_append_none` is re-proved one step at a time.

### D100. Open dependent values: decided in the checker, open membership, and the unit of work (dep lane then model lane, 2026-11-21; the unit is DONE, D105)

**Found by the attack** (`Dep/Records.lean` on `spine-dep`, kernel-checked). The model typed a leaf held in a dependent field by the declared type at the CURRENT index: at every data model, the type the model reads a leaf at ignores the leaf (`leaf_read_type_blind`), while the checker read `5` at `Nat` and `()` at `Unit` under one index. And the model had no counterpart of the checker's invalidation: after an index was rewritten it kept a proof of a proposition the write had made false (`write_keeps_proof`). The same attack found an accepted proof of `False` in the checker (closed by main's D110), and the red team another in the same area (closed by main's D112); entry D102 below.

**Decided in the checker, not the model** (route B; main's D112). A leaf's meaning (`.el0 A a`: a number, a unit, a function) is tagged only by its Lean carrier, which does not determine its code, so the model cannot type a leaf by what it holds; only a tree describes itself. Making the model follow would need a new form of meaning for functions at three levels. So a dependent field is a TREE field (a data inductive at every instance) or a LEAF field; a leaf field is invalidated by a write or borrow through an index its type mentions, as a proof field is, is checked when assigned, and is never typed by content; [Entry] invalidates for every `&` capture on both paths. The uniform variant (trees invalidated too) changed at least 22 verdicts of the suite and was refused.

**Model rulings** (the orchestrator): whole reads and whole borrows are strict (fail unless the value is a closed member), which reverses D94's "reads are not strict" for whole reads; a field read gives an open member; where the checker treats lent parts as unknowns the strict test holds at every typed future; ends stay non-strict.

**Open membership** (`Dep/OMem.lean` on `spine-dep`, proved there): `OMem dm Γ s T` is `moved`; or a partly moved member; or a tree at `T = dm.tyR d r` each of whose fields is `moved`, fits its declared shape at a completion of the earlier fields' moved parts, is a tree open at its own record of the declaration the parent's record names, or is a tree of a declaration found by name that a strong update stored. It replaces `pmem`'s `OpenIn`, which admitted any value in an open field (`Records.open_admits_any`), and the "below a whole member" form, which has no instance once an index is rewritten to a value at which a field's type is empty (`below_no_member`). Proved: `omem_of_pmem_member`, `closed_iff_mem`, `field_cases`. The completions are needed: with the current fields only, "a partly moved member is an open member" is false at D99's `C(⊥, 7)`.

**Two guesses.** (G1) a read along any path from an open member gives an open member of the type the read gives it. (G2) every state the checker reaches has an open member of its stored type at every binding; tested on probes, not on recorded states.

**The unit, all of it the model lane's** (all five steps are built and on `spine`; D104 was met on the way and D105 says what was built): (1) the typed read: DONE (D99); (2) open membership in `Data.lean`, in `SGood`'s `bind`, `holder` and `TypedF`; (3) the invalidation mirrored in `Run.lean` (`Decisions.invalidated`, aligned with `Machine.openWrite`; applied after `assignAt`, after `borrowPlace`, and at the entry for a capture by borrow); (4) strict whole reads and borrows, and strict tests at lent parts over typed futures; (5) the lemma with no guesses, `ReadOk` and `MemReads` deleted, its context instantiated at a concrete model, and applied to a run that writes an index and to one that reads a field of an open value. Then the frame lemmas of D95.

### D101. Conversion and `Eq` as relations, proved in two halves (the rules trial, 2026-11-20 to 21; branch `rules-trial` ea3fbb37, not merged)

**What the trial tested** (docs/35, theme 1): whether stating what the checker computes as a relation splits an area into (A) each rule is sound in the model, with no machine state in the statement, and (B) a successful run yields a derivation, with no model in the statement.

**Three of the area's statements were false** (`Rules/Counter.lean`, `EmptyDomain.lean`). `conv_sound_stmt`: `Eq(Nat, And(True, Nat), Nat)` and `Eq(Nat, Nat, Nat)` are convertible, both members of `Prop`, and mean different propositions; a typing gap. `mkEqM_step_stmt`: `Eq(Nat, ⊥, σ₀)` computes to `False` while the assignment may send an unreferenced `σ₀` to `moved`; it lacked a hypothesis on the sides, which the caller could not supply, since no statement concluded that an evaluated value is a member (D81). `convPi_sound_stmt`: a comparison kept the η-record its private copy made, naming an unknown of type `Empty`, after which no assignment is typed; a fault of the checker's bookkeeping, fixed as main's D103.

**Proved** (standard axioms; rebuilt on the branch): (B) for all five mutually recursive comparison functions (`conv_refine'`, about 470 lines, no model in scope) and for `mkEqM` (`mkEqM_refine'`); (A) for conversion on hereditarily well-typed values (`convV_alike`, with `beq_alike` and the pinning lemma `meanV_ind_pinned`) and for all six rules of `Eq` (`eqTo_alike`); composed, `conv_sound_T`, `conv_sound_T_fo`, `mkEqM_sound_T`. Under named hypotheses: `PremSoundT` (the function rules' premises), `UnitOk`, `CodeOk` (code; outright for first-order values), `LibOkT` (`And`, `True`, `False`), `FieldTysSound`, `FieldTyIdx` (now by construction, D92), `SideMem`. They are stated against the model as it was BEFORE D97; their typing hypothesis is the trial's own `HWT`, which D97's `hwt_of_fit` now derives. Re-basing is owed.

**Decisions are not invariant under conversion** (`Rules/DecProbe.lean`). `F (n : Nat) : Unit := ()` and `H (n : Nat) : TF(Z) := ()` are convertible; `F`'s declared result reads `unit` and `H`'s `other`, so the checker accepts `let x = F(0); Mk(x, x)` and rejects it with `H`. Intended by the rules (main's D80). For the proof: the congruence for code cannot be "decisions agree on terms that differ by convertible values". It is benign on an invariant swept on every recorded state (`Rules/ShapeSweep.lean`: 76,568 states, 625,140 sealed programs, 68,500 closures): every sealed program has [Close]'s shape, and closure code holds values only as an inlined proof, a stuck block's binder types, or a `J` block's statements; the one decision that can differ there is η at `Unit`.

**What is left of the area:** the function rules' premises (the simulation's); the comparison of code, a generalisation of the substitution induction owed for a stuck call's text; `LibOkT` (a short argument from faithfulness, not written) and `UnitOk`.

**The verdict** (docs/35; accepted). Use relations and the two-half split where the checker DECIDES A JUDGEMENT BY COMPUTING. Where a check is "this value has that type", the fundamental lemma and the soundness of comparison at types suffice. Keep the machine-only part of every area free of the model. The trial's larger finding was not about bookkeeping: the checker's comparison is untyped and the model typed, and no statement said an evaluated value is a member of its type. That is what led to D94 and D97.

### D102. Main's checker decisions since its D101, and what each asks here

This branch holds main's checker up to its D101. Each row is a decision of main (`ochr/core/DECISIONS.md` there) and what the spine owes when main is merged.

| Main's | What changed in the checker | What it asks of the model and the statements |
|---|---|---|
| D102 | the join's drop decides "do nothing" by the copy decision and the place's existence, never by a `⋆` found there | nothing new: the reference's `dropAt` already tests no meaning (D93); `dropAtJoin`'s held machine step (D88) can be generated |
| D103 | a comparison keeps nothing but the counters of names (`Env.restoreNames`) | the comparison steps conclude that the state is as it was but for names (the trial's `Quiet`); `convPi_sound_stmt`'s counterexample goes; the held comparison steps (D88) can be generated; `EqJAsm` moves to `restoreKeep_grows` |
| D104 | every temporary has a stored type (`Frame.tempTys`), D81's design | `Good` and `Rel` speak of typed temporaries directly: the prototype `GoodT`/`TempTys` becomes the definition; `posTyM_setAtE`'s temporary case; `TypedFuture` types a label held in flight |
| D105 | a constructor's parameter is not inferred where it stands only at positions of a higher universe | D91's residual closes; `unifyTyS` need follow nothing further |
| D106 | no access ends a borrow a value in flight holds (one check, in `endTopLoan`) | a clause for the steps that access with a value in flight (the join's drop, a `let`'s drop, a pop, a later argument): they end no label a temporary holds; `Regress/JoinPrefixBorrow`'s expected messages change |
| D107 | positivity: nestable parameters are a greatest fixed point, own occurrences included | D85 is closed; piece (4) of D92's decomposition is stated against this rule; `Exists/Positivity.lean`'s battery flips to rejected |
| D108 | a generalisation record is looked up exactly (`Value.exact`) | the slice's `neutralKey` leaf and the same gap in `canonNeutral_step` and `substEnv_neutral_step` close: the record found is the program asked |
| D109 | `blockCaptures`' loop is bounded by the fuel | `step_blockCaptures` and agreement across fuels for the block's captures become provable (D88) |
| D110 | a strong update stores unchecked only first-order data (`Decide.strongStorable`) | open membership's "a tree a strong update stored" case (D100) holds only trees; closed a proof of `False` |
| D112, amended | tree and leaf fields; a leaf is invalidated with its index; [Entry] invalidates on both paths; typing reads through a mark | D100's whole unit; `Decisions` gains `invalidated`; closed a proof of `False`; the amendment repaired a regression of the first landing (ordinary reads of a field lent to a call's returned borrow were rejected) |

Queued on main, not landed: an internal assertion that no borrow is left below the top of a value without its end (to be D111), and the two fixes of `Id` (entry D103 below; to be main's D113 and D114). (Since landed: main's D111, merged here at f8ebe797, D108; and main's D113 and D114, being merged. To come: main's D115, no value the checker holds lives outside the state while anything runs, and a fix for `clone` of a proof.)

**Three accepted proofs of `False`** were closed by D107, D110 and D112 (docs/18's risk section has the programs). So `Ochr.Consistency.consistency` was false of the checker on 20 and 21 November, and is false of the checker this branch holds. Each was found by an attack, not by the suite: building the model for every declaration (D92), stating what an open value means (D100), and a red team.

### D103. `Id`: two faults of the checker, found by a red team; what the red team did not find (2026-11-21; recorded for the `Id` statements)

**Found** (a read-only agent whose one job was to make the checker accept a wrong thing).
- `Id` is provable by `refl` between two sides that differ, when a side writes through a borrow it re-pointed: [Obs-ref] writes its fresh unknown through the re-pointed borrow BEFORE reading what the side left there, so the side's own write is hidden. Seven variants. Not a route to `False` by a reading of the code: an `Id` is only ever used through the conjunction of equations it computes to, and no rule replaces one side by the other.
- [Id-typestate]'s no-ending clause is judged on the run that checks the program, so it decides differently on different paths: an accepted theorem has a rejected closed instance.

**For this branch.** The model's `Id` (D74) is the intended one, so `idType_step`'s statements are false of the checker until main's two fixes land; then `IdAsm` and `Regress/IdWrite` are re-checked against them.

**Not found**, about 135 targeted programs with rejected controls: `Id` in unusual frames, proofs that are never run, large elimination, the recursion check, sorts and universes, splits and stuck matches, calls that do not compute, the computation of `Eq`; two live borrows of one place, use after move, and a declared reading that says copy for a resource. Two of its observations bear on the model: a borrow type cannot be written where a type is expected, so no type parameter is instantiated at a borrow and data holds none ((WF8) rests on that); and no program of the suite reads a dependent field while it is lent to a call's returned borrow, which is why the suite saw neither the third proof of `False` nor the regression of its fix. In bulk, about 5,900 closed calls each computed a value of its declared type, and 409 definitions kept their verdict when each borrow was passed through an identity call; those counts are over the blocks its generators reached, and a re-run over the blocks a generator fault skipped is pending.

### D104. `fund_all` is false at a write to a field path; the ruling: a test at every typed future, discharged by a fact about the checker's states (the model lane's finding and the orchestrator's ruling, 2026-11-21; kernel-checked at `spine-model-dep` b0284e04; the repair BUILT on `spine-model`, below)

**Where.** On the model lane's working branch `spine-model-dep` (c0539e02 and after; not merged, and not building outside `Strict/`), D100's steps 2 to 4 are in progress: `OMem` is in `Data.lean` and `pmem dm Γ s T := OMem dm Γ T s`; `ReadOk` and `MemReads` are deleted, so `FundCtx` is its globals and sorts clauses and has instances; `readPlace` is strict; `.assign` and `.borrow` at a field path invalidate and then test `lentOk` (the position holds an open member of its type at every typed future). This is the first form of `fund_all` whose context has an instance.

**Found** (`Strict/FieldWriteCE.lean`, standard axioms, for every data model; rebuilt and axiom-checked by the orchestrator). `setContent pos steps v` writes, at EVERY future `φ'`, the path update `Sem.updAt … steps` of the old value at `φ'`, and `err` where that update fails (`getD .err`); the run checks only that the update succeeds at the state's typed future (`SState.typedFuture`: each live label its record). A position whose value READS a live label at a strict prefix of `steps` has, at the typed futures that give the label another open member, a value the path does not enter, so the write leaves `err` there. `SGood` says nothing that excludes such a position: it does not tie a position's reads of live labels to the sites [Access] ends (`SState.lent`), so the access before the write need not end the label.
- The state `s₀`: one frame; `x₀ : Nat` reading label `0` whole (`fun ψ => ψ 0`: a lender with no site recorded); `x₁ : Nat` holding label `0`, the number `1`; `heldTy 0 = Nat`, `heldMem 0 = 1`. It is good (`s₀_good`).
- Moving out `x₀.0`, the predecessor (`setContent … [.0] ⊥`), succeeds: at the typed future `x₀` reads `1`, and becomes `S(⊥)`. At the typed future giving label `0` the number `0`, `x₀` then reads `err`, an open member of nothing (`s₁_bad`).
- So `OpsPlace.setContent_field_moved_sgood`, one of D95's three guesses, is false as stated (`not_setContent_field_moved_sgood`), and so is the lemma: `not_fund_all`, at a context whose `FundCtx` holds (`fundCtx_Kc`), the term `x₀.0`, from `s₀`.

**Not a matter of dependent types.** Every write of `⊥` at a field path has this form: a read's move (`setContent`), the join's drop (`dropAt`, its own write of the same shape), and D100's invalidation; `borrowPlace`'s write at the lender has it too. The `sorry`s of the three field-path guesses had been hiding it since D95. On `spine` (01515cbe) the same `setContent` and the same guess stand; the lemma there is vacuous first (D98, D99), which is why it took the deletion of `MemReads` to see this.

**Considered** (the lane's three options). (a) `getD .moved`: write `moved`, not `err`, where the update fails. (b) A strict test that the write is defined at every typed future. (c) A clause of `SGood` tying a position's reads of live labels to the lent sites.

**Ruled: (b).** In the strict run a write at a field path succeeds only if the position holds an open member of its type at every typed future afterwards. For an assignment or a borrow of a field that is the working branch's `lentOk`. For a write of `⊥` it reduces to: the path update is defined at every typed future; that the result is then an open member is a lemma of (I), not a test.
- *Why not (a).* It is cheap and the junk state survives: refill the field and read the variable whole, and it is a member at one future and `moved` at another. (A working commit on the lane, 3ba25c37, holds (a) with a state-wide `lentOk`; it is from before the ruling and is replaced by it.)
- *Why not (c).* It is true only of the states the checker reaches, and would be a sizeable invariant carried through every operation of the run, in a lemma whose point is to have no checker in it.

**What discharges the test: an obligation of (II), named.** After the checker's access of a write at `x.p`, `x`'s stored value has no live loan and no mark along `p` (the access has ended every borrow lent on the path); so its meaning has the path at every typed future. It is a fact about the checker's states, to be a clause or a lemma of the simulation, with `Strict/FieldWriteCE.lean` as its regress file. It is read off the checker so far, and is being measured on every `setPlace` the checker makes, at every call depth.

**Also decided for the same unit.** The state between a borrow of a field and its test is not good (the label exists, the invalidation has not run). So the borrow clause's case of (I) is stated for the composite; or the invalidation runs before the label is created, if the checker's order allows. The model lane is choosing after reading `Machine.lean`. Related, from the lane's notes: the invalidation's ends must leave the written position's own label alone, or its lenders inherit content nothing checked.

**The rule of D98, a second time.** The fault was found by the first concrete run applied to the lemma once its context had an instance. Proofs of every clause had not found it, because the clause that fails sat under a `sorry`. A `sorry` under a lemma reported as "every clause proved" is to be read as a place the lemma may be false.

**Built** (model lane, 2026-11-21, `spine-model`; `fund_all` with no `sorry`, `#print axioms` the three standard axioms). A write of `⊥` at a field path is made only where the path update is defined at every typed future: `setContent` fails otherwise (the read's move), `dropAt` and D100's `invalidateOne` leave the state; that the result is an open member is the model lemma `Strict/WriteMoved.omem_updAt_moved`, a corollary of `omem_movedOf` (open membership is closed under moving parts out). An assignment or a borrow at a field path ends, after the invalidation, with `lentOk` = `StateOk` (every position an open member at every typed future), which also covers `borrowPlace`'s lender write (`getD .err`: an `err` is no open member); a write or a borrow at `[]` or `[*]` is whole and not tested (testing `[*]` rejected a run the checker accepts, `Regress/CallInPlace`'s `*x := S(*x)` in a hand-built frame). The middle of the borrow and assignment cases is `Strict/Struct.SStruct` (`SGood`'s clauses that read no value), which the invalidation keeps and `StateOk` completes: the composite, with the checker's order (borrow, then `openWrite`). `Strict/FieldWriteCE` is now the regress record: the run is stuck at `s₀` (`runT_p₀`). `Strict/Witness` applies the lemma to four runs that write at a field path, among them a borrow of an index field whose dependent sibling the invalidation moves out. Also built: `readTy` types a tree of another declaration than its field's by its own declaration found by name (`ownTy`; the checker's `depFieldType` by content). Two `sorry`s were added under `Steps/` for the slice lane, each the step from "at the typed future" to "at every typed future" (`DropOneAsm.dropOne_exists`; `MovedPlace.joinDropOne_noop`, where it needs the run's typed future to be a typed future, records at `heldTy` vs the relation's `holderTy`); the first is this entry's obligation of (II).

### D105. (I) is proved: the repair as built (model lane, 2026-11-21; on `spine` at 2a7982f7; `fund_all` and everything under `Meaning/Strict/` with no `sorry`)

**What is proved.** `Strict.fund_all`, for every clause of `runT`: from an `SGood` state, a successful strict run of a term whose embedded values are good returns a good result (a whole member of its type, `moved`, or a value with no data at `err`), its label recorded and live or unread, and leaves an `SGood` state. `FundCtx` has two fields, `globals` (every global a good result with no label) and `sorts` (no declaration of sort 2), and an instance at every data model (`Witness.fundCtx_Kw`). Checked at 2a7982f7 by `#print axioms`: `fund_all`, `setContent_field_moved_sgood`, `borrowPlace_field_sgood`, `setContent_field_sgood`, `omem_movedOf`, `omem_updAt_moved`, `fundCtx_Kw`, `w_move`, `w_refill`, `w_joinDrop`, `w_borrowIndex`, `FieldWriteCE.runT_p₀`, `hwt_of_run`, and the operation lemmas, all on the three standard axioms.

**How it was built** (D100's unit, steps 2 to 5, with D104's ruling).
- *Open membership is the model's membership at a position* (`Data.lean`): `OMem dm Γ T s` is `moved`; a member; a partly moved number; or a constructor value of `T`'s declaration and record whose fields are each `moved`, `fits` (below a whole value fitting the shape at a completion of the earlier fields) or `at` (an open member of `readTy` at a completion). `pmem dm Γ s T := OMem dm Γ T s`: its first argument is now the MODEL, not a `DataRead`. `OpenIn` is deleted. A partly moved member is open (`omem_of_below`); closed is member (`closed_iff_mem`); moving parts out keeps it (`WriteMoved.omem_movedOf`, mutual with the fields; a `⊥` write is such a move, `updAt_moved_props`).
- *Reads are strict* (`readPlace` tests `ReadGood`: the value read is a member of the type the read gives it, or `⋆`/a type at `err`), so `ReadOk`, `MemReads` and `FundCtx.reads` are DELETED, which is what made the context inhabitable (D98).
- *The write rule* (D104's ruling (b)). A write of `⊥` at a field path is made only where the path update is defined at EVERY typed future: `setContent` fails otherwise (a read's move); `dropAt` (the join's drop) and `invalidateOne` leave the state. That the result is an open member is `omem_updAt_moved`. An assignment or a borrow at a field path is followed by the invalidation and then by `lentOk`, which is `StateOk`: every binding and every holder an open member at every typed future. The test is state-wide, not of the written position only, because the invalidation's ends pass contents between positions. It also covers `borrowPlace`'s lender write (`getD .err`: an `err` is no open member). No `getD .moved` remains.
- *`lentOk` does not test a write or a borrow at `[]` or `[*]`* (`steps = [] ∨ steps = [.deref] ∨ StateOk`). Testing `[*]` rejected a run the checker accepts (`Regress/CallInPlace`: `*x := S(*x)` in a hand-built frame whose `self` binding is `err` at a Π-type), and the lemma never used the test there: the root and `*` cases are proved from the clause's own membership test.
- *The middle of the borrow and assignment cases is `Strict/Struct.SStruct`*: `SGood`'s clauses that read no value (`held`, `holderTy`, `acyc`, `fresh`). The state between the write and the test need not be good (a later dependent field may not fit), but it is `SStruct`; a borrow at any path leaves it (`sstruct_borrow_park`), a write at a path and [Access] keep it, so the invalidation does (`SStruct.invalidate`); and `sgood_of_stateOk` closes. This is D104's "the clause is stated for the composite", with the checker's order kept (the borrow, then `openWrite`).
- *The invalidation* (main's D112, `Machine.openWrite`): `Decisions.invalidated`; `invalidateOne` accesses each place and writes `⊥` where the write is defined; applied after the `.assign` write, after `borrowPlace`, and in `entryCap` for a `&` capture.
- *`readTy` takes the program's declarations and has a `byContent` case* (5d3e2556): `readTy Γ d r c i earlier f`; a tree in a tree-shaped field that is of ANOTHER declaration than the shape names (what a strong update stored before an index write changed the field's declared type) is typed by its own declaration, found by name in `Γ`, at its own record (`ownTy`), as the checker's `depFieldType` types an open field by its content. Before, the model read it at the shape's declaration at the tree's record, garbage for a foreign tree, and the strict test would have failed where the checker accepts. A fitting tree has the shape's name and record (`readTy_fit`), so a member never reaches the lookup, and D97's payoff lemma stays lookup-free. `Places.Sem.stepField` takes `Γ`.
- *The witness* (`Strict/Witness.lean`): `w_move` (`x.0` moved out of `x = 1`); `w_refill` (`x.0 := c` from the state `w_move` left: the strict assignment test, the invalidation and `StateOk` pass); `w_joinDrop` (`match y { Z => c, S(_) => x.0 }` at `y = 0`: the join drops `x.0`); `w_borrowIndex` (`&x.n` of `x = C(3, 7)` in `C(n : Nat, v : X(n))`: the invalidation moves out the dependent sibling, and `StateOk` passes at every typed future). `Strict/FieldWriteCE` is the regress record: on the good state where the old run moved out `x₀.0` and left a bad state, the run now fails (`runT_p₀`).

**What (II) owes for it** (`Meaning/OPEN.md`, table (II)): after the access of a write at `x.p`, `x` reads no live label along `p` (measured on main: no live loan or mark on the path of any of 44,141 writes per mode, at every call depth, typed and untyped, inside and outside private copies; but see D106); every reached state has open members at its positions, and `Decisions.invalidated` agrees with `openWrite`; every tree in a tree-shaped dependent field is of a declaration of the program; the soundness of [Repack] at a strict read (a whole open member that passes [Repack] is a member).

**Open on the model's side.** One case of `readTy`: a field that is a tree field statically, whose declared type computes to a LEAF at the current index, holding a stale tree; the model reads it at the leaf, the checker by content. It needs the checker's static leaf decision (`Decide.fieldIsLeaf`) in the model. And the frame lemmas: `callFrame`, the body's run, `popCallFrame` and `SState.endInto` for the call in place, and `bodyAt`'s frame.

**Two `sorry`s were added under `Steps/`** with the write rule, each the step from "at the run's typed future" to "at every typed future": `DropOneAsm.dropOne_exists` and `MovedPlace.joinDropOne_noop` (where the run's typed future must be a `TypedFut`: the records at `heldTy` against `Rel.records`' `holderTy`). They were labelled the simulation's obligation. D106 says why the simulation cannot discharge them as things stand.

### D106. The seam between (I) and (II): the lemma's typed futures are wider than the relation's (the seventh lead's finding, 2026-11-21; kernel-checked; RULED (A) and BUILT, below; built on `spine-model-dep` and on `spine` since 14853a79)

**Found.** (I) tests a write at a field path (`setContent`, `dropAt`, `invalidateOne`, `lentOk`) at every `TypedFut`, where each live label gets an OPEN member of its record's type, `moved` and partly moved values included. `Rel` compares the checker's state with the reference's only at futures giving each live label a WHOLE member, and must: off members the reference reads a returned borrow's lent place through the backward function and the checker reads the future's value raw (D66). Off those futures `Rel` says nothing of the reference state. So no statement taking `Rel ∧ SGood` proves a write at a field path.

**The smallest case.** The checker's frame: `z ↦ loan₀ : Nat`, `y ↦ borrow₀ 0 : &Nat`, `x ↦ S(Z) : Nat`; nothing is lent at `x`; the term `x.0 := Z`, which the checker accepts (it is the state the checker reaches at that point of an accepted definition). The reference state has the same skeleton, holders and lending; its `x` means `fun ψ => if ψ 0 is a number then 1 else 0`; `heldTy 0 = Nat`, `heldMem 0 = 0`. `Rel` holds: at futures giving label `0` a number, `x` means `1` on both sides. `SGood` holds: `0` and `1` are members. `⊥` is an open member of `Nat`, so `ψ 0 = ⊥` is a typed future, where `x` is `0`; the strict `.assign`'s `setContent` tests the path `[.0]` there, and `0` has no predecessor. The strict run fails; the checker's succeeds.

**Kernel records** (standard axioms; each rebuilt on its branch; `Strict/OpenFutureCE` is on `spine` since 14853a79, `Regress/FieldWriteGap` only on `spine-lead7`). `Regress/FieldWriteGap.fieldWrite_gap` at `spine-lead7` f902db84: at every data model and assignment, `Rel` (identity renaming) and `SGood` and `FundCtx`, and `runT` of the write is `none` in or out of tail position at any hint. The checker's own step is run by `#guard`, since its [Access] goes through well-founded recursion the kernel does not unfold. `Strict/OpenFutureCE` at `spine-model-dep` e2d28065: the model's side alone (`s₀_good`, `x_at_numbers`, `run_stuck`).

**What it touches.** Every `⊥` written at a field path, and `lentOk`. D105's two `sorry`s under `Steps/` are this very step. The measured fact (no live loan or mark on the path of any write the checker makes) is true and is not enough: it gives the reference's path at the futures `Rel` compares, and the test asks it at more.

**Proposal (A): one notion of typed future, at members.** In `TypedF` and `TypedFut` (so in `SGood`, `setContent`, `dropAt`, `lentOk`), each live label a MEMBER of its record's type, as `Rel` already has it. Cost to (I): for the state after an end to be good the holder's content must be a member at every typed future, so ends become a strict point. (II) discharges that from [End]'s whole check (main's D98: no end over moved-out content, anywhere) and its [Repack] (main's D64); `EndWhole`, today a named fact the lemma does not use, becomes a test. D104's obligation then holds as worded: after the access the checker's `x` has the syntactic path `p` with no live loan or mark on it, so its meaning, which is the reference's at every typed future, has `p`.

**Alternative (B'): keep (I), widen `Rel`.** Add to `Rel` a path-by-path comparison at open futures, masked at the fills of returned borrows. Heavier, and every step would carry it.

**Status: under evaluation, NOT ruled.** The orchestrator leans to (A), on what a future means: a label's value in a future is its borrow's content when the borrow ends, and that is never partly moved; a partly moved CURRENT content is state, not future. But D94's ruling 3 went the other way (partly moved futures; a first ruling for strict ends was reversed on the model lane's evidence), so that evidence is being re-examined first: the model lane is asked what forced partly moved futures, and to try (A) on a scratch branch.

**The other seam closes with no new clause** (futures off the live labels, the second risk the sixth lead named under M2): `Rel`, `SGood` and `TypedFut` all fix the future to `φ` off the reference's live labels; the checker's `Membership`, which is free off the live labels, is no longer an invariant under D107's form; no step reads the checker's state except through `Rel`, and inert loans read `φ` on both sides; at the top level the result is read at the run's future, which the assembly chooses, and a closed constant has no live label there.

**Ruled: (A)** (the orchestrator, 2026-11-21, on the model lane's evidence). Why partly moved futures had been admitted: the END alone. `endAt_sgood` makes the holder's current content the ended label's value in its lenders' future, and the holder clause knows that content only as an OPEN member (it may have been moved out through the borrow, D53 (h)). D94's `moved` in `callOfL`'s undecodable branch was a consequence, not a second cause: with partly moved futures a returned label could hold a value that does not decode. The run's own typed future was never a reason (it gives each live label its record, a member). The probe (`probe-whole-futures` 7320ee95: typed futures at members, nothing else changed) broke `endAt_sgood` alone for real; two `sorry`s in the call lemmas were bookkeeping (the undecodable branch, unreachable at whole futures); everything else built after `.mem` wrappers.

**Built** (model lane, on `spine-model-dep`, merged into `spine` at 14853a79; `fund_all` and every witness on the three standard axioms, checked again at 0e728883). `TypedF`/`TypedFut` give each live label a whole MEMBER of its recorded type. The STRICT END: a borrow may end only where its holder's content is a member of its recorded type at every typed future (`Run.EndOk`; `Strict.EndOkF` is the same at the invariant's level, the former `EndWhole`); the run tests it before every end it makes, and fails otherwise, by one Prop test per end site, the state functions unchanged (`SM.guarded`, `SM.guardMod`, `optCheck`). The sites, each an obligation of (II) discharged by the checker's rule that no borrow ends over moved-out content (main's D98) and its [Repack]:
- the reads' access (`.place`, a call's head, `clone`/`peek`/`inplace`): `ContentOk`;
- `.borrow`'s access: `ContentOk`; D112's invalidation after it and after `.assign`: `InvalidateOk`;
- `.assign`'s access and the old borrow's end at a root: `AssignOk`;
- `.seq`'s discard of a borrow: `DiscardOk`;
- `.letIn`'s drop, the held borrow's end or the lenders' access: `EndOk`/`LetLendersOk`;
- a match's access for the tag: `TagOk`; [Entry] (the lent prefixes' and the captures' accesses, the invalidation for a capture by `&`): `MatchEntryOk`; the join's drop (lent prefixes, a root's end, the access): `JoinDropOk`; `J`'s entry and join: the same two;
- `.fix`'s captures' accesses: `FoldOk (capStep K) (CapOk K φ)`; a closure body's frame pop: `EndsOk`.
Not tested: the ends inside `Id`'s observation (in the MEANING of the type, on a copy after hypothetical writes; not a step of the run's state, and the lemma does not need it). Outside `runT`: the in-place call's frame pop (`CallRef`, the frame lemmas still to write).
- The cost to (I): `endAt_sgood` takes the test (its one real change); `access_sgood`, the drops and the let's end inherit it; every clause lemma takes its test from the guard. In the call lemmas the undecodable branch is unreachable at a typed future (`Lvl.MemEnc`, `Lvl.dec_of_mem`: a member of a code's type decodes), so `moved` there no longer matters to (I); it is left as it is (any value works at futures nobody types; a meaning cannot fail pointwise). Core lemmas repaired, with a congruence lemma per test (`RunCongr`).
- Witnesses (`Strict/Witness`): the four field-path runs, plus a borrow ended over content moved out through it, which fails (`run_endBad`; the checker rejects the program by D98), and the same after a refill, which runs and leaves a good state (`w_endOk`). Records: `Strict/OpenFutureCE` (this entry's case on the model side) DISSOLVED: label `0` holding `⊥` is no typed future, and the write is defined at every one; `Strict/FieldWriteCE` still stuck, and EXCLUDED (no site recorded, so related to no checker state).
- What (II) now owes: the end tests above; and `heldTy l = holderTy s l` for live `l` (true of every state the run produces, `SGood.holderTy`; not a clause of `Rel`), which is all D105's two `Steps` `sorry`s still need (`DropOneAsm.dropOne_exists`, `MovedPlace.joinDropOne_noop`). One new `Steps` `sorry` for the same reason: `EqJAsm.J_step_asm`, `J`'s entry and join tests.

### D107. The form of (II)'s statements (the sixth lead's M2, the seventh lead's `Pre`/`Ext`/`Out`; 2026-11-21; settled in outline, to be a new file `Steps/Form.lean`; not on `spine`)

**Decided** (the orchestrator, on the sixth lead's recommendation). Membership leaves the checker's invariant and lives on the reference state. A step concludes that the strict run succeeds. `SGood` after a step is NOT a conclusion of the step: it is `fund_all` applied to the run the step concluded succeeded (`FundAt`'s fourth conjunct). Why: membership is then proved once, by (I), and no step re-proves the membership of positions it does not touch; this is the uniform endpoint of D86.

**The form** (the seventh lead's first report; names may still move):
- `Pre K ρ st`: `TypedAssignment K.dm K.M K.Γ ρ st`; Δ's types and the goal have levels; `WF K.dm K.M K.Γ ρ st`; `CtxOk K ρ st`. No `Membership`.
- `Ext K ρ π st st' ρ' Δ' π'`: the tables grow; `ρ'` and `Δ'` agree below `st.absTy.size`; `π'` agrees below `st.nextLoan`.
- `Out K ρ φ π st st' s (x : SM Res) v T?`: there are `ρ' Δ' π' r s'` with `Ext`, `x s = some (r, s')`, `meanV K' ρ' (φ ∘ π') r.2 v = r.1`, `StrAgree` of `T?` against `r.2`, and `Pre` and `Rel` of `st'.withTemp v T?` at `K' := { K with Δ := Δ' }`.
- `eval_sim K ρ φ fuel`: for a run `Runs (eval fuel typed t hint) st bh (v, T?) st'`, from `Pre`, `Rel K ρ φ π st s`, `Strict.SGood K.dm K.Γ φ s`, `t.valsOk (Strict.ValsP K ρ φ)`, `t.absBelow st.absTy.size`, `t.hasHolePrim = false`, for an untyped run that `runT` of `t` from `s` succeeds, and `DecTab K.D st'`: `Out` of `runT K ρ φ st.tailNext (hintM K ρ hint) t`.
- `Sim K ρ φ st st' x v T?`: for every `s π` with `Pre`, `Rel` and `SGood`, `Out`.
- `sim_seqS`: from `Strict.FundAt K.dm K.Γ φ x₁`, `Sim` of `x₁`, `Sim` of `x₂ r` at every extension, and that `x₂` reads nothing the extension changed: `Sim` of `x₁ >>= x₂`.

**Three notes on it.** `SGood` between two sub-steps is `sim_seqS`'s hypothesis `FundAt`'s last conjunct; inside one step (after a `let`'s push and drop) it comes from the model's operation lemmas (`sgood_pushBindShaped`, `sgood_letDrop`), not from `fund_all` alone. The run sits at the step's STARTING `(K, ρ)`: it reads only the term's unknowns (`run_congrA`), so a composed run is a plain bind; that is why `t.absBelow` is a new hypothesis. And (I) is `Strict.fund_all` itself.

**Where the seventh lead departs from the sixth's handoff.** The bridge `membershipT_of_sgood` is needed by no step: the seam is at the strict tests (D106), not at a bridge. `RepackSound` (a whole value that is an open member and passes [Repack] is a member) cannot be parked: a strict whole read needs a closed member. The column's read is proved for declarations with no dependent constructor, where a whole open member is a member, and `RepackSound` is stated for the general read; it is not a fact about the checker alone, so it needs an owner, and under D106's (A) it serves ends too.

**Proved first, on a narrow column** (the orchestrator's instruction, before the 81 statements are restated): composition; a typed `let`; a read; an assignment at a field of a place (which waits on D106); `zero`, `succ` and `seq`; and the top-level assembly for `def C : Nat := let x = S(Z); x`; with a concrete instance of every bundle of hypotheses.

**Then the one-pass migration** (`Steps/LEAD-HANDOFF.md`, items M1 to M11, each with what it waits on): typed temporaries in the statements (`MState.withTemp v T?`; the flight deleted; "every temporary of a typed run has a type" is not a state clause, the statements conclude it of the temporary they push); holes; embedded values good; the join's guarantee as a fact about the checker (main's D106); the slice's record clauses; [Entry]'s captures out of `CtxOk`; the return path through `RepackSound` and `return_member`; calls on the checker's state after main's D115; `Id` against main's D113 and D114; `split_coverage` and `RefineOk` in the slice's forms; `stepField_dat`. And four lemmas (II) owes that (I) does not: one per equality rule (`cong`, `symm`, `trans`, `rewrite`), each "if the premises' propositions as the checker computed them are true, the checker's conclusion is true", since D94 types every erased proof at `prop True` and the clause checks only that the premises run.

**Two more statements refuted on the way** (`Regress/Suspects.lean`, standard axioms). `owners_sound_stmt`: after `let r = &x; let s = &*r`, `r` means the reborrow's final content, so it depends on label `1`, but `owners 1` is `[x]`; the converse direction misses a reborrow's holder, and the statement is to be restricted to the direction footprint coverage uses. `proof_step_stmt`: `(⋆ : Eq(Nat, 0, 1))`, an inlined captured proof, runs typed to `⋆` at `False`; the statement lacked the strict run's own precondition on embedded values.

### D108. The proof line on main's checker (its D102 to D112, with D111): what the merge forced (merge lane, 2026-11-21; f8ebe797)

**Result.** `lake build OchrMeaning` and the default `lake build` (the checker, the examples with every verdict asserted, the proof library) were green at f8ebe797. Declarations using `sorry` in the library build: 169 before the merge (at 8ae30435) and 169 after, the SAME 169 by name. No statement was weakened, deleted or commented out; no new `sorry`; no statement was found false by the checker's changes. One file of the library shrank: `WF.lean`, by a definition main now owns.

**Signatures followed, smallest change.**
- `Value.outerLoans`/`outerLoansList`: main's D111 copied the definition into `Ochr/Obs.lean`, clause for clause, to assert (WF2) at a read; the library's copy is deleted and the checker's used.
- `assignPlace` gained the assigned value's type (main's D104): `assignPlace_step_stmt` and `assign_wf_stmt` quantify over it.
- `observe`'s re-pointed bindings are triples (place, fresh unknown, its type): `observe_step_stmt`, `IdAsm.idType_split`, `fresh_realize`, `grows_observe`.
- `runBody` gained the call's type `B` (the body's result is held at it across the pop): `runBody_step_stmt`, `Call.callOf_inPlace_rel`, `CallAsm.runBody_front`/`runBody_back`, the mirror `bodyTail`; `callFn_front` names the call's type once; `callFn_step_asm` and `runBody_step_asm` re-assembled.
- `pushTemp`/`pushTempAt` gained the stored type and a site name: the mirrors `fixReturn` and `bodyTail` follow; `PosLib.runs_pushTemp` restated with the checker's effect (`Env.pushTempTyE`) and re-proved. `MState.withTemp`, through which the statements read a value in flight, still pushes the value alone: typed temporaries in the statements are the migration's (D107), not the merge's.
- `accessInside` passes the place to `endTopLoan` (main's D106): the copy of its loop in `MovedPlace` follows.
- `recordedNeutral` looks a record up through `neutralLookup` by `Value.exact` (main's D108): `runs_neutralLookup_inv` (new), `runs_recordedNeutral_inv` re-proved through it.

**The join's drop** (main's D102), re-proved. `dropAtJoin` no longer skips a place's own `⋆` by its value: only a `⋆` under a `⋆` ancestor (`underProof`) is skipped, and any other goes to the copy decision. `runs_dropAtJoin_inv` restated to the new cases and re-proved, with `runs_underProof_inv` and `eq_proof_of_beq`. Its two consumers re-proved: `DropOneAsm` (the `⋆` case still contradicts (C⋆) at the grain) and `MovedPlace`'s re-rooted drop, where a re-rooted prefix holding `⋆` is now excluded up front by `not_below_proof`.

**A proof that pins a checker run.** `ProgSanity.one_accepted` (`checkItem` of `def one : Nat := S(Z)`, by `decide`) timed out on main's checker, whose run is longer (stored types; D111's assertion at a read); `decide +kernel` checks it.

**Verdicts main changed on purpose, flipped** (each with a comment naming the decision): main's D105 (`DMProbeSolve.SH` accepted → rejected: the checker and the model now agree there); main's D106 (`Regress/JoinPrefixBorrow`'s `TK`: the message is [Access]'s, still rejected); main's D107, outside the default build (`Exists/Positivity.lean`'s four paradox blocks and `Exists/ConsistencyCE.lean`'s `Curry` now reject, the acceptance guards negated). No verdict changed that main's `DECISIONS.md` does not explain.

**Outside the default build and stale before the merge, not touched:** `Steps/BorrowCE.lean` and the eleven pinned `Regress/*_<sha>.lean` (their errors name the model's own definitions).

**Next.** A second, small merge: main's D113 and D114 (the statements about `Id` are restated against them in the migration, D107) and the test on recorded states at every call depth; then main's D115 when it lands.

### Entries D109 to D112 (2026-11-21, late morning): the second merge of main and the model's `Id`, the existence statement, the facts about the checker other lanes need, the lead's column

Written from the commits each entry names and the lanes' notes. What is called proved was checked by `#print axioms`: on `spine` at 0e728883 for D109 and D110, on `spine-lead7` at 4a0f10d7 for D112, each in a clean detached checkout. The checker's decisions are cited as "main's D-number".

### D109. The second merge of main; the model's `Id` is behind the checker's since main's D113 (merge lane, 2026-11-21; 1ebf10ff, on `spine` since 1e226c6a)

**The merge.** Main's D113 and D114 and the test on recorded states at every call depth, merged into this line (b6056546; the library made green at 1ebf10ff). `lake build` and `lake build OchrMeaning sweep` green; `lake exe sweep --probes`: every real clause holds, both planted clauses reported. Declarations using `sorry`: 168 before, 169 after, the one more being `idType_step_asm` (below). No statement was weakened or deleted. What it forced:
- Main's D113 ([Obs-ref]: a re-pointed binding's content is moved out of the borrow as one more component of the observation, the fresh value put in its place, and `Id` compares that component). `observe_step_stmt` restated: the run returns a third component, one entry per re-pointed binding, each meaning at its binding's content type the reference's counterpart, `refTargets`/`refTargetsOf` (new, beside `refObs` in `Steps/Statements.lean`: after the side's run each re-pointed binding's content is held as a temporary and the written value put in its place, every live borrow ends, and the held contents are read, which is what main's D113 `observe` does). `IdAsm.idType_split` and `cells_of_observe` restated likewise. Each is marked "RESTATED BY D113" in its docstring; none is proved.
- Main's D114 (a side of `Id` may end a borrow it consumes, on every path): `RunLib.endBorrow_succ` follows the checker's new body.

**Found.** `idType_step_stmt` is false since main's D113. The model's `Id` (`run`'s `.id` clause, over `Run.observation`) compares the result and the positions only; `observation` puts the written value in each re-pointed binding's place and never reads the content it replaced, which is the blind spot the checker had before main's D113. At main's `Hide` (`let x = 0; let w = 0; let y = &w; Id(Unit, y := &x, (y := &x; *y := 5))`, `Equality.ObsRef`) the model's `Id` is true and the checker's conjunction holds `Eq(Nat, 0, 5)`, so "the model's `Id` true gives the conjunction true" fails. In `idType_step_asm` the step `key` (the conjunction holds exactly when the two reference observations are equal) is false for the same reason; its proof is now `sorry`, the old proof at 3347196b, and the rest of the assembly is unchanged. Found by reading the clauses against `Hide`; no kernel-checked record of it yet.

**Ruled: the model follows the checker** (docs/18 on main). `observation` reads the moved-out contents as `refTargetsOf` does, and the `.id` clause compares them; with concrete runs (`Hide`, whose `Id` the model must make false; `Id(Unit, y := &x, y := &x)` and the twin that writes on both sides, true); and a check against main's D114 (a side that ends a borrow it consumes on every path: does the strict run succeed there, and is ruling (A)'s strict end met at the end the side makes?). The ends inside `observation` are not tested by (A), since they are in the meaning of a type, on a copy; whether reading the moved-out content makes them matter is part of the item. Queued for the model lane (`Strict/HANDOFF.md`, "Next" 2); not built. Footprint coverage and the type-state's clauses have not been tried against main's fixed rule.

**Replaces** D103's "the model's `Id` (D74) is the intended one": it was, against the checker before main's D113.

### D110. The existence statement was false for every program with a declaration; the model repaired; the first instance (existence lane, 2026-11-21; 1765a998, bc86f861, 71921e55, d4b7a5ee, 78946a91, 0e728883; on `spine` since 0e728883)

**Found** (`DataModel/Exists/RecordsCE.lean`, kernel-checked at 1765a998 on the standard axioms; found by attacking the statement before any of its pieces was proved). `exists_dataModel` asks `DataFaithful` at every prefix of the program, which asks `CtxFaithful`, which asks `RecordsFaithful`; and `RecordsFaithful` had no instance as soon as the context declares one inductive (`recordsFaithful_false`, `ctxFaithful_false`, `dataFaithful_false`). Every accepted program declares the library's `Pair`, `False`, `True` and `And`, so the statement was false for every program the assembly applies it to. The cause: `Code0.level` gave every data code level 1, so the raw data code of a `Prop`-sorted declaration over the model's own carrier was a member of `Type₀`, although it is the meaning of no type the checker has (such a declaration means, as a type, only its proposition: `DataModel.tyR` gives `prop (Nonempty car)`). A type parameter ranges over the universe, so faithfulness had to hold at that code, and two clauses collide there: `RecordsFaithful.types`, at the parameter list holding it (which `ParamsTyped` admitted, any parameter being allowed to be a type value), forces the entry at the parameter's key to be a leaf holding that code, and `leafOk`, which read every key of that form and so the parameter's key too, forbids a leaf holding a data code over the model's own carrier.

**A fault of the model, not of the statement's wording.** Restricting `types` and `leafOk` to genuine keys does not save it: `Box (A : Type) := MkBox(x : A)` at `A :=` that code must read its field at it, which only the forbidden leaf gives; and with the leaf allowed, the strict `MkBox[A](x)` at that `A` builds a tree whose field does not fit the leaf, so it fails where the checker accepts `def f (A : Type) (x : A) : Box(A) := MkBox[A](x)`, and the simulation would have broken at the same place. (This part is argued in the module's docstring, not kernel-checked.)

**Ruled, in two parts** (the orchestrator; the lane's commits call them (M-a) and (S), and this (M-a) is not D111's).
- *The model.* A `Prop`-sorted declaration has one meaning as a type, its proposition, and its data code has no level: `Code0.level (.data d _ _)` is `none` when `d.sort = 0` (`Sem.lean`, bc86f861), so the code is in no universe (`RecordsCE.xTy_not_mem_univ`). Two proofs that a data code has level 1 by `rfl` needed the sort (`Levels.tyR_level`, `Strict/Simple.goodRes_tyR`); nothing else changed. `hwt_of_fit`'s exception, which is about constructor VALUES of a `Prop`-sorted declaration, is untouched.
- *Faithfulness at genuine keys* (approved by the lead; d4b7a5ee). `RecLeafOk` asks `LeafOk` only at genuine field keys (a constructor of the declaration, a field below its arity, the earlier fields' small readings of that length); a parameter's key and the first step into a parameter's record hold whatever the parameter is. `RecordsFaithful.types` is asked only at type parameters of a parameter list of the declaration's length, and `leafOk` at lists of that length. `ParamsTyped` is membership only: its disjunct "or any type value" is DELETED. It came in at 84b67f95 with no stated reason, served nothing (the run reads a parameter strictly, so at a type value that is not a member the runs faithfulness speaks of were undefined anyway), and was how the refutation went. `CtorMem.fitsM_of_mem` tracks the genuine key. The refutation no longer typechecks against the restated bundle; `RecordsCE.lean` is kept outside the default build as a record, its proof standing at 1765a998.

**The first instance** (`DataModel/Exists/PreludeModel.lean`, 78946a91; in the default build since 0e728883). The library's declarations as the checker stores them (`preludeDecls`) and a model `dmPre`: `Pair` and `And` by the knot of a two-parameter declaration whose fields are its parameters, `False` and `True` by the empty record, each parameter's type its sort. Proved, on the standard axioms, at every run context whose data model is `dmPre` and whose declarations are the library's (any decisions, globals, unknowns and assignment): `recordsFaithful`, `fieldsFaithful`, `paramsFaithful`, `ctxFaithful`; `dataFaithful` at every program state declaring the library, under any decisions; and, at one concrete context with the checker's decisions, `instance_data`, the existence statement's conclusion at the library's state. The runs are read through piece 3's first lemmas (`RunLemmas.lean`: `runT_var`, `runT_sort`, `runT_teleFrame_param`). **What rests on a native assertion:** that the library's program state is what the checker produces from the library's items needs the checker run in the kernel, which it cannot do; `prelude_inds`, a `#guard`, asserts natively that the checker's declarations after the library are `preludeDecls`, and every theorem is about a state or context whose declarations are `preludeDecls`. The lead's rule from this: a restated bundle of hypotheses stays pinned to its instance by the default build.

**Piece 4 is false for a `Prop`-sorted declaration nested at a `Prop` parameter** (`DataModel/Exists/PropNestCE.lean`, 71921e55; in the default build since 0e728883; standard axioms). `inductive Q : Prop := MkQ(h : And(Q, True))` is accepted, and rightly (asserted natively, with two more declarations of the kind). The run reads its field's type as `And`'s code at `Q`'s own type, which for a `Prop`-sorted declaration is a proposition that reads `Q`'s record at every depth. `not_guarded`: for every record function of `And` faithful at its first field, `Q`'s field type is guarded at no set of nestable parameters (the records of a proof nested `k` times agree on every path no longer than `k`, and `Q` is inhabited at one and empty at the other, `chain_inhabited`). So piece 4, guardedness from the checker's nesting rule, holds for data-sorted declarations only, and every `Prop`-sorted declaration goes through piece 5, truth first: the least predicate closed under its constructors, then records whose own occurrences are leaves holding that proposition, then that proposition equivalent to the carrier being inhabited. That the least predicate is a fixed point needs each earlier declaration's membership monotone in its nestable `Prop` parameters, an induction of its own over the declarations (`Proto/PropSorted` did not need it: `Ev` nests nowhere). `Decomposition.lean`'s beliefs are corrected to say so, and piece 6 is recorded as false as first stated and repaired by the two rulings.

**Where it leaves the proof.** The nine pieces of D92 (`DataModel/Exists/Decomposition.lean`, outside the default build), none proved, the first stated (`runT_congrDM`): (1) the run reads a model only at the context's declarations; (2) the same with the declarations narrowed to those a term can reach; (3) the canonical reading of a declaration's field types, and its agreement with the run; (4) guardedness from the nesting rule, for data-sorted declarations; (5) every `Prop`-sorted declaration truth first, with the monotonicity above; (6) the records' parameter clauses as restated; (7) the parameters' types; (8) facts about the checker alone, D111's (8a) and (8b); (9) the assembly. The riskiest are (3), (4) and (5)'s monotonicity. `FieldLevelsFaithful` is stated in `Faithful.lean` and asked by no consumer yet.

### D111. Facts about the checker alone that other lanes assume (2026-11-21; written down here; none in Lean yet)

What the simulation and the existence proof assume of the checker, each to be stated and proved with no model in scope (D88), by the machine lane once its induction is closed. The forms below are the orchestrator's. The machine lane's working notes (`Steps/Machine/HANDOFF.md` in its worktree, not yet committed) propose a Lean form for each and judge three of the five doubtful as worded, for the reasons given with each.

For (II):
- **(M-a) No live loan or mark on a data write's path.** At every data write through `setPlace`, the root's stored value has no live loan and no live mark along the path (`firstLiveLoanOnPath env (valAt env pos) steps = none` at the state of the write). It is what discharges the strict run's test at a write to a field (D104), through the relation. Measured on main with D115 built: no live loan or mark on the path of any of 48,804 writes; the one exclusion is the join's mark over a lent place (six times), which writes a mark, not data, and is excluded by name. It is a property of `setPlace`'s call sites, not of one function (`openWrite`, `readPlace`'s two moves, `borrowPlace`, `assignPlace`, a capture's move, `dropAtJoin`; the mark write is `joinEnds`'): the access before each write loops until the path has no live loan, and what must be shown, site by site, is that nothing between the access and the write puts one back.
- **(M-b) A successful access ends no label a temporary holds** (main's D106, checked in `endTopLoan`). It is what lets a step that accesses with a value in flight (the join's drop, a `let`'s drop, a pop, a later argument) keep the temporaries' labels. The machine lane's reading: `endTopLoan`'s check is skipped inside private copies, so the fact holds only outside them, and it needs "ending one borrow leaves every other borrow where it is".
- **(M-c) A borrow ends only over whole, repacked content.** When `endBorrow` succeeds on a label, its holder's content has no moved-out part, and in a typed run it passed [Repack] at the borrow's type (main's D98, D53, D64). It is what discharges every strict end of D106. An inversion of one function's body.

For the existence proof (D110's piece 8):
- **(8a) Decisions depend only on the globals a term mentions.** Two states with the same declarations that agree on every global name a term mentions give decisions that agree on that term, at every scope and on each subterm. The machine lane judges it doubtful as worded: a decision about a global reads the global's entry, whose stored type can mention other globals, so the agreement must be closed under the globals' types; and the two states must agree on the frozen readings of any unknown the term holds. Its proposed form: the second state's globals extend the first's (the chain of items only appends) and the declarations agree.
- **(8b) An accepted declaration mentions only globals of the state it was checked in.** If `checkItem (.ind d)` succeeds from a state, every global name in `d`'s parameter and field-type terms is a global of that state. The machine lane judges it doubtful: the decision procedures answer for an unknown constant without failing (an unknown name is reported only where evaluation meets it), and the evaluation of a field type at generic values need not reach every subterm (an arm a match on a known constructor does not take, a λ's body). To be measured first, over the suite's declarations and with a probe that puts an unknown name under a Π in a field; if the checker does not look every name up, the fix is a checker change, not a proof.

### D112. The form on the lead's branch, and the column so far (seventh lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead7` 4a0f10d7, not on `spine`; checked there)

**What is built** (879a35f0 and after). D107's form as Lean. `Steps/Form.lean`: `Pre` (read off the checker state's kernel, `MState.norm`: the run context's static part `CtxS`, the syntactic invariant `Inv`, the assignment's facts `Asg`; no membership), `Ext` (with `Ext.trans`), `Out`, `Sim`, `eval_sim`, `sim_seqS`. `FormKern.lean`: `Pre`, `Rel` and `DecTab` read only the kernel, so the bookkeeping a checker function does around a sub-run changes nothing a step assumes. `FormErase.lean`: nothing the simulation reads reads a temporary's stored type. `FormMachine.lean`: the facts about the checker alone the column uses, as hypotheses for the machine lane (`Mode`, `EvalInv`, `ZeroInv`, `InvCore`, `SuccInv`, `Quiet`, `VarNumInv`, `LetInv`, `DropNoopInv`); `Mode` is the flags under which the checker's read moves as the strict run's does, without which a step statement is false inside `clone`, where the checker copies. Two changes to D107's form, each forced by a step: `Out` says the result's label is the checker's borrow renamed (a `let` moves the holder of the value it binds into the binding, on both sides); and `Inv` keeps only the clauses of well-formedness that read no meaning, (WF7) and (WF9) being left out until a step needs them.

**Proved at 4a0f10d7** (standard axioms): `sim_seqS` (two steps compose, the reference state between them good by (I)'s last conjunct); `zero_core`; `succ_core`, for an argument whose value is a number (the strict point passes because the argument means a number; the general step needs the value in flight to hold no live loan and the soundness of the argument's type check); `var_core`, the read of a variable bound to a number at `Nat`, both ways the copy decision goes; `eval_of_core`, the step at `eval` from the step at `evalCore`; the bindings' plumbing, `rel_pushBind_num`, `letLenders_noop`, `refd_sub`, `rel_popBind_noSites`, `rel_popBind_rd`; and `Regress/FieldWriteGap.fieldWrite_gap`, D106's record on the relation's side.

**Not reproducible at its commit.** `let_core` (`Steps/ColumnLet.lean`, 8339fbdc, reported proved) does not build at 8339fbdc or at 4a0f10d7: it gives `CoreSim` and `EvalSim` an argument that only an uncommitted change to `Column.lean` in the lead's worktree adds. Since `Meaning.lean` imports the file, `lake build OchrMeaning` fails at the branch's head. Not counted as proved until it builds from a commit.

**A statement refuted** (`ColumnPlumb.PopBindCE.rel_popBind_false`, standard axioms, at every run context, assignment and future). The plumbing statement for the pop of a `let`'s binding as first stated (the popped value has no loans and holds no borrow, meaning nothing is lent at it) is false: `Value.loans` does not walk the declared types a closure records for its captures, while `Value.sitesOf` of a sealed program does, so a binding holding the sealed program `⌈clo [] Z [(other, loan 0)]⌉` has no loans, holds no borrow, and is a fill site of a live label. (WF15) excludes such values; `Rel` does not. The false statement is deleted; its corrected forms are `rel_popBind_noSites` (no site at the binding) and `rel_popBind_rd` (the first form's hypotheses with (WF15)'s clause for the popped value).

**A finding** (from `let_core`'s commit). The checker's typed `let` evaluates its tail with no expected type, while the reference passes the `let`'s own; so the step needs the tail's run to be independent of its hint, which in general is the hint independence of tails (`run_hintIndep`'s condition). The column takes it as a hypothesis (`hwhint`).

**What is left of the column**: the assignment at a field of a place, where the write test is met (with (M-a) of D111 and `heldTy l = holderTy s l`); the smallest assembly, `def C : Nat := let x = S(Z); x`, whose hypotheses are instantiated at the state `checkDef` starts from in `ColumnStart.lean` (statements, to be proved; the checker's run of the body is `#guard`ed, not kernel-evaluable); and carrying the branch onto ruling (A): it is based on `spine` 3347196b, before typed futures at whole members and the strict ends, so every column lemma that runs the strict run is to be checked again over the end tests.

| 27 | `.ref` (2163) | `evalType` | runs `A` under `SM.stmtIf` | agree | |
