| 28 | `.id`, `idType` (3657), `observe` (3624), `obsResult` (911) | each side on a copy; writes through the re-pointed borrows; ends every borrow; compares the result and the footprint's owners | each side from the same state; the same writes and ends; compares the result and EVERY position of each side's end state, the temporaries the side parked included | **A5** | `IdBorrow` |
| 29 | `J` (2166) | endpoints, motive, proof on copies; at convertible endpoints the transported term, then `joinDrop`; otherwise a stuck block | the same copies; the transported term; no join | agree, below (the join); stuck `J` is `closeBlock_step`'s | |
| 30 | `clone`, `peek` (2263) | reads copy (`withCopying`); a partly moved place is read as it is | `readPlace … true`, which fails on a partly moved place | **A14** (no program) | |
| 31 | `inplace` (2270) | read in place | `SM.headOf` | agree (since D19) | |
| 32 | `hole`, `split` | | fail | excluded (`NoHoles`), errors | |
| 33 | `.ascribe` (2277) | the type on a copy; the term at it as hint | the same, but the term's hint and the result type are `.plain _`: no Π-shape, no data | **A7** | `AnnFn`, `NilAnn` |
| 34 | a field read (`Sem.follow`) | a field has its declared type at the parameters | the field's type is a `TyM`: a function in a field has no shape, a `D(ā)` field no data | **A12** | `UseBoxFn` |
| 35 | [Open], [Repack] (`openWrite`, strong update, `repackCheck`) | a dependent value is open between writes: typed by its content | `ctorM` gives `err` for whole fields that do not pack, and the next step into it fails | **A13** | `Refill2` |
| 36 | private copies (`restoreKeep`) | the label counter survives a copy (D37) | `SM.onCopy`/`SM.stmtIf` restore it | **A15**: no meaning differs, but the run's next fresh label is not the checker's (matters when steps are composed) | |

**Summary: 36 cases. 19 agree (5 of them only since D19/D21, 4 up to "below"); 3 are the statements' business; 14 carry one or more of fifteen divergences A1–A15.** In the order of how much accepted code each breaks:

- **A9, A8** (rows 13, 21, 23): an unannotated pair or `Cons`, a bare `Nil` as an argument or a result: the run fails. Nearly every program over a parameterised type.
- **A1** (rows 3, 4, 5, 8, 9): [Access] ends what the whole variable's meaning depends on, where the checker ends what the place read (or, for a match, the tag) depends on. The run ends a borrow the checker keeps, and a later write through it is lost.
- **A3** (row 10): a match on a proof with other than one arm fails; it should be `⋆` unrun, as every proof is.
- **A5** (row 28): `Id` is false in the run whenever its sides park different numbers of temporaries, which is whenever one borrows and the other does not.
- **A7, A10, A11, A12** (rows 33, 19, 23, 34): a function or a `D(ā)` loses its structure through an annotation, a closure value's captures, a call's result, a field.
- **A2** (row 5): D67's owned access. D19's R5 put the right-hand side first, which is right, and exposed this: the full access then ends the reborrow the right-hand side just made.
- **A13** (rows 4, 5, 35): open dependent values have no meaning in the model.
- **A4, A6, A14, A15**: a join after a by-type match, the join's entry set read after the arm, `clone`/`peek` of a partly moved place, the label counter. No accepted program found that shows them; A6 and A4 change which places are `moved`, which `Id` and `Eq` observe.

**Batch status (kept current at every commit).** The batch goes in four stages, the core lemmas re-proved after each (`#print axioms`: the standard three).

- Stage 1, DONE: **A2** (`.assign` skips the access at a holder root written whole; the old borrow's end carries the content back), **A3** (`SM.erasedIf`: every wrapped clause is `•` unrun at `flags.2`, as `evalAt` erases; kernel-checked `Regress/Adequacy_flipped.erased_unrun`), **A4** (no join after a match on a proof), **A5** (`observation` reads the positions the side STARTED with), **A6** (`joinDrop` takes the entry's moved places, read before the arm), **A7** (`.ascribe`, `.eq`, `.id` hand on the annotation's shape and data; `.tind` records its data; a parameter binding keeps its domain's data). A cost of A3: a term's run now depends on the erasure flag, so `Program.lean`'s sanity lemmas (`defMeaning_one` …) assume the decisions do not erase `S(Z)`.
- Stage 2, DONE: **A8** (`PiParam.data`: a call's argument is run at its parameter's type WITH its data, so a bare `Nil` argument reads its parameters; a closure's body is run at its codomain with its structure, `RunCongr.fixCod`, so a bare `Nil` result does), **A9** (`runFields` threads the solved parameters, `Machine.evalCtor`'s `sol`: the written ones or the hint's first, then each field's returned type fills the unsolved ones, `Run.unifyTyS`; a field is run at its declared type once the parameters that type reads are solved; kernel-checked `Adequacy_flipped.ctor_no_hint` and four `unifyTyS` examples). Residual, as rows A11/A12: the inference reads the data a field's run returns, which a call's result and a field read do not carry, and it does not look for a parameter nested two deep (`List(Pair(A, B))`).
- Stage 3, DONE: **A10** (`capsTys` binds each capture at its recorded type WITH its structure: `tyShape` and `tyDataV`, two functions in the run's mutual block, read a stored Π-type's shape off its term in its captures' frame and a stored `D(ā)`'s parameters; kernel-checked `Adequacy_flipped.captured_pi_has_shape`) and a sixteenth divergence found while planning, **A16**: `MState.sstate` bound each checker binding at `Value.tyS` of its stored type, which had no Π-shape, so from `SM' K ρ st`, the state every step statement starts from, a call through a function-typed binding failed. Fixed: `CodeMeaning.tyShape` (the run's `tyShape` at the constant future) is read by `Value.tyS`; a deferred parameter type (`CodeMeaning.paramTy`) is the parameter's binder with its structure; `deltaOf` and `globalTyS` are now `Value.tyS` (kernel-checked `stored_pi_has_shape`). **Cost, a restated hypothesis:** `FillShape` (the substitution pair's hypothesis on the filler) gains "the filler is not itself a Π-type or `D(ā)` type value". A loan standing as a recorded type has no structure to read and its filler would; no stored type holds a live loan, so this never arises in a reachable state, but the pair is proved for every term. RESIDUAL: [End] of a borrow of a type-valued place holding a Π-type or `D(ā)`; the fix is the hypothesis "no recorded type of the term holds the loan" in place of this one.
- Stage 4, DONE (but NOT fixed for packed data until D25: `ctorM` meant `err` at an ill-typed future, which made a read of one field depend on a lent sibling's label): **A1**. `SState.access s p o` takes an observation `o : Sem → Sem` of the position's meaning and ends the live labels what `o` observes depends on (`SState.obsDep`; at `o = id` it is the old access). A read, a borrow, an assignment and a drop observe the content at the place's path with its type (`Run.obsAt … wholeOb`), so reading `x.b` leaves a borrow of `x.a` live; a match observes the tag only (`Run.tagOf` with `natTag`/`indTag`, replacing `contentOf` in the two match clauses), so the borrows inside a scrutinee's fields stay live until an arm reads the field. `access_FT` (the fact the congruence in the future rests on) now says: after the access, what `o` observes factors through the non-live labels; `contentOf_congr` gets the content back from it (`follow_of_wholeOb`), `tagOf_congr` is it directly. Kernel-checked: `Adequacy_flipped.access_ends_nothing` (an observation that reads no live label ends nothing) and `access_other_field` (the pair instance: observing the second component ends nothing, the whole meaning reads the label). **Two statements were false and are restated:** `access_step_stmt` claimed the checker's state below the reference's full access for every `owned`, false at a borrow variable written whole (the checker's owned access ends nothing there: A2 at the statement level); `assignPlace_step_stmt` was against `setContent` after a full access, missing the old borrow's end and the new one's hold. Both are now against what the run does: the `.assign` clause's tail is the named function `Run.assignAt`, and the statement is stated against it.

**After the batch.** Fixed: A1–A10 and A16. Still rows, each its own redesign: **A11** (a call's result has no Π-shape or data: `PiOut` carries a `TyM`), **A12** (a field read has no structure: the data model's field types are `TyM`s), **A13** (open dependent values have no meaning), **A14** (`clone`/`peek` of a partly moved place), **A15** (the label counter after a private copy). New residuals the batch recorded: parameter inference does not descend two deep and reads data that A11/A12 lose (stage 2); a type-valued filler that is a Π-type or `D(ā)` is excluded from the substitution pair (stage 3). The run's side of every row is still read off the clause except where a kernel-checked lemma is named; a kernel-checked run of one accepted program per form remains the open check (it needs a concrete data model and decisions under `run`, which is noncomputable: by `simp` lemmas per clause, as `Attack/` does for [Close]).

**Plan.** One definitions batch for A1–A10 (the join's entry set, the proof skip, the observation, access by observation, D67's owned access, structure through annotations, parameters, results of `fix` and captures, parameter inference in `runFields`), then one repair of the four core lemmas. A11, A12 (structure of a call's result and of a field: `PiOut` and the data model's field types carry a `TyM` only) and A13 (open values: `Sem`, `ctorM` and `Membership` together) are not in the batch; each is its own redesign and stays a row here until done.

### D20. What the step skeleton still lacks to compose (second lead, 2026-11-13; open, ranked)

Found while trying to derive `nfSealed_step` from the hypothesis at `fuel`. No step proof exists yet, so none of this was visible. Each item is a fault of the statements (not of the checker), with its fix. They are read off the statements and the checker's code; none is kernel-checked, and each should be before its fix is taken as settled:

1. **Sequencing. DECIDED: the simulation form; the old form is KERNEL-CHECKED FALSE; relation and central statement in Lean, the 72 statements not yet migrated.** A step was stated from `SM' K ρ st`, the checker's own state read as a reference state, and concluded `Below (mean st') s'.toMean`. Two faults, both of the statements:
   - *It does not compose.* After a first step the reference is in its own state `s'`, not in `SM' st'`; the second step's statement says nothing of a run from `s'`.
   - *It is false wherever a borrow was held* (`Regress/StaleTemp.lean`). `Below` reads every position, temporaries included. The reference parks a borrow in a temporary of the top frame until it is bound, and when it is bound the temporary stays, reading `⊥`; the checker carries a value in flight in its monad and has no temporary there; a missing position means `err`, and `err ⊑ ⊥` is false. On the accepted program `let r = &y; ()`: the checker (typed) ends with one binding and no temporary (`checkerNoTemps`, a `#guard`); the reference run ends in a state whose temporary `0` reads `⊥` (`let_borrow_run`, `aF_temp`, kernel-checked); no state with no temporary there is `Below` it (`not_below_stale_temp`, kernel-checked). So `letIn_step_stmt`, `eval_step_stmt` and `evalCore_step_stmt` were false on the simplest program that holds a borrow. (The pilot's `withTemp` aligns the one value in flight at a read or a borrow; it does not survive the value being bound.)

   **The alternative considered and rejected: one lemma "the run is monotone in the state".** It is false for `Below`: two states can be `Below`-related by a witness future that no [End] would fix (the lower reads `5` where the upper's holder holds `7`; a read then gives `5` against `7`), and `Below` relates meanings only, so two states with equal meanings and different binding counts resolve `var 0` differently. For a relation generated by early ends it would still need the run from the lower state to FAIL when it uses a holder the lower state has forgotten, and a write through `*r` does not fail (`Sem.updAt` at a `deref` of `⊥` succeeds). In the simulation form the checker's own run is a hypothesis, and the checker never uses a holder it ended, so that case does not arise.

   **The simulation form** (`Steps/Sim.lean`). A step is stated from ANY reference state `s` with `Rel K ρ φ st s`, and concludes `Rel` of the two states after. `Rel` has three parts, each forced by a counterexample above:
   - `skel`: the same frames, each with the same bindings at the same type meaning, data and declared reading.
   - `fresh`, with a renaming `π` of the checker's labels to the reference's, carried as a parameter of `Rel` and extended by each step. The two runs do NOT issue the same labels and cannot be made to: the checker runs an erased term on a private copy and keeps the label counter (D37), so a proof that borrows (`lemma(&x)`) consumes labels; the reference does not run a proof at all (the audit's A3), and its run of one could fail. The first version of `Rel` asked for equal counters and was false after any such proof. So shapes are compared through `π`, the checker's state and value are read at the run's future pulled back along `π`, and `fresh` says every label the checker has issued is renamed below the reference's counter, so the next labels are new on both sides.
   - `early`: for some list `E` of labels, the checker's state is the reference's after the borrows of `E` ended, compared at the SAME future (`SimEq`): bindings position by position; a borrow in flight in the checker's state held, by LABEL, by some temporary of the same frame whose content is above it; temporaries otherwise not compared. The futures compared are those that agree with the run's off the labels live after the ends, which is item 2's repair, folded in.

   Proved (kernel-checked, standard axioms): `Rel.start` (the relation holds at the start, `Rel K ρ φ id st (SM' K ρ st)`), `sim_seq` (two steps in the simulation form compose; with the old form the second premise exists only at `SM' st₁`), `Sim.of_below` and `Sim.of_simEq_below` (the new order is implied by the old where the old holds), and `StaleTemp.sim_stale_temp` (the new order HOLDS on the instance where `Below` fails).

   **Migrated to the simulation form:** the three refuted statements, `evalCore_step_stmt`, `eval_step_stmt`, `letIn_step_stmt` (`Statements.lean`; their area theorems are `sorry` as before, so nothing was re-proved). **Not yet:** every other statement with a `Below (MState.mean … st') s'.toMean` conjunct against a run from `SM'`: the leaves (read, borrow, assign, access, drop, pop), matches, calls, `J`, [Close], the tail forms. `Statements.lean`'s header says which. On the way, `hns` of `evalCore_step_stmt`, `letIn_step_stmt` and `J_step_stmt` was strengthened from "not a statement" to "neither a statement nor erased": since the audit's A3 the run skips an erased term, which `evalCore` runs, so the old side condition left them false at an erased term.

   **What the migration needs first, in order of risk:**
   - (a) **The renaming on a label that survives a private copy.** `Rel`'s renaming absorbs the audit's A15 (the reference restores the counter after a copy and reuses the labels it issued inside; the checker does not): those labels are dead on both sides. That is so only if no label the checker issues inside a private copy is still in its state after the copy. D37 keeps the counter because some records survive a copy; whether a LOAN can is not checked. If one can, it has no counterpart in the reference.
   - (b) **Shape agreement.** The skeleton leaves out a binding's Π-shape: the checker's binding reads it off its stored type (`Value.tyS`, at the constant future, in the frame of the type's captures), the reference's binding has the shape its run made (`runBinds` in the current state). They are the same only if a Π-type's shape does not depend on the state or the future it is read in. A call step needs it. Believed true (types are hole-free and closed over their captures); unstated.
   - (c) **`Rel` at the start. PROVED:** `Rel.start : Rel K ρ φ id st (SM' K ρ st)`, with no renaming and no early end.
   - (d) **`Rel` under an extended assignment**: `eval_sim_stmt` takes `Rel` at `ρ` and runs at `ρ'`. Item 7's locality (`run_abs_congr`) is what carries it.
   - (e) **Each leaf step from a related state**, not from `SM' st`: [Read], [Borrow], [Assign], the tag of a match, a call. This is where "an early end is harmless" is now proved, once per leaf, with the checker's run in hand: the leaf reads a place the checker has not forgotten, so the reference's access ends, among the labels of `E`, exactly those the place depends on, and what it then reads is what the checker read. The riskiest is the join's drop set (`movedPlaces` reads which roots are `⊥`, and a forgotten holder is `⊥` in the checker's state and not in the reference's) and `Id` (its observation reads every position the side started with, temporaries included).

2. **`Below` reads the reference state at futures other than the run's.** The run at `φ` stores constants read at `φ` (`let x = loan_ℓ` binds `fun _ => φ ℓ`), while the checker's `x ↦ loan_ℓ` means `φ' ℓ` at `φ'`. So `Below (mean st') s'.toMean` is false whenever a binding holds an inert loan, which is every sealed program's re-run with a hole. The order should range over futures that agree with the run's off the reference state's live labels.
3. **Inert loans in a nested run.** A sealed program's re-run binds its holes (`loan_ℓ` whose borrow is outside the run) in its private environment, where (WF2) and `TypedFuture` know nothing of `ℓ`. So the nested states are not `Good` and the hypothesis at `fuel` does not apply to them. `Good` needs the free labels of a nested run typed, as `GoodF` types the labels in flight.
4. **Untyped runs.** `evalCore_step_stmt` concludes "the reference run does not fail" for every term, typed or not. For `typed = false` on arbitrary text it is false (an unannotated `let x = Nil` succeeds in the checker's untyped run with a bare constructor and has no meaning). An untyped run is of checked code only (a callee's body, a sealed program's text); the statement needs that as a hypothesis, in the form the proof can use: the reference run of the code from the state above does not fail (membership of the callee's meaning gives it for a body, `Membership` and the substitution lemma for a re-run).
5. **`eval` is not `evalCore`. STATED.** `Machine.eval` is `evalAt`, which classifies the term, runs an erased one on a copy and restores `env`; the skeleton stated only `evalCore`, and every recursive position of the checker calls `eval`. Now `eval_step_stmt` is in the skeleton (statement, IH field, knot, area theorem `sorry`): `eval` against the run, with no side condition, since the run erases at `Decisions.flags` as `evalAt` does at `preFlags`. `evalCore_step_stmt`, `letIn_step_stmt` and `J_step_stmt` gain `hns` (the term is not a statement): since D19 the run of a statement is on a copy and `evalCore` does not erase, so without it they would be false. `proof_step_stmt` is stated for `eval`: for `evalCore` its "the state is unchanged" was false of a proof-valued `let` whose right-hand side moves a place (read, not kernel-checked).
6. **Free parameters of every statement. KERNEL-CHECKED AND FIXED** (but for `K.D`). Nothing tied `K.Γ` to `st.inds` (a constructor of a declared type has no meaning at `K.Γ = []`), `K.dm` to covering `K.Γ`, or `K.C.global` to `st.globals`: `.const n` means `K.C.global n`, so `evalCore_step_stmt K ρ φ 10` is false at every `K` whose `K.C.global "one"` is not Σ's (`Regress/ConstFree_70247f07.lean`, unbuilt record; the kernel runs the checker's `evalCore` on the global). Fixed: `CtxOk K ρ st` replaces `DeltaOk` as the hypothesis of all 72 statements; it holds the state's declarations, a data model covering them, `DeltaOk`, label-free globals (`run_congr`'s `hK`) and `GlobalsOk K st`: each global has its stored type, a constant means its stored value at it, a function means the closure of its `fix` term with its shape, and each is a member of its type (the outer induction of docs/18's theorem, now a named hypothesis). `Regress/ConstFree_flipped.lean` (built) is the positive form. Still free: `K.D` outside the `*_aligned` statements, and `K.Λ`.
7. **The run context changes with the state.** `K.Δ` is `deltaOf` of the state's unknowns, so a step that creates unknowns must hand on an extended context with its extended assignment (`∃ ρ' K'`), and what was established under `K` must carry to `K'`: the run reads the assignment and `Δ` only at the unknowns that occur in its term. That locality lemma, `Lemmas.run_abs_congr`, is PROVED (2026-11-21, from `AssignCongr.run_congrA`; standard axioms). Still to do: no statement extends `K` yet (the eight with `∃ ρ'` conclude under the `K` they were given).
8. **Unknowns of a discarded world. KERNEL-CHECKED AND FIXED.** `exitWorld` keeps the types of the unknowns an arm created (D37), and `TypedAssignment.mem` asked `ρ σ ∈ ⟦Δ(σ)⟧` of every `σ < absTy.size`. An arm's field unknown can have an empty type in the world the assignment selects, so no assignment of the state after such a split would be typed; and `exitWorld_step_stmt` was false outright, since it concludes `Good ρ st'` at the same `ρ`, which is unconstrained at the arm's unknowns (`Regress/ExitWorld_f32a7048.lean`: `¬ exitWorld_step_stmt K (fun _ => err) φ fuel` at every `K`, `φ`, `fuel`; unbuilt record). Fixed: `MState.Refd st σ` (σ occurs in a value of Ω, a stored or deferred type, the goal, a record in force, or the type of a referenced unknown), and `TypedAssignment.mem` and `Membership.absTy` range over referenced unknowns, as docs/18's "each abstract value σ of Ω" says. `Regress/ExitWorld_flipped.lean` (built): the state of the record is `Good` at the assignment that refuted it.
9. **`st'.core = st.core` after a re-run.** `nfSealed_step_stmt` (and `substV_*`) claim the core unchanged; `restoreKeep` keeps the unknowns and the η and generalisation records a nested run made, and an untyped run can η-refine at a join (`movedPlace`). Whether a re-run does so on reachable states is not settled; the statement should hand on an extended assignment instead.
10. **The renaming lemma** (`run_counter_indep`, above), needed by 5's sealed and body cases.

The ranked list above ("Risk list, ranked") is superseded in its order by: D19's R1–R5 (the run is wrong on common programs), then 1–4 here (nothing composes without them), then 5–10, then its items 5–8.

### Milestone 2: the reference run (`Run.lean`, the meaning of code [Q2]) — in progress

Done (all building, structurally recursive, no `partial`):
- `Pi.lean`: per-level carriers `el0/el1/el2` with encodings; `PiParam`/`PiOut`; `piCar0` (telescope with final contents of borrow parameters; output data × finals or `Back` with an injective backward function), `piProp` (impredicative), `piCar1`/`piCar2`. Three `rfl` examples against `Slice.lean`'s shapes.
- `SState.lean`: the run's state, a Value-free `Env` (bindings with type, meaning as a function of the future, declared reading, and the guarded-recursion bound for a function's binding of itself; temporaries; shape; label counter); `endLabel` (lifted `semEnd`), `access` (ends, iterated in label order, exactly the labels the position's meaning depends on), `MState.sstate`.
- `Apply.lean`: `dec0`/`dec1`; `applyTele` (apply a Π-carrier element along its telescope); `mkTele` (build one from a body's run at every argument, `none` where the body is undefined or ill-typed, or the backward function is not injective: such closures mean `err`). `BodyOut` is what a body's run yields: result and finals as functions of the future, and the returned label.
- `Places.lean`: `Decisions`, the interface to `Decide.lean` (flags, placeDecl, declOf, copy, piSort, joinMoves): the *aligned* decisions the run must make exactly as the checker does; the step lemmas prove the fields equal the checker's functions. `Sem.step/follow/updAt` on meanings at a type (`*` is the identity; fields through `DataModel.paramsOf`/`fieldsOf`/`fieldTy`; re-packing through `ctorM`).
- `Run.lean`: `SM`, `RunCtx`, results `SVal × TyM` (a result carries its type, since projections and hints need it), `readPlace` (access, copy or move by the declared reading), `borrowPlace`, `setContent`, and `run` for place, borrow, assign, let (binding at the rhs's type; drop = forget), seq, const, val, sort, nat/unit/zero/tt/succ, eq (both sides on copies; equality in `Sem`), the proof primitives (`⋆`), ref, clone/peek/inplace, ascribe.

**D8 revised (2026-11-09).** data-generic refuted the carrier-by-choice reading: a carrier does not determine the parameters (`Pair(True, B)` and `Pair(PLift True, B)` have one carrier `PLift True × El B`, and packing `[⋆, b]` succeeds at one and fails at the other), so `pack_congr` is unprovable and `paramsOf` could pick parameters at which the actual fields do not pack. The hint is now *structured*: `TyS` (in `Pi.lean`) gained `data : Option (String × List Sem)`, the declaration's name and its parameters' meanings, and a `ps = []` constructor value reads its parameters straight off `hint.data` (no choice). `Value.tyS` builds the hint of a stored type value; Nested fields and parameters get structured hints from `DataModel.fieldData`/`paramData` (data-generic, landed ccc9510a), so nested `ps = []` values are covered; only fields reached by projection (`Sem.follow`) still lack data. `paramsOf` stays only as an unused fallback. `rank_lt` is likewise restated relative to declared recursive positions (`recArgs`, data-generic), since carrier equality cannot order ranks (`Nat = Nat × Unit` is consistent).

### D9. The run's types carry the Π-shape (`Run.lean`, `TyS`)

Applying a function value needs its Π-shape (`PiParam`s and `PiOut`): neither the opaque carrier `ty0 (piCar0 …)` nor the value can give it back, and recomputing it from the callee's Π-type *value* means running that value's domain terms, which are not subterms of the call, so `run` could not stay structurally recursive. Storing the shape in `Sem` is impossible (`PiParam.dom : List Sem → TyM` would put `Sem` in a negative position). So the run computes *structured* types: `TyS := (m : TyM, shape : Option (List PiParam × PiOut))`, and every result, binding and (through `CodeMeaning.piShape`, to add) stored Π-type carries its shape. A call evaluates its arguments at the parameter types from the callee's shape (`runArgs`, D82's [Call-type]) and hands `callHole` evaluated pieces. `run` also takes the type its context expects (`hint`, D8): a `let` binds at its right-hand side's type, an assignment runs its value at the place's type, fields at their field types (`runFields`), parameters at theirs (`runParams`), `Eq`'s sides at `A`.

Filled so far: constructor values (`ctorOf` + the `.ctor` clause, parameters read off the hint when unwritten) and `D(ā)`; Π-type formation (`.pi` clause with `runDoms`: each domain term run in the forming state extended by the earlier parameters' meanings, the output likewise, the carrier chosen by `Decisions.piSort`: `piProp`/`piCar0`/`piCar1`/`piCar2`, and the shape kept in `TyS.shape`); calls (`callOf` on the evaluated callee and arguments: `applyTele` along the shape; a data result fixes each lent place's future at the argument's label (`TyS.label`) to its final content; a borrow result takes a fresh label `k` and each lent place reads its component of the backward function at `k`'s final content; a statement call runs on a copy, by `Decisions.flags`).

Known gaps in what is filled, for milestone 3 to state or for a later pass: (i) a recursive call's guard (`SBinding.recBound`) is not yet checked in the `.call` clause (it belongs with `fixHole`, which sets the bound); (ii) a call whose result is itself a function gets no shape (`PiOut` carries only the output's `TyM`; extend `PiOut.data` with an optional shape when a higher-order-returning example needs it); (iii) `runDoms` binds the earlier parameters with type `err` and no declared reading, so a domain term that reads a parameter's declared type (copy-or-move inside a type-level match) reads `other`.

Also filled: matches (`.matchNat`/`.matchInd`: the arm by the tag of the accessed scrutinee's meaning, a proof scrutinee by its type's one constructor (D45), then D84's `joinDrop` setting every place in `Decisions.joinMoves` to `moved`; `runArm` keeps the recursion structural); `J` (the transported term, the other arguments statements on copies); `Id` (`observation`: each side run on a copy at `A`, every live borrow of the copy ended, the result and every position compared, all under the constant future). Known gap: the match's access uses read mode where the checker's uses match mode (ends only loans at a neutral head, D29); the reference ends only what the tag depends on, which is at most that, so the step lemma is unaffected, but `contentOf` should take a mode when the step lemmas are written.

Closure formation (`.fix` clause) is filled: the closure means `el0 (piCar0 …) (mkTele … g)` where `g` runs the body in the forming frame extended by `self` and the parameters (the body's layout `[caps…, self, x̄]`, captures being the forming frame's bindings read in place), ends the borrows the body made (labels ≥ the label counter at formation: the frame pop) and reads each borrow parameter's holder for its final content; forming moves the captured places whose readings are not copy (D80). A recursive `fix` (`by xⱼ`) is its guarded fixpoint **by stages** rather than by `WellFounded.fix`: stage 0 binds `self` to `err`, stage `k+1` to stage `k`'s closure, and the function at arguments whose decreasing argument has rank `r` (`DataModel.rank` through its carrier; a `Nat`'s value) is stage `r+1`. A chain of calls that decreases by at least one in rank per call never reaches stage 0; a call that does not decrease does, means `err`, and `mkTele` then makes the whole closure mean `err`: [Rec]'s accepting direction is exactly what makes the guard never fire (docs/24 §4). The lemma that stages stabilise for a decreasing body (the semantic form of [Rec], `Slice.lean`'s `stage_stable` generalised) is milestone 3's.

Known gaps of the `.fix` clause: (i) a borrow parameter whose holder the body moved (passed on whole) reads `moved` as its final content where the checker's pop would end the borrow wherever its holder went; (ii) the parameter bindings carry no declared readings (`SBinding.decl`), as in `runDoms`; (iii) `self`'s binding is not marked with `recBound`, since the guard is realised by the stages instead.

**The knot** (`Run.lean`, after the mutual block). `meanV K ρ φ hint : Value → Sem` is `Value.mean` with the code-dependent forms given by the run itself: a sealed program is its program run from `emptyState` at the hint, read under the future; a closure or Π-closure is its `fix`/`pi` term run in a frame of its captures at their recorded types (`capsVals`, `capsTys`, `capsState`; D51/D82); a global is `CodeMeaning.global`. `run`'s `.val` clause uses `meanV`, so the whole thing is one structural recursion over `Term`/`Value`. `codeMeaning K ρ : CodeMeaning` packages `meanV` for `Value.mean`; its assignment argument is ignored (a state's meaning is only read at the state's own assignment), and `paramTy` reads the shape of the Π-type value through `run`. `meanV_eq_mean` (stated, `sorry`): `Value.mean K.dm (codeMeaning K ρ) K.Γ ρ φ hint v = meanV K ρ φ hint v`, by induction on `v`; every clause but the code-dependent ones is the same text.

**Pure substitution** (`Subst.lean`): `Value.substLoan`/`Term.substLoan`, replacing `loan ℓ` by a value everywhere (inside sealed programs, closures and types), the pure half of the checker's `substV`; re-normalisation is the run's own clause.

**Statements closing milestone 2** (`Lemmas.lean`, `sorry`): `meanV_substLoan` and `run_substLoan` (filling a loan is fixing the future, for values and for a term's run from a state; hypotheses: `w` does not mention `ℓ`, and `w`'s meaning is hint-free), `meanV_congr` (a meaning reads the future only at the value's loans). Still to write for milestone 2: the generic `DataModel` by stages (D4) and the program meaning (globals by recursion on the program, instantiating `CodeMeaning.global`), and `Decisions` instantiated by the checker's `Decide` functions (the latter is where "aligned" is discharged, so it may be milestone 3's).

### Milestone 3: the step-lemma skeleton (`Steps/*.lean`) — started

`Steps/Borrow.lean` fixes the shape: `Runs f st fuel a st'` (the checker's `runMachine` succeeds), `Good ρ st` as the only hypothesis on the state, the conclusion `Below (MState.mean … st') (semantic step on (MState.sstate … st))` with `Exact` where the step is exact, and `Good ρ st'`; the semantic side is `SState`'s primitive (`access`, `readPlace`, `borrowPlace`, `setContent`, `dropLast`); the checker's functions are referred to as `Ochr.readPlace` etc., since the run's shadow them. Statements: `endBorrow_step` (exact = `semEnd`), `accessPath_step`/`accessInside_step` (below `access`), `readPlace_step`, `borrowPlace_step` (exact against the run's, with the copy decision aligned through `Decisions.copy`), `assignPlace_step`, `dropTopBind_step`, `popFrame_step`.

One file per checker area, one lemma per rule, each of the form "if `runMachine (f …) st fuel = ok a st'` and `Good ρ st`, then for every typed future the result and `st'` are below the reference step on `st.mean ρ`, and `Good ρ' st'` for the extended assignment". Rough partition and counts, by `Machine.lean`'s sections: `Steps/Borrow.lean` (End, Access ×3, Read, Copy, Borrow, Assign, Drop ×3, popFrame: 12), `Steps/Subst.lean` (substV, substEnv, nfSealed, canonNeutral, renormAll, refine: 6), `Steps/Call.lean` (callFn, runBody, closeCall ×3 rows, callType/declCallType, paramTypes, recCheck's accepting direction: 9), `Steps/Match.lean` (evalMatch, evalMatchInd, evalMatchByType, splitThenClose, generalizeNeutral, blockCaptures, closeBlock, enter/exitWorld: 8), `Steps/Eq.lean` (mkEqM, conv, convPi, convFn, rewriteGoal, cong/symm/trans: 7), `Steps/Obs.lean` (observe, idType, footprint coverage, owners: 4), `Steps/Types.lean` (evalType, piSort, capture, evalTInd, evalCtor, fieldTypes, repackCheck, lentUnknown, valType? soundness: 9), `Steps/Eval.lean` (evalCore's remaining forms, letIn, seq, ascribe, prim: 8), `Steps/Tail.lean` (checkTail, checkFix, checkDef, checkInd, Ind-decl conditions (I1)–(I9): 6), `Steps/WF.lean` (preservation of each WF clause per step family: 7). About 75 statements, each owned by the spine; provers own private auxiliaries. The five supporting lemmas of docs/26 (membership, `valType?` soundness, footprint coverage, conversion soundness, WF preservation) are spine statements in `Types`, `Obs`, `Eq` and `WF`.

### Milestone 4: assembly (`Consistency.lean` under `Ochr/Meaning`)

`consistencyBounded` from: the program meaning (globals by recursion on the program), Corollary "an accepted definition means an element of its type" from the step lemmas by induction on `checkTail`'s case tree, conversion soundness at the leaf, and `⟦False⟧ = prop False`. `#print axioms` must show `sorryAx` plus the standard three. Then sketches for Target B (error reflection and the borrow-structure lemma against `Model/Safety.lean`'s shape; realizability [Q4] stated, not proved) and Target C (the context lemma stated).

### D113. The non-recursive `self` was `err`, so accepted programs had no meaning; the frame property was false as stated; the in-place pop is strict (model lane, third agent, 2026-11-21; `spine-model3` fa8847a6, c257d46e, 8896a075)

**Found** (`Strict/FrameCE.lean`, standard axioms). (1) A `fix` with no decreasing parameter bound `self` to `err` in its body's frame (`bodyAt`; `RunCongr.fixG`'s `none` case). `err` is an open member of no type, and the strict test after an assignment or a borrow at a field path (`lentOk` = `StateOk`) reads every binding, so the body of every such function failed and its closure was `err`: the checker accepts `def SetFstOwned (p : Pair(Nat, Nat)) : Unit := p.0 := Z`, `def SetFst (r : &Pair(Nat, Nat)) : Unit := (*r).0 := Z` and the suite's `PartMove`, which had no meaning (kernel-checked at the `.fix` clause at fa8847a6, `setT_none`; the body-level record `body_errSelf`/`body_goodSelf` stays). The exemption of `[*]` from `lentOk`, made when testing it rejected `*x := S(*x)` in `Regress/CallInPlace`, was this fault met there. (2) `FrameProp.run_frame` was false as stated (`run_frame_false`): in place the same state-wide test reads the caller's frames, of which `FrameRel` says nothing; a caller binding holding `err` made the run in place fail where the closure's ran.

**Ruled and built** (the orchestrator). A non-recursive `fix` is the recursive one at rank 0: `g := fun args => stage (rankOf args + 1) args` in both cases (`rankOf` is 0 without `dec`), so `self` is stage 0's closure at the weakened function type, a member of it (`stage_mem` at bound 0, its hypotheses vacuous). The checker never puts `self` in scope in a non-recursive body (its D31), so nothing reads it. Repaired: `fixG_congr`, `fixG_bodyEq`, `fixG_congrA` (generic in `dec` now), `Regress/CallInPlace` (`gInc`, `gId1`, their `self`s). `run_frame` restated (statement only) with the in-place state good (`Strict.SGood`), `FundCtx` and the term's embedded values good, so `fund_all` carries it; `run_counter_indep`, its no-caller-frame case, is stated alone (that case needs no goodness; `Steps/Call.nestedRun_sim` keeps its statement).

**The in-place pop is strict** (c257d46e): `CallRef.runBodyRef` succeeds only where the pop's ends (`popEnds`, factored out of `popCallFrame`) pass `EndsOk`, as `bodyAt`'s pop does: under ruling (A) every end is a strict point. `endInto` (the renaming of the returned borrow) is not an end. Statement changes under `Steps/`: `CallAsm.runBodyRef_eq` carries the guard; `CallAsm.runBodyRef_extend`'s conclusion is the guarded `if`; `runBody_step_asm` reads the guard off `hok`. The obligation to (II) is the checker's D98 at the pop, as at `bodyAt`'s.

**Tried and kept out: the test at `[*]`.** With the stage-0 `self` every concrete run through `*` passes in substance (the one hand-built state re-proved, `Attack/RunEqs.S₅`, passes), but the regress lemmas through `*` (five in `Regress/CallInPlace`, those of `FrameCheck` and `CallInPlaceD99`) are stated over an ARBITRARY `self` binding or arbitrary meanings, where `StateOk` needs new hypotheses; and from a good state the test at `[*]` always passes (`Strict/OpsPlace` proves those cases from the clause's own membership test). So it would cost restatements and change no result.

**Statement-level findings not yet restated.** `FrameRel.held` asks the parameters' records (`heldMem`) to agree, false at the call whenever a borrow argument was written through before it (in place the record is the content when the borrow was made, in the closure the argument; nothing reads a parameter's record), and does not ask `heldTy` at the parameters, which the strict ends read. At a call the two frames differ at `self` (in place the callee, in the closure the stage below): the knot owes "the body runs the same with either `self` below the bound". The intended frame lemmas of (I) (the call's frame, the strict pop, `endInto`, a closure body's frame) are listed in `Strict/HANDOFF.md`, "Next".

### D114. The relation asks that the reference hold each label at ONE position (`Rel.uniq`) (eighth lead, 2026-11-21; the borrow column; `spine-lead8`)

**Found** (by reading, while stating the `let` of a borrow for `def B : Nat := (let x = S(Z); let y = &x; *y := Z; x)`; not kernel-checked). `SimEq` matches the temporaries holding a borrow by label, each checker temporary to SOME reference temporary and each reference one to SOME checker one, not one to one; and nothing else in `Rel` or `Strict.SGood` says a label has one holder. So a reference state with TWO temporaries holding the label `π l` of the borrow the checker has in flight is related. The strict `let`'s `pushBindShaped` moves only the first holder into the binding (`takeLabel`, by `holderOf`), and the second stays a holder that no checker position matches: `Rel`'s clause (5) fails after the push, and the end of the borrow at the read leaves the label live there. So the step for a `let` whose right-hand side is a borrow is false as the form stood.

**Changed.** `Rel` (`Steps/Statements.lean`) gains `uniq : ∀ p q l, s.shape p = some l → s.shape q = some l → p = q`, a fact about the reference state alone, as `holders` is. `Rel.start` takes it of the checker's state read as a reference state (whose shape is the checker's: WF1), and `Rel.start_noLive` derives it, with the records, where the checker's state holds no borrow, the form every caller had (`ColumnStart`, `CallAsm`, four `Regress` files). Every construction of `Rel` carries it: unchanged shapes (`of_kern`, `of_env`, `private_copy`, `rel_erase`, `rel_withTemp_inert`, `rel_pushBind_num`, `rel_setNum`, `botWrite_rel`, the slice's and the drop's records), a dropped binding (`rel_popBind_noSites`), the borrow's new temporary (`ColumnBorrow.rel_lend`). No proof changed otherwise.

**Considered.** The uniform home is `Strict.SGood` (it is a property of the strict run, kept by every operation, so (I) would prove it once); `Strict/` is the model lane's. Until it moves there, `Rel` carries it as it carries `holders`.

### D115. The borrow column: five steps with a live label, proved; the assembly open (eighth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead8` 37117383)

**What.** For `def B : Nat := (let x = S(Z); let y = &x; *y := Z; x)`, the first column with a live label, each step in D107's form (`CoreSim`, typed runs), on the three standard axioms: `borrow_core` (`Steps/ColumnBorrow.lean`), `letB_core`, `assignDeref_core`, `seq_core` (`Steps/ColumnLetB.lean`), `readEnd_core` (`Steps/ColumnReadEnd.lean`). The assembly at `B`'s start state (`column_B_ff`) is not done; `Steps/LEAD-HANDOFF.md` has its plan.

**What carried the live label.** No change to `Pre`, `Ext`, `Out` or `Sim`; one to `Rel` (D114, `uniq`). The new label is renamed by extending the renaming (`renameAt π lc lr`, `Out`'s label conjunct); the relation is compared at futures that may give the new label any whole member, the old positions reading no label at or above the reference's counter (`SGood.indep_fresh`) and the checker's only labels that occur (renamed below it, `Rel.fresh`); temporaries are matched by label (the two sides' temporary indices differ); the record is the borrowed content, which the strict `.borrow` tests. The strict END at the read (ruling (A), D106) passes through the relation (`typedFut_rel`, `heldTy_eq_holderTy`), and the lender is read through the future at the holder's content (`SGood.holderIndep`, `Rel.records`), as lead7's plan said.

**Assumed** (facts about the checker alone, `FormMachine.lean`, unreviewed): `BorrowNumInv`, `AssignDerefInv`, `SeqInv`, `VarEndInv`, `LetInv`, `InvCore` over `ColTerm` extended by `&x`, `*y := u`, `u; w`. Three of the four borrow facts as first stated were false (a lazily typed binding, a reborrowed content, the lender at another type; refuted by the checker's compiled runs, `ColumnB`'s sanity runs) and are restated. One precondition beyond the column's: no position has a site of a label at or above the counter (a mark is a site and not an `Occurs`, so `Inv`'s labels-below-counter clause does not give it).

### D116. `InvCore` dropped: the invariant is carried through each step's exact state change; the machine lane's `SuccInv`/`LetInv` forms adopted (ninth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead9`)

**Ruled** (the orchestrator, 2026-11-21): `InvCore` ("`evalCore` of a column term keeps `Inv`") cannot be proved as stated (it quantifies over states and paths no step uses), and the machine lane's proved forms of `SuccInv` and `LetInv` replace the lead's.

**Changed.** `InvCore` and `ColTerm` are deleted. Each step carries `Inv` from where it has it (its entry's `Pre`, or a sub-step's `Out`) through the exact state change its inversion fact concludes, by a state-level lemma "`Inv` before, the change, `Inv` after" (`FormMachine.lean`, bundled as `InvSteps`, for the machine lane): a number or `()` pushed or popped as a temporary; a number binding set to `⊥` or overwritten by a number; the value in flight moved into a pushed binding (a number at `Nat` read `.other`; a borrow of a number at `&Nat` read `.ref .other`); the top binding (`⊥` or a number) popped; a number binding lent with the borrow in flight; a write through a borrow binding; the end of `B`'s borrow by the read of its lender. `SuccInv` and `LetInv` are now the machine lane's `SuccInvR`/`LetInvR` word for word (`ModeEq` restated in `FormMachine`, converted in `ColumnCFF`; `succInv`, `letInv` there are their proofs); what lead8's `LetInv` also said (`u` leaves the top frame's readings and a frame) is the caller's, from the column's own run facts. `SeqInv` and `AssignDerefInv` conclude `ModeEq` across their stretch, never `Mode` at a later state. `AssignDerefInv` was FALSE (the machine lane's review): the write's drop of `y`'s old content ends it if it is a borrow (`y ↦ borrow_l (borrow_m z)`, `x ↦ loan_m`: `x` gets `z` back); its `Kern` clause now asks the old content not a borrow. `succ_core` is for typed runs only (its untyped stretch is `Quiet` with no run to carry `Inv` through).

**Found.** `Inv` across a `Quiet` stretch is NOT a state-level fact: `Quiet` leaves `nextAbs` and the types of the unknowns a private computation makes free, and `Inv` reads them ((WF6)'s `absTy.size = nextAbs` and its bound on the unknowns of `absTy`; (WF8) and (WF11) on `absTy`). So each inversion fact's quiet stretch carries `KeepsInv` (a conjunct of `VarNumInv`, `BorrowNumInv` (re-proved so by the machine lane), `AssignNumInv`, `AssignDerefInv`, `VarEndInv`), and the type check of `S` has `InvExpectTy` (a comparison keeps `Inv`): facts about the computation, for the machine lane; `InvExpectTy` only where the type is checked against itself (for arbitrary types a whole-machine fact).

**Found by the machine lane** (machine3, by reading, with `Γ` one inductive): six of the ten state-level lemmas were FALSE as first stated, all because `Inv`'s (V) reads `D.placeDecl` at every place for the step's FREE table `D`, which the alignment pins only where a `placeDecl` run succeeds: pushing or popping a binding moves the readings and the indices, and a number put where a loan or nothing was meets readings the old `Inv` never constrained (a number's `.0`). Fixed by `PlaceLocal D` (a place's reading is its root's reading and its steps; no place under `.other` or `.ref .other` reads an inductive; machine3's text, proved of the fuel-free table) and, for the writes, the written binding's reading. The columns carry `PlaceLocal K.D` beside `DecTab`.

**Then: the borrow column assembled** (`Steps/ColumnBFF.column_B_ff`, standard axioms; no change to the form). As the plan said: the steps' preconditions by the EXACT environment (`Env.erase` equal to a literal: one empty frame; `x ↦ S(Z)`; `x ↦ loan_l`, `y ↦ borrow_l c`), from which `BorrowPre`, `DerefVar`, `ReadPre`, the readings and the bindings' readings follow by computation; the decisions at `B`'s three scopes kernel-evaluated (`y`'s reading is `.ref .other`); the checker's runs of each sub-term carry the shapes from one to the next and give the steps' side conditions (the value bound, the binding dropped, the mode after a sub-run, the readings kept). Each step's conclusion met the next one's precondition; the composition through `let_core`/`letB_core`/`seq_core` (extending the renaming at the new label, ending it at the read) needed nothing beyond them. `column_B_ff` assumes `AssignDerefInv`, `SeqInv`, `VarEndInv`, `InvSteps` and `PlaceLocal` of the fuel-free table. Found on the way: `DropNoopInv` concludes no mode, so the mode after a `let` is unknown (not needed by `B`).
### D117. "Each label has one holder" moves into the model's invariant (`Strict.SGood.uniq`) (model lane, fourth agent, 2026-11-21; `spine-model4`)

**Tried to refute first** (by reading every place the reference changes a shape). A label gets a holder in three ways only: made fresh (`borrowPlace`, `callOfL`'s `.ref` row, `bodyAt`'s borrow parameters: the counter, held nowhere, `ShapeAt`), moved (`takeLabel` then one new holder: `readPlace`'s move, `pushBindShaped` for a `let` and `callFrame`'s parameters, `assignAt` at a root), or renamed (`endInto k g`, `g` reserved and never held). Everything else only forgets entries (`ShapeSub`: ends, [Access], drops, the join's drop, writes) or runs on a copy (erased terms, `anyArm`, the observation). A borrow argument kept by the caller (a non-borrow parameter) stays parked, not copied; a borrow returned through a match is the arm's temporary; a capture by value marks its position `⊥` and keeps the stale entry, still one position. The one spot that needs an argument rather than a reading is `popCallFrame` (outside `fund_all`): it re-parks the returned label after dropping the top frame's entries, so the label must be held in the top frame; every clause returning a label returns one parked in the top frame (by induction on the clauses), which the frame lemma `sgood_popCallFrame` will have to carry. No duplication found.

**Changed.** `SState.Uniq s := ∀ p q l, s.shape p = some l → s.shape q = some l → p = q` (`Meaning/Uniq.lean`, with one lemma per shape operation; the only non-trivial one is `takeLabel_free`: with `ShapeAt` and `Uniq`, `takeLabel l` leaves no holder of `l`, since it forgets the first holder `find?` meets and that is the only one). `SGood` and `SStruct` gain `uniq`; every constructor supplies it (`Ops`, `Struct`, `OpsCall` with `callOfLS_uniq`/`parkState_uniq`, `OpsPlace`, `Match`), so `fund_all` carries it with no new hypothesis. Witness states: `sA`, `sC`, `sE`, `sF`, the two CE files; a run that makes a label and moves it from its temporary into a binding: `Strict/WitnessLet.lean`, `let y = &x; c` (`run_letBorrow`, `sG2_good`: `y` holds the label at one position, `w_letBorrow`). One repair outside the lane: `Steps/ColumnStart.sgood_stC` gains `uniq` (empty shape). `Rel.uniq` is untouched; the simulation's lead can now take it from `SGood` and drop it.

### D118. The reference reads a `let` binding's reading BEFORE its right-hand side runs, as the checker does (model lane, fourth agent, 2026-11-21; `spine-model4`)

**Why.** The checker's `let x = u; w` takes `u`'s syntactic reading (`declOf`, under `withLive`) before `eval u` and pushes the binding with it (`Machine.evalCore`'s `.letIn`); the reference read it after running `u`, in the scope `u` left. The simulation then had to assume "`u`'s run leaves the top frame's readings" (the readings conjunct of `hnumu`/`hbor` in the columns), false for a `u` that writes. **Changed.** `Run.lean`'s `.letIn`: `let d := K.D.declOf (scopeOf s) u` first, then `u` runs, then `pushBindShaped … { decl := d }`. Repairs: `RunPresInd`, `RunCongr`, `Strict/Clauses2` (the pre-state's scope), the two restated copies of the clause (`Steps/ColumnLet.runT_letIn`, `Attack/RunEqs.run_letIn`), and the columns' push (`ColumnLet`, `ColumnLetB`: `hpb` rewrites `scopeOf s` by the start state's relation, no longer through the readings conjunct, which those two proofs no longer use there).

### D119. The reference hands expected-type hints as the TYPED checker does; no `typed` flag (model lane, fourth agent, 2026-11-21, the orchestrator's ruling; `spine-model4`)

**Ruling.** The reference follows the checker that CHECKS (`eval` with `typed`, which `checkDef` runs, Check.lean). The checker's two modes differ at every hint site, not only at tails: let/`;` tails and both matches' arms (typed: none; untyped: the hint); an assignment's right-hand side (typed: `placeType?` iff it is a constructor or an embedded value; untyped: none); a call's argument (typed: iff constructor, embedded value, `rewrite`, `rewriteR` or hole; untyped: iff `needsCtx`); a constructor's field (typed: iff (parameters and constructor/`rewrite`/`rewriteR`) or embedded value or (dependent declaration and dependent field); untyped: iff the parameters are solved and `needsCtx`); `J`'s transported term run in place gets none in both. Mirroring the untyped mode would make the strict run fail on accepted programs: it leaves hints out exactly where it checks nothing, and the strict constructor with no record fails (`ctorOut … none`). The agreement at untyped runs (an in-place call's body, a sealed program's re-run) is the business of those lemmas on the simulation's side.

**Changed.** `Run.lean`: `Term.assignHinted`, `Term.argHinted`, `Term.fieldHinted` (the typed checker's syntactic tests); the `let`'s and `;`'s tails, both matches' arms (and `anyArm`'s) and `J`'s transported term run at `.plain err`; `.assign`, `runArgs`, `runFields` hand their type only at those forms. Repairs (no result changed: every concrete run kept its stated result; only intermediate statements and hypotheses moved to the no-hint run): `RunPresInd`, `RunCongr`, `AssignCongr`, `RunSubst`/`RunSubstAbs` (the three tests commute with substitution), `HintIndep` (the tail cases are one run on both sides), `Strict/Clauses2`, `Witness`, `WitnessLet`, `Regress/{CE4,CE5,Adequacy}_flipped` (Adequacy's two theorems now take the arms' runs at no hint), `CodeRuns`, `CallInPlace`, `Attack/RunEqs`, `DataModel/ParamSolve`, `DataModel/Exists/RunLemmas.runArgs_one` (statement: the argument's run at its hint form). Steps (minimal, no new `sorry`): `ColumnLet.runT_letIn`, `ColumnLetB.runT_seq` restated; the columns' `hxw'` is the tail at no hint (`hwhint` used once instead of twice); `ColumnWrite.runT_assign` restated; `EqJAsm.runT_J_some` and the statement of `J_arm` (already a `sorry`) at the transported term's no-hint run.

**Probes** (`Strict/WitnessHint.lean`, standard axioms). `def SetNone (p : &Opt(Nat)) : Unit := (*p := None)` and `def UseIt : Opt(Nat) := (let x = Some(1); (SetNone(&x); x))` are accepted, `def TailNone : Opt(Nat) := (let y = Z; None)` rejected (checker verdicts). `setNone_inPlace`: SetNone's body runs in place at its frame above UseIt's (the right-hand side `None` handed the place's type); `runT_noneT_noHint`: with no hint, as the untyped checker hands it there, it fails; `tailNone_none`: TailNone's `let` fails at every outer hint.

### D120. `Rel.uniq` removed; the `let` steps' hint and context bridges removed (tenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead10`)

**Why.** D117 put "each label has one holder" into the model's invariant (`Strict.SGood.uniq`), which every step has of its reference state, so the relation's copy of it (D114) only had to be re-proved at every construction of `Rel`. D118 and D119 made the reference read a `let` binding's reading before its right-hand side and run a typed `let`'s and `;`'s tail at no hint, as the checker does, so the columns' `hwhint` (the tail at the `let`'s hint equals the tail at none) was the identity, and `hwcong` (the tail at the extended `Δ`/assignment equals the tail at the old) follows from `runT_congrA` for a tail with no unknowns, which the steps already assume (`hwa`).

**Changed.** `Rel` loses `uniq`; `Rel.start` loses its hypothesis; `ColumnLetB.rel_pushBind_borrow` takes `s.Uniq` (its caller passes `SGood.uniq` of the reference state after the right-hand side). `let_core`, `letB_core`, `seq_core` lose `hwhint` and `hwcong` (`ColumnLet.wcong`). `assign_core` and `assignDeref_core` lose `hhint` (the right-hand side's run at the place's type equals its run at none): they take `u.assignHinted = false`, and `AssignNumInv`/`AssignDerefInv` gain the conjunct "where `u` is not a constructor or an embedded value, the checker hands it no hint" (`Machine.evalCore`'s `.assign`, read: `if typed && (u matches .ctor .. | .val _) then placeType? … else none`), which the reference's `Term.assignHinted` mirrors. `succ_core` keeps its `hhint`: `Run.lean`'s `.succ` still runs its argument at `Nat` while the typed checker hands none (D119 did not change that clause; harmless for terms whose run reads no hint). Both column theorems (`column_C_free`, `column_B_ff`) hold with unchanged statements, on the standard axioms.

**Superseded in part (D121):** the assign steps' change above was reverted at the merge with the machine lane's proved `AssignDerefInv`; they take `hhint` again and no fact carries a hint conjunct.

### D121. The assign steps keep `hhint`; no hint conjunct on the machine's facts (eleventh lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead11`)

**Why.** The machine lane meanwhile restated and PROVED `AssignDerefInv` (its conclusions after `u`'s run under "`y` holds `borrow_l w`, `w` a number, at `st₁`"), without D120's last conjunct (`u.assignHinted = false → hu = none`). Re-adding it would put an unproved fact back under `column_B_free`. It is not needed: the conjunct only served to rewrite `u`'s strict run at the hint the checker handed it into its run at no hint, and for every fragment term that run reads no hint at all (`Frag.frag_hint`, proved by unfolding: each clause the fragment uses hands its sub-terms fixed hints), and for the column's `u = Z` likewise (`runT_zero`). So the hypothesis `hhint` (the right-hand side's strict run is the same at every hint) is discharged by the caller with no fact about the checker.

**Changed.** `assign_core`, `assignDeref_core` take `hhint` again (as on `spine` before D120); `AssignNumInv` loses D120's last conjunct; `AssignDerefInv` is the machine lane's proved statement, unchanged. Both columns: no assumed fact, standard axioms.

### D122. The fragment's states: `FSt` gains `order`, `lent`, `frame`; `FStT` for a value in flight; `FragRun` (eleventh lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead11`)

**Why.** The fragment's `let` (now proved, `FragLet.letF_core`) needs three facts of its states that `FSt` did not carry. (1) The binding a `let` drops may be lent: `endLoansOfTopBind` would end its borrower. In the fragment the borrower can only be the value in flight (then the checker rejects, `DropLentFail`), because a lender's binding precedes its holder's (`&x` needs `x` in scope, and no fragment write stores a borrow into an older binding): `FSt.order`. Without it a lent dropped binding's borrower could be an older binding and the drop's case analysis does not close. (2) `InvEnd` asks the lender be a binding read `.other`: `FSt.lent` (a lent binding is a number variable). (3) The `let` pushes into a top frame: `FSt.frame`. And a sub-term's run ends with its value in flight, which `FSt.held` ("every loan's borrow is held at a binding") excludes when the value is a borrow: rather than weaken `FSt` (the entry condition of every sub-term, where no borrow is in flight), the run facts conclude `FStT`, the same clauses on the end state with the value parked as the last temporary of the top frame, which may hold that borrow (`tempLast`).

**Changed.** `FSt` + `order`, `lent`, `frame`. New `FT.val`, `FStT`, `FragRun` (`Frag.lean`; `FragNatRun` is its corollary). `FragCases` loses `letIn`; it and `frag_sim` moved to `Steps/FragSim.lean`, which bundles the assumed facts as `FragFacts`. Nothing in `Pre`, `Ext`, `Out`, `Sim`, `Rel` changed.

### D123. A function's own name has the context's type in the relation (`Binding.refTy`) (thirteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead13`)

**Why.** At the start of a function's body (`Machine.checkFix`'s generic state; probed for `def I (n : Nat) : Nat := S(n)`: frames `[{}, {I ↦ .gfn "I" at Π(n:Nat).Nat read .pi .other, n ↦ σ0 at Nat read .other}]`), `Pre K ρ st ∧ Rel K ρ φ π st s ∧ SGood s` was unsatisfiable for the knot's context and circular for any other. `Rel.sim` makes the reference's `self` value `meanV (.gfn I) = (K.C.global "I").1`; `Rel.skel` made its type the checker's stored Π-type read by `K.M = codeMeaning K`, whose `pi` is the run's reading, the FULL `Π Nat → Nat` (no choice of `K.C` changes it); `SGood.bind` then needs `(K.C.global "I").1` to be a member of the full type. With `K.cur = (I, b)`, `GlobalsOk.memBelow` makes it the stage-`b` closure at the WEAKENED type, and at `b = 0` (the only step a non-recursive function needs) `stageFn _ _ _ 0` returns fill values: not a member. With `K.cur = none` or `b ≥ 1`, `GlobalsOk` asserts the function's own membership: the conclusion assumed. The two sides type the function's own name differently BY DESIGN (the checker stores the declared type and enforces termination separately; the model's body frame, `fixBodyAt`, binds the stage approximation at the weakened type), so no relation that makes stored types agree position by position can hold there; the relation is the place to say so, and (I) stays uniform (every binding of a reference state is a member of its type).

**Ruled (orchestrator).** The reference type of the function being checked is the context's, `(K.C.global f).2`, wherever it sits with a type, in two stages: (1) at a binding, in the one translation the relation reads types through, `Rel.skel` unchanged; (2) QUEUED for the model lane: at a capture inside a closure value (`capsTys`, `codeMeaning.clo`).

**Stage 1, as built.** `Binding.refTy` (`SState.lean`), read by `MState.sstate` for every binding's type: the stored type's code (`Env.hintAt`), except at a binding the checker MARKS as naming a function whose body is being checked (D78's `recUid`: the function's binding of itself, and a closure's capture of it) and stores at a Π-type (`Binding.piTyped`), whose type is `(C.global b.hint.name).2`. The ruling said "a binding whose VALUE is `.gfn f`"; keyed on the value, every write lemma (`skel_setAtE` and its five generic callers in the old stack, e.g. `dropOne_exists`, where the moved binding's old value is arbitrary) needed a new hypothesis, since moving a `let g = F` binding would change its reference type (equal again only through `GlobalsOk.ty` and an invariant on stored types that the generic stack does not have). Keyed on the mark it is write-stable (a write changes only the value), it is what the checker's own [Rec] keys on (D78: by syntax, never by comparing function values), and on reachable states it agrees with the value-keyed rule: the marked bindings are exactly those holding the function being checked (by the probe below), and for a finished global held by an unmarked binding the context's type and the stored type's agree (`GlobalsOk.ty`). Changed: `PosLib.sstate_tyAt_bind` (now `refTy`; `sstate_tyAt_bind_hint` for an unmarked binding), `sstate_tyAt_m` (no marked binding), `ColumnPlumbAux.skel_eq` (`refTy`), `ColumnRead.rel_bind_refTy` (new; `rel_bind_ty` keeps its statement with an auto-param `b.piTyped = false`, discharged at every caller from its stored type), `FormErase.refTy_erase`; the skeleton lemmas unfold `refTy`. `column_C_free`, `column_B_free`, `frag_run_ff` build with unchanged statements and the standard axioms.

**Why stage 1 is sufficient where it is: the probe** (`Regress/SelfCapture.lean`, asserted). A non-recursive `F`: `let g = F`, `Ap(F)`, `MkBox(F)`, returning `F`, a λ calling `F`: REJECTED, "not in scope in its own body (D31)". A recursive `R` (`by n`): `let g = R`, `Ap(R)`, `MkBox(R)`, returning `R`, `let g = R; g(m)`: REJECTED, "[Rec] R occurs in its own body other than as the head of a call (fix L1)"; a λ calling `R` at its own parameter: REJECTED ("not a strict subterm"). ACCEPTED: `def R7 (n : Nat) : Nat by n := match n { Z => Z, S m => let h = (λ(x : Nat) : Nat => R7(clone(m))); h(Z) }` (and `R9`, the closure moved into another binding): the name flows into a λ's captures (D78 keeps the mark), the closure value `h ↦ .clo [.gfn R7, …] t rd` records the capture at the full Π-type. Not a termination hole (the call inside the λ is checked against the refinement). It is what stage 2 covers: today `Rel.sim` at `h` compares two closures whose capture types differ, and the closure value's frame is not good. So stage 1 covers every non-recursive function and every recursive one whose name no λ captures. The checker fact it rests on (for the machine lane, `OPEN.md`): in an accepted definition's body the function's own name occurs only as the head of a call, or, for a recursive function, inside a λ's captures.

**Not covered (noted, not ruled).** A λ's own `self` binding while the λ's body is checked (`checkFix` marks it too; its value is the closure, its hint the λ's name) gets `(C.global λname).2`: wrong, but that case (the simulation of a closure formed in a body, from the checker's check of the λ) has the same mismatch as the top-level one and is the knot's for λs.

**Also.** The checker's body state has TWO frames (frame 0 for borrow parameters' cells), `fixBodyAt`'s one: bridged by an ASSUMED model fact, `FrameDrop0` (`Steps/ParFacts.lean`; the frame property at no parameter label and an empty caller frame). Queued for the model lane: whether the reference's body frame should mirror the checker's two frames, which would remove the bridge.

### D124. The merged checker on `I` and `P`; the surface's `S(n)` is a call (fourteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead14`)

**The merge.** `spine-lead14` = `spine-lead13` + `spine-machine5` (main's D115 and D116 in the proof line, `PlaceLocal.unit`, `SeqBorInv` restated, nine fragment facts proved). No textual conflict; `OchrMeaning` builds; `column_C_free`, `column_B_free`, `frag_run_ff` and `ParBody.body_simI` are on the standard axioms. D115 puts call arguments, an assignment's right side, a new borrow and a join's result through the state as temporaries; it does not touch `S` (`evalCore`'s `.succ` case is unchanged: `S` evaluates its argument and wraps it, nothing pushed), nor the read of a variable, nor `checkFix`'s start state, so `ParSucc.succA_core`, `ParBody.body_simI` and the facts of `Steps/ParFacts.lean` still say what the checker does. Constructor fields passing through the state concern `.ctor`, not `S`.

**Checked by running the checker (`Steps/ParGuards.lean`, new; every `#guard` runs in the build).** The body's start state, as the checker RECORDS it at the body's entry (`Config.recordStates`): frame 0 empty; frame 1 `f ↦ .gfn f` at `Π(n : Nat). Nat` read `.pi .other`, marked (`recUid = some 0`), then `n ↦ σ0` at `Nat` read `.other`; `absTy = [Nat]` (readings `[.other]`), `nextAbs = 1`, no loan, goal `Nat`, typed, no refinement, one [Rec] context (`entries = [σ0]`, no candidate: not recursive). It is the same for two different bodies, and equals the hand-built `stF f` on every field the relation and the steps read. From it, the core body `S(#0)` gives `S(σ0) : Nat` and leaves `n ↦ ⊥`, no new unknown. `VarNumAInv` (lead13's, previously without a run) is now run at `σ0`, `S(σ0)`, `S(S(Z))`: typed and untyped, in tail position, under a handed-down reading, mode kept; in `clone`'s mode the read copies.

**Found: the surface's `S(n)` is a call.** The surface resolves `S` applied by call syntax to the built-in function `succFn = fix _ (n : Nat) : Nat := S(#0)` (`Surface.lean`; the core `S` is reached by numerals, `Term.ofNat`, and by `succFn`'s body), so `def I (n : Nat) : Nat := S(n)` written in the surface is the core `I(n) = succFn(n)`: the checker checks `succFn`'s body at its own generic call, then the call (probed: `[Def] _: goal Nat`, then `[Call-type] … (σ0) : Nat`). The columns are about core programs (`bodyC`, `bodyB` already wrote `S(Z)` as the core `.succ .zero`, which the surface writes `1`). So the column `I` (core body `S(#0)`) is the check of `succFn`'s body, the piece every surface `S(…)` uses; a column for the surface's `S(n)` needs the call column. `P`'s surface body resolves exactly to the core `match #0 { Z => Z, S _ => #0.0 }` (asserted). No change made; recorded so that no one reads "column `I`" as the surface program.

### D125. A match in tail position: the checker splits without a join, the reference joins (fourteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead14`; ruling ASKED)

**Found** while stating `P`'s body simulation (`def P (n : Nat) : Nat := match n { Z => Z, S m => m }`; `Regress/TailJoin.lean`, asserted by running the checker). In tail position the checker splits the unknown `σ0` (`checkTailAt`'s `.matchNat`, worlds `σ0 := Z` and `σ0 := S(σ1)`) and runs the return continuation at each arm's leaf with NO join ("split at the generic call, never closed off as a block"): leaf Z holds `n ↦ Z`, leaf S holds `n ↦ S(⊥)`. The reference (`runT`'s `.matchNat`) runs the arm `ρ 0` selects and then drops at the join in tail position too (`joinDrop K φ entry …`; only [Entry] is skipped in tail position, `matchEntry K φ (!tl)`). The join's moves for this match (`Decide.jdMoves`, which `Decisions.joinMoves` is aligned with) are `[n]`: the `S` arm moves `n.0`, a number's predecessor, which the join's grain takes whole. So at the leaf the reference holds `n ↦ moved` where the checker holds `Z` or `S(⊥)`: `Rel.sim` fails at `n`, and `Out` (every step's conclusion in `Steps/Form.lean`, the leaf's relation included) cannot be proved for any body whose tail is a split match that moves something in an arm. Results agree; the frame that differs is popped next on both sides (`checkFix`'s `popFrame`, `fixBodyAt`'s end).

**Options.** (A) the reference skips the join drop in tail position, as it already skips [Entry] there: the tail match of the reference is then the checker's split, arm for arm (model change in `runT`'s `.matchNat`/`.matchInd`; it changes no result: after a tail match the body's frame pops). (B) the checker joins in tail position (checker change; buys nothing). (C) the tail statement relates the leaf to the reference only off the top frame's places the join drops (a seam in the form). Recommended: (A), the uniform endpoint. Asked of the orchestrator; `P`'s body simulation waits for the ruling.

**Ruled (orchestrator): (A)**, the reference skips the join's drop at a tail-position match "as the checker does", the reference's tail flag to be true exactly where the checker runs without a join. **Found before building it (lead14; pinned by `#guard`s in `Regress/TailJoin.lean`): the checker does NOT run without a join in tail position in general.** It joins on two of its three tail paths: (1) `checkTailAt`'s `.matchNat`/`.matchInd` on a KNOWN scrutinee (`.zero`, `.succ _`, a constructor value with several constructors) runs the arm with `joinCont`, which drops the join's places before the continuation (`Machine.lean`, `joinCont`: `for q in drops do dropAtJoin …`); (2) the run of a callee's body (`runBodyCore`: `tailNext := true`, then `eval`) reaches `evalMatch … (tail := true)`, whose arms go through `joinEnds … tail`, which in tail position skips only [Entry] and still calls `joinDrop` (`if tail then return ← joinDrop fuel' mt run`). Only (3), the SPLIT of an unknown at the generic call (`checkTailAt`'s `.abs σ` case), has no join. The reference always runs on values, so today it agrees with (1) and (2) and differs from (3) alone; (A) as ruled would make it differ from (1) and (2). The checker's own tail position is therefore not one notion; the uniform endpoints are (B′) the split joins at each leaf as (1) does (`joinCont` around `k` in each world: a checker change, which makes `P`'s leaves `n ↦ moved` on both sides), or (A′) no tail-position join anywhere: (A) plus the checker's (1) and (2) dropping no join in tail position. The guards (derivation counts of `[Join]` and `[Split]`, all accepted): an unknown scrutinee in tail position splits with no join as a function's body (`T1`), a λ's body (`T4`), a match in an arm of a tail match (`T5`, four worlds), under `let` (`T6`) and under `;` (`T7`); a known scrutinee in tail position joins (`T2`: `let x = 1; match x {…}` drops `x`); a callee's run joins (`T3`: `T1(1)`); and `eval` of `P`'s match on a known `n` drops `n` in tail position, untyped (a callee's run) or typed, as out of it (`joinedK`). Not in tail position an unknown is closed off as a block (`T8`, no split) and a known one joins (`T9`). No pattern binding is pushed before a leaf (`S m => m` reads the place `n.0`). The reference's tail flag is threaded as the checker's tail position is (a `let`'s body, a `;`'s tail, an arm, a body run by `fixBodyAt`), so the flag is not where the two differ: the checker's tail position joins or not by whether the scrutinee is an unknown. NOT built; re-ruling asked. The ruling's point about (B) (a tail join taking whole a place an arm partly moved may end a borrow the arm returns) applies to (1) and (2) as they stand, so it bears on the checker too.

**Re-ruled (orchestrator): (B′), a CHECKER change; the reference stays exactly as it is.** The split of an unknown at the end of a body drops at the join like the other two paths (`joinCont` around the continuation in each world), landed on main as main's D119. Reason against (A′): it changes two checker paths and the model for no accepted program gained (a place the join drops is always in the frame popped next, so a result borrowing it cannot survive either way), and it makes the end of a body a special case of the match rule; (B′) leaves one rule for every match. Fallback to (A′) if the landing loses an accepted program that is not at fault. `P`'s body simulation waits until D119 is merged into the proof line. **Guards that flip when D119 arrives** (`Regress/TailJoin.lean`): `leaves` (leaf Z `n ↦ Z` and leaf S `n ↦ S(⊥)` become `n ↦ ⊥`, the join's drop of `n` before the continuation) and the "no join" counts of `T1`, `T4`, `T5`, `T6`, `T7` (each world's leaf drops what the join names: a `[Join]` per leaf where a place is dropped); the `[Split]` counts, `T2`, `T3`, `T8`, `T9`, `joinedK` and the pattern-binding guard stay.

### D126. `FrameDrop0` is the parameter-free case of the open frame property; the mirror that removes it (fourteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead14`; RULED: the mirror; stage 1 and column `I` built by model5, `spine-model5`)

**Asked**: prove `FrameDrop0` (`Steps/ParFacts.lean`: the reference run from `[{}, fr]` with no holder succeeds as from `[fr]`) or find where it fails and say what the reference's body frame would have to be. **Found.** It is the instance of `FrameProp.run_frame` (the model lane's one open simulation, `sorry`) at no parameter: `ks = []`, `σ = id`, `s₀` the start state with its empty frame 0, `s'` that state without it; every clause of `FrameRel` is immediate there except `lentTop`, which needs the in-place state's `lent` empty off live labels (`FrameDrop0` leaves `lent`, `heldTy`, `heldMem` free; the column's state, `SM'` of a state with no loan, has them empty). So it is exactly as open as `run_frame`'s parameter-free case. No failing state found (frame 0 is empty; every read of the run resolves in the top frame or through labels, of which there are none). A proof for `I`'s term alone would shift `readPlace` (`contentOf`, the access, `setContent` and its whole-state strict test) by one frame; not done. **The mirror** removes it: `fixBodyAt` pushes `self` and the parameters onto the creation state's TOP frame; pushing a fresh frame instead makes a global's body state with no borrow parameter `[{}, {self, params}]`, which is `SM'` of the checker's start state exactly (`checkFix`: `env := #[{}]`, `pushFrame`, captures, `self`, parameters). For every closure to match: captures in the pushed frame (today the base frame, `stateOf 0 (capsState …)`), and borrow parameters' cells as frame-0 bindings `x° ↦ loan l` at the content type, as `checkFix` makes them (today temporaries of the body frame; `bodyAt`'s finals, `callOf`, `FrameRel`'s "the closure has one frame" read them). Recommended: the mirror, first in the "fresh frame" form, which covers `I` and `P`. Meanwhile `Steps/ColumnIFix.lean` proves the reference `.fix` clause's reading of `I` (`runDoms … [.nat] = psI`, `codOf … .nat = outI`, the body's run independent of the assignment, the tail flag and the hint, `EndsOk []`), which both routes need.

**Ruled (orchestrator): the mirror.** The model takes the checker's shape: a closure's body runs in `[{borrow-parameter cells}, {captures, self, parameters}]`, the cells frame-0 bindings `x° ↦ loan l` at their content type, as `checkFix` makes them; `FrameDrop0` is deleted. Stage 1: a fresh frame for `self` and the parameters (covers `I` and `P`). Stage 2: the captures in the pushed frame and the cells in frame 0, together with D123's stage 2 (captures in closure values; `Regress/SelfCapture.R7`).

**The checker's shape, pinned first** (model5, `Steps/FrameGuards.lean`; every `#guard` runs in the build). The state the checker records on entry to the body, for a borrow parameter (`Sb (r : &Nat) : Unit`: frame 0 `r° ↦ loan 0` at `Nat`, frame 1 `Sb ↦ .gfn Sb` marked, then `r ↦ borrow 0 σ0` at `&Nat`), a recursive function (`Rc … by n`: a global's frames, the [Rec] candidate `0`), a λ with a capture (frame 0 empty; frame 1 the capture `κ ↦ σ0` flagged as a capture, the λ's own binding marked with its own [Rec] context, then `x ↦ σ1`), and a λ with a borrow parameter inside a function with one (frame 0 holds the λ's OWN cell only; the label and unknown counters run on). So the target is the checker's shape. Two details matter for the model: the λ's body starts from a FRESH environment (the enclosing frames are gone), and it runs the CLOSED term (`capture` renumbers the free variables to the captures).

**Stage 1, as built (model5, `spine-model5`).** The literal stage 1, an empty frame pushed on top of the creation state for `self` and the parameters, would have made every capture unreachable: the reference resolves a variable in the top frame only (`SState.varPos`), and `runT` is structural on the term, so it cannot run a renumbered body; every closure VALUE with a capture (`.val (.clo …)` runs its code in `stateOf 0 caps`, whose one frame is the captures) would have lost its meaning. Built instead: the body runs in the creation state UNDER a fresh bottom frame (`SState.under`: frame `f` becomes `f + 1`, positions by `Pos.up`, holders and lent sites moved with them, frame 0 new and empty), `self` and the parameters pushed on the top frame as before (`Run.lean`'s `.fix` `bodyAt`, `RunCongr.fixBodyAt`). For a global (formed in `[{}]`) the body state is `[{}, {self, params}]`, the ruled shape; for a closure value `[{}, {caps, self, params}]`, the checker's exactly; frame 0 is where stage 2 puts the cells. A λ formed inside a body keeps its creation frames below its own (the seam the reference cannot avoid without closing terms; the knot for λs owes it). `under` changes no meaning, holder or label, so the invariant `run_congr` carries holds under it (`Under.rinv_under`); the congruence lemmas start from it. Regressions moved to the new frame: `Regress/CallInPlace` (`Inc`, `Id1`), `Regress/FrameCheck`, `Strict/FrameCE`. `FrameRel` (the open frame property's relation) is restated for the closure state the reference now builds (two frames, the closure's frame on top); `run_frame` stays open. `Strict.fund_all`, `column_C_free`, `column_B_free` and `frag_run_free` keep their statements and the standard axioms.

**Column `I`, assembled with no model fact** (`Steps/ColumnIRun.lean`, standard axioms). `start_I`: `fixBodyAt`'s state for `I` at an argument `a` IS `SM'` of the checker's start state `stI` under `σ0 ↦ a`, every field (`env_SM`: `[{}, frI]`).
