So the body's run there is `body_runI`'s, and `FrameDrop0` is deleted (`Steps/ParFacts.lean`). `column_I_run`: the `.fix` clause run of `I` from the empty state succeeds at `Π(n : Nat). Nat`, leaves the state as it was, and the closure is a member of the type (`Witness.fixClo_mem_nonrec`, `run_fix_of_mem`). `column_I_ff`: the same at the checker's fuel-free decisions at the body run's end, for every successful typed run of the checker's `eval` on the body from a state with `stI`'s kernel in the plain mode; it ASSUMES 2 facts about the checker, `VarNumAInv` and `InvNumA` (read against the checker and judged true, unproved), and nothing about the model. `FixRunI` (stated, for the machine lane): where `checkDef defI` succeeds from the empty state, `checkFix` ran the body's typed `eval` at the goal `Nat` from such a state; `#guard`ed by `checkDef defI`'s derivation (the goal, the read of `n` moved, the return of `S(σ0) : Nat`, one path end, no split, no join). `column_I_def`: from `checkDef defI`'s acceptance; ASSUMES 3 facts about the checker, `FixRunI`, `VarNumAInv`, `InvNumA`.

The obstacle was the finals' argument (each cell stays ordered through the body's run, `OrdKeep`), stated for temporaries only; it now ranges over CELLS, `IsCell s c := IsTemp c ∨ c.frameOf < s.topIdx` (a place resolves in the top frame only, `resolve_top`), with no change to the run. `under` pads a frameless state with one frame so the closure's frame is always above frame 0. Not done: D123's second stage (a closure value's capture of the function being checked, at the context's type; `Regress/SelfCapture.R7`), and a column for a function with a borrow parameter (its start state should equal `SM'` of the recorded checker state, as `start_I` shows for `I`).

### D127. The first call, column `J` (`def J : Nat := I(Z)`): the checker at the call pinned, the step in the form proved, and the frame property does not apply at a call as stated (fifteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead15`)

**The program.** `I` as in column `I` (core body `S(#0)`), then `def J : Nat := I(Z)`, whose core body is `.call (.const "I") [.zero] false`, exactly what the surface `I(0)` resolves to (asserted). The column is about the call, not about `I`'s surface body (D124).

**What the checker does at the call** (`Steps/CallGuards.lean`, every `#guard` runs in the build; the callee's states by `Config.recordDeep`). `J`'s body starts from one empty frame with `I`'s global in Σ, no unknown, no label, goal `none` (the goal is the hint), not in tail position (`stJ`; the surface probe's recorded entry agrees). The head and the argument are evaluated typed and held as temporaries of frame 0 (main's D115); `callType` gives the call's type, `Nat` (the body's own type is discarded: the call's type is always `callType`'s). `runBodyCore` pushes the callee's frame ON TOP OF THE CALLER'S frames (not a fresh environment): the temporaries leave frame 0 as it binds them, `self` is `I ↦ .gfn I` at the declared Π-type read `.pi .other` and NOT marked (at the generic call it is marked), and the parameter is `n ↦ Z` LAZILY typed (`ty = none`, deferred type parameter 0 of `I`'s Π-type at `[Z]`; at the generic call it is stored at `Nat`). The body runs UNTYPED, in tail position, at call depth 1, with no hint; the read of `n` returns `Z` with NO type and moves it; `S` returns `S(Z)` at `Nat`. The frame pops and the call returns `S(Z)` at `Nat` with the state's kernel `stJ`'s again.

**What the reference does at the call.** `runT`'s `.call` clause never runs the callee's body: it runs the head (`.const "I"` gives the context's global), the argument, tests the borrow arguments (vacuous: no argument carries a label), and applies the head's MEANING (`callOf`).

**Built (`Steps/ColumnJ.lean`, standard axioms).** `callJ_sim`: the step for `I(Z)` in the form (`Sim`: from every reference state related to the checker's at `J`'s start and good, `Out`), at every context whose global `I` called at `0` returns `1` and keeps the state, with the decisions the checker's at `stJ`. `column_J_ff`: from the checker's typed runs of `I`'s body (its generic call) and of `J`'s body, the strict run of `J`'s body from `stJ` read as a reference state, with `I` meaning the closure the reference's `.fix` clause forms for it, succeeds with `1`, a member of `Nat`, at `Nat`, and the checker's value `S(Z)` means it. ASSUMES 3 facts about the checker: `CallJInv` (new: the checker's call returns `S(Z)` at `Nat` and keeps `stJ`'s kernel; guarded), `VarNumAInv`, `InvNumA`. `column_J_def`: from `checkDef`'s acceptance of `I` and of `J`; assumes `FixRunI` and `ConstRunJ` (new: `checkDef defJ` ran the body's typed `eval` from a state with `stJ`'s kernel) too. Reference lemmas, no assumption: `runT_bodyJ` (the strict run of `I(Z)` is `callOf` of the global at `0`), `callOf_unlabelled_state` (a call whose arguments and result carry no label keeps the state), `fixClo_I`, `fI_mem`, `callOf_fI` (`I`'s closure called at `0` gives `1`, by `Closure.closure_apply'` and column `I`'s body run `fixBodyAt_I`), `flags_bodyJ` (by kernel evaluation of the checker's decision).

**Where each conclusion comes from.** (1) That the strict run SUCCEEDS comes from the callee's meaning being a member of its function type (half (I) through the context, `GlobalsOk.mem`): a member applied at member arguments that reach a data codomain gives a result (`ApplyMem.app_mem'`, `closure_apply'`). The form lets the step use exactly that and nothing about the checker's run of the body. (2) `Out` asks the meaning of the checker's VALUE, computed by the checker's own untyped run of the body in place, to equal the reference's result, the meaning applied. Membership gives only that the result lies in `Nat`. In `J` the two are agreed by computing both: the meaning applied is `1` (`I`'s global means the closure the `.fix` clause forms, `GlobalsOk.fn`; column `I` runs its body at every number from the checker's typed generic run), and the checker's value is `S(Z)` (`CallJInv`). That does not generalise: a general call step must get the agreement from the untyped run simulated (`eval_sim` at `typed = false`, whose hypothesis `hok`, that the strict run in place succeeds, the membership supplies) against the reference's run of the body IN PLACE (`CallRef.runBodyRef`), and that run related to `callOf` by the frame property (`callOf_inPlace`, `FrameProp.run_frame`). (3) The type: `Nat` on both sides (`callType`; the code's codomain at `0`). (4) `Pre` and `Rel` after: the kernel is `stJ`'s again and the value in flight a number.

**Found: the frame property's relation never holds at the start of a call.** Since D126 the closure's body state and the in-place state have the same shape, and for `J` they are the same state but at one binding: in place (`CallRef.callFrame`, as the checker) the frames are `[{}, {I ↦ the global's meaning at Π(n : Nat). Nat, n ↦ 0}]` at counter 0; the closure's meaning runs from `[{}, {I ↦ stage 0 at the weakened Π(n : Nat). Unit, n ↦ 0}]` at counter 0 (`fixBodyAt`; column `I`'s `start_I`). So the instance of `run_frame` `J` would need is the parameter-free one with the identity renaming and an empty caller frame, and it does not apply: `FrameRel.top` asks every top-frame binding to agree (`BindRen`: the same type and the same meaning), which fails at `self` for EVERY call, recursive or not (in place `self` is the callee's own meaning at its declared type; in the closure it is the stage below at the weakened type). `run_frame`'s docstring names this ("the knot owes it"), but no statement owns it, and `callOf_inPlace` assumes it away. Options: (S1) restate `FrameRel` so that the top frame's binding 0 (`self`) is exempt, asking only that each side's `self` be a good member of its own stored type (the strict whole-state tests read it), with the hypothesis that the body reads `self` only in recursive calls; a non-recursive body never reads it (the checker rejects every read of a non-recursive function's own binding, D31: read whole, called, or bound by a `let`, pinned in `Steps/CallGuards.lean`), and a recursive one is the knot's (`fixStage`, [Rec]); (S2) prove each call column as `J` is proved, by computing both sides: no frame property, but a fact about the checker per call and no general step; (S3) make the reference's in-place frame bind the stage as the closure does: then the relation between the checker's in-place state (`I ↦ .gfn I`, meaning the global) and the reference's fails at `self` instead. Recommended: (S1). Ruling asked.

**Ruled (orchestrator): (S1), refined.** The frame relation does not ask equality at the function's binding of itself; it relates the two sides there by a clause of their own, `SelfRel`. Why not (S3) or any reading that makes the two equal: under recursion the checker's nested in-place frames all hold the same name, while the closure's stages descend one per call, and no reading of a binding from the state and one context gives stage k−1 in the outer frame and k−2 in the inner; equality there is wrong in principle, and the relation there is the recursion argument's seam. (S2) is not a step. The form: (1) the clause is keyed on the function's binding of itself as the construction places it (the checker marks it at the generic call, D78/D123; in a frame of `{captures, self, parameters}` its position is the number of captures), not on "binding 0"; (2) `SelfRel`: each side's value is a good member of its own stored type (the whole-state strict tests read it), and nothing else for now; (3) the frame property is stated, for now, for bodies in which the function's own name does not occur, a syntactic class that holds of every non-recursive function (the checker rejects every other use of the own name, D31, `Steps/CallGuards.lean`; apart from a λ's capture of it); it is NOT stated or assumed that a body "reads it only in recursive calls". **Planned extension, not built:** for a recursive function `SelfRel` gains "the two agree when applied to arguments below the current one", supplied by the outer induction on the decreasing argument. Next, in order: the untyped body step in place on `J`; the smallest call from a non-empty caller state, `J2 := (let x = S(Z); I(x))`, with exactly the instance of the frame property its step needs stated as one named model fact and `J2` proved assuming it; every theorem reported with its checker facts and model facts counted separately.

**Entry conditions the general call step meets (none affects `J` as proved).** The callee's parameter is lazily typed in place, so the untyped read of it needs a fact `VarNumAInv` does not cover (it asks `b.ty.isSome`); `CallJInv`'s proof needs it. The checker's untyped run hands the body no hint (unless `needsCtx`), where the reference's in-place run and the closure's run hand it the codomain: hint independence (`HintIndep.runT_hintIndep`) bridges for tail-hint-free bodies, `S(#0)` among them. The relation reads `n`'s type through `codeMeaning.paramTy` (the lazily typed binding's deferred type), which gives `Nat`, as `callFrame` stores it. `Pre` at `J`'s start for a concrete context needs `GlobalsOk.fn` for `I`, the context's meaning equal to the `.clo` meaning at EVERY assignment and future: closed code's meaning independent of both, which no lemma gives yet (D110's frame locality); so `column_J_ff` is proved directly, not through `callJ_sim`.

### D128. The untyped body step in place fits the form; the frame property's instance at `J2`, stated as one model fact (fifteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead15`)

**The untyped body step in place, proved in the form** (`Steps/CallBody.lean`, standard axioms). `bodyLazy_sim`: `Column.EvalSim` at `typed = false` for `I`'s body `S(#0)` from every callee state whose parameter is LAZILY typed (`ty = none`, as `runBodyCore` binds it) and holds a closed numeral read `Nat` by the context (`Env.hintAt` through `codeMeaning.paramTy`): the strict run succeeds with `S` of the numeral at `Nat`, and `Pre` and `Rel` hold after, the parameter moved or kept as the aligned copy decision says. Nat`, then `n ↦ a` at `Nat`, as `CallRef.callFrame`) and from `closureJ2 a` (`[{}, {I ↦ stage 0 at Π(n : Nat). Unit, n ↦ a}]` at counter 0, as the `.fix` clause's `fixBodyAt`, column `I`'s `start_I`) satisfy `FrameAgreeS` at the renaming `l ↦ c + l`, `c = s₂.nextLoan`, with no parameter labels (`ks = []`, no cells). The body holds no occurrence of `I`'s own name. **Check on the model's side** (kernel-checked): `frameRelS_start`, the relation holds between `J2`'s two states whenever `f` is a member of `Π(n : Nat). Nat` and the caller's holders and lent sites are its own positions; so the fact's hypothesis is met where `J2` uses it. The checker's side is the `#guard`s above.

**Proved toward it:** `closureRun_of_checker` (steps (3) and (4) below compose: from the callee state related to `inPlaceJ2` and `FrameJ2`, the closure's run of the body succeeds with the meaning of the checker's untyped value; checker facts `BodyLazyInv`, `InvNumA`; model fact `FrameJ2`; the callee state's `Pre`, `Rel`, `SGood`, `FrameStart`, `FrameRelS` as hypotheses). ### D129. The frame property: false as restated by D113 (a dead label read through the renaming); restated over `FrameRelS` with a locality clause; proved on the label-free fragment; `FrameJ2` proved (model6, 2026-11-21; `spine-model6`)

**Tried to break it first.** The candidates the lead named, read against `runT` clause by clause, with no parameter label (`ks = []`, `J2`'s case): (1) the whole-state tests (`StateOk` after a write or a borrow at a field path, `EndOk`, `AccessOk`, `setContent`'s definedness over typed futures) read the caller's frames in place; with no parameter label the run never changes the caller's frames (it reaches them only through parameter labels), the caller's labels have no site in the callee's frame (`FrameStart`), so [Access] at a callee position never ends one, and the in-place state good at the call (`SGood`) answers every later test on the caller's frames (they read only labels below the call's counter, whose typed futures do not change). (2) [Access] runs as many rounds as there are live labels, more in place (the caller's): the extra rounds end nothing. (3) The label counters (`l ↦ c + l`): fresh labels are issued in the same order on both sides, and `σ` is monotone there, so every label-ordered fold (access rounds, the pop) ends the same labels in the same order. (4) A caller state holding a live borrow: the typed future gives its label its record in place only; no callee position reads it. (5) A borrow parameter whose lender is below / a returned borrow passed in: the parameter labels get no site in the callee's frame on either side (in the closure none at all), so [Access] never ends one; their ends' order (the pop) does not matter because no parameter's holder content reads another parameter's label; the parameters' records `heldMem` still differ when the caller wrote through the borrow before the call (D113's open note: no run reads them, the clause should not ask them). **Found, and kernel-checked: `FrameProp.run_frame` as restated by D113 is FALSE** (`Strict/FrameLocCE.run_frame_loc_false`, at every data model, the context `Kw`). `FrameRel` compares a callee-frame value with the closure's as functions of the future through the renaming (`∀ ψ, v ψ = v' (renF σ ψ)`), but the run reads a value at ONE future, the state's typed future, which gives a label no position holds the run's future itself: in the closure `φ l`, in place `φ (σ l)`. The counterexample: one variable `x : Nat` above an empty frame, reading label `0` in the closure (counter `1`) and label `5` in place (counter `6`), `σ l = l + 5`, nothing live; at the future giving `5` the number `1` and every other label `0`, reading `x` gives `1` in place and `0` in the closure, both runs succeeding; `FrameRel`, `FrameStart` and the in-place state's `SGood` all hold (`SGood.fresh` bounds the labels read by the counter only). No call makes such a state: every binding of a callee's frame starts future-free (`callFrame`, `bodyAt`), and a run keeps every position's dependence within its live labels (`Locality.Inv1`; an end substitutes the label away). **Changed:** the false `sorry` theorem `FrameProp.run_frame` (used by no proof) is deleted, its docstring replaced by the finding; the restated property is `Steps/FrameFrag.RunFrameS`, a named proposition (no `sorry`): over `FrameRelS`, the function's own binding not read by the body (its de Bruijn index in the closure's top frame not free), with the LOCALITY CLAUSE (the closure's positions read only its live labels; its top frame none of the parameters' labels), the in-place state good and `FrameStart` met. `run_frame_obs` and `run_counter_indep` keep their `sorry` (both untouched).

**Proved: the frame property on the label-free fragment** (`Steps/FrameFrag.lean`, standard axioms; ASSUMES no fact: of the decision table only that it invalidates nothing, and only where the term assigns, which the fuel-free table does by definition). `LFree a n t`: `Z`, `S(u)`, a read of a variable, `x := u` at a variable (where `a`), `let x = u; w`, `u; w`, over `n` variables after the function's own binding; every fragment term (`FTy`) with no `&` and no borrow-typed variable is one. `run_frameS_lf`: from `FrameRelS`-related states (no parameter label), the closure's state holding no borrow and lending nothing, its top frame's other bindings and its temporaries future-free with no label, a label-free term runs, at ANY tail flags and hints on the two sides, to the same result and `FrameRelS`-related states, or fails on both (`FrameAgreeS`). It needs neither `FundCtx`, nor `FrameStart`, nor the in-place state good: the label-free run makes, ends and accesses no label in the callee's frame and reaches no whole-state test (its places are whole variables, where `lentOk` holds at `[]`). The proof is one lemma per clause of `runT` over an internal relation `LF` (the two top frames EQUAL but at the function's own binding: with no label every other binding is future-free, so "related through `σ`" is equality; the caller's frames and every record of the in-place state as at the call; the closure's state with no holder and no site), read off `FrameRelS` by `lf_start` and given back by `lf_end`; the combinators (`agree_erasedIf`, `agree_strictS`, `agree_guarded`, `agree_bind`, `agree_smBind`), the frame operations (`lf_setAt`, `lf_pushBind`, `lf_dropLast`, `lf_scope`) and the reads (`access_self`, `accessOk_self`, `setContent_nil`, `lf_contentOf`, `lf_readPlace`, `lf_placeTy`, `lf_assignAt`, `lf_letEnd`) are general enough for the labelled clauses to reuse.

**`FrameJ2` PROVED** (`frameJ2_holds`, standard axioms, no assumption: `I`'s body `S(#0)` is `LFree false 1`). So `closureRun_of_checker_free` (steps (3)+(4) of D128's plan) assumes 2 facts about the checker, `BodyLazyInv` and `InvNumA`, and NO model fact. `FrameJ2`'s hypotheses `FundCtx`, `FrameStart` and `SGood` are not used (they may be dropped from its statement).

**What the general `RunFrameS` still needs, clause by clause** (the docstring of `RunFrameS` keeps the list): `&x`, and `x := u`/`*y := u` with a label: [End] and [Access] on related states, the typed futures related through `σ` by the locality clause, the strict end tests equivalent; `let`/`;` ending a held borrow: the same; a field path: `StateOk`, the caller's frames by `SGood` and `FrameStart`, the closure's by the relation; `match`: `movedPlaces` (top frame at `lentFuture`), the entry, the join's drop; `.fix` in the body: the inner closure's body states have one more frame than `FrameRel.frames` allows, and its meaning's equality needs its finals to read only the returned label (locality again); `.call`: `callOf` on related states; `Id` with parameters: `run_frame_obs`. **Changed (`FrameRel.held`, as D113 said):** the records `heldMem` are asked to agree off the parameter labels only, and the held types `heldTy` at every live label of the frame, the parameters' included (a parameter's record is in place the content when the caller's borrow was made, in the closure the argument, and they differ whenever the caller wrote through the borrow before the call; no run reads a parameter's record; the strict ends read every held type, and at the call the parameters' agree by `BorrowArgsTyped`). `Regress/FrameCheck` re-proved (two `rfl`s).

### D130. Frame locality of a global's meaning: `GlobalsOk.fn` for the global `I` at `J`'s start, proved (model6, 2026-11-21; `spine-model6`)

**What the start state of a program with an earlier global needs.** `Pre` at `J`'s (or `J2`'s) start asks `GlobalsOk` of the context, whose `fn` clause asks the context's ONE meaning of `I` to be the closure the `.fix` clause forms for `I`'s code AT EVERY assignment and future, the clause's type `I`'s type. Three facts make it hold: (1) frame locality of closed code in the assignment and the future: a closure with no capture whose code holds no loan and no unknown means the same at every assignment and future (`Steps/GlobalsLocal.meanV_clo_closed`, for every such code, from the run's proved locality lemmas `meanV_congr` and `meanV_congrA`; the globals must carry no label); (2) `I`'s closure does not read the context's own entry for `I` (`clo_I_ctx`, by computation: `I`'s code reads no global), so `J`'s context with `I` meaning `fJ` (the closure at any one assignment and future) is a fixed point; (3) the clause's type: three decisions of the table at the empty scope (`I`'s `fix` not a proof, its Π-type at sort 1, its parameter read `.other`), which every table aligned with the checker at `stJ` makes (`fixDecJ`, by evaluating the checker's decision functions at `stJ`). **Proved:** `fn_clause_I_tab`: the `fn` clause of `GlobalsOk` for every state whose globals are `[I]`, in the context `KJ dm D (fJ dm D)`, at every table `D` aligned with the checker at `stJ`; standard axioms, NO assumed fact. **Also proved (same file):** `fn_clause_I_tabI` (the same `fn` clause at every table aligned at `I`'s generic call, `stI`); `fJ_eq_fI` (at such a table, where the checker ran `I`'s body, `fJ` IS column `I`'s closure `fI` at every assignment and future) and so `fJ_mem` (`GlobalsOk.mem`'s content for `I`: `fJ` a member of `Π(n : Nat). Nat`); `callOf_fI_at` (`I`'s meaning called at any number `a` gives `a + 1`, the state as it was: `ColumnJ.callOf_fI` generalised, for `J2`'s call at `1`, D128's step (2)). These three assume what `ColumnJ.fI_mem` does: the checker facts `VarNumAInv`, `InvNumA` (and the machine lane's proved `EvalInv`, `SuccInv`, `InvSteps`, `EvalLow`). **Not done:** one table for both: `J`'s body is run under a table aligned at `stJ` (`flags_bodyJ`), while `fJ_mem` is at a table aligned at `stI`; the two states have the same globals but differ in the unknowns' readings (`absDecl`), so `DecTab.of_core` does not carry one to the other; either the decisions `I`'s body reads are evaluated at `stJ` (redo `fixBodyAt_I`'s decision facts there, as `fixDecJ` does for the empty scope) or the column takes a table aligned at both. The trivial clauses (`ty`, `const`, `memBelow`) are not written.

### D131. The first split, column `P`: the checker's worlds pinned; the form says a split as it stands, with a tail wrapper; the split's step proved assuming one checker fact (sixteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead16`)

**The program.** `def P (n : Nat) : Nat := match n { Z => Z, S m => m }`, core body `match #0 { Z => Z, S _ => #0.0 }` (`ParGuards.bodyP`). `checkFix` checks it once, in tail position (`checkTail`), from the generic state `stP` (`ColumnI.stI` with `P`'s name and global). The scrutinee holds the unknown `σ0`, so the match SPLITS.

**What the checker does at the split** (`Steps/SplitGuards.lean`, every `#guard` runs the checker; since main's D119 is in this line). Two worlds, `Z` first. World `Z`: `refine` puts `Z` at `n` and records `0 := Z`; the arm reads nothing; the join drops `n` (`joinCont`); the continuation `k` runs on `Z : Nat` from exactly `leafZ` (`n ↦ ⊥`, `refs = [0 := Z]`). World `S`: a fresh `σ1 : Nat` read `.other`, `refine` puts `S(σ1)` at `n` and records `0 := S(σ1)`; the arm's read of the place `n.0` MOVES it (`n ↦ S(⊥)`, recorded by the checker at the arm's exit), the value `σ1 : Nat`; the join drops `n`; `k` runs on `σ1 : Nat` from exactly `leafS` (`n ↦ ⊥`, `Δ = [Nat, Nat]`, `refs = [0 := S(σ1)]`). After both worlds the snapshot is back (`n ↦ σ0`, no refinement) with the counters and `σ1`'s type kept (`endP`). The run fails where `k` fails in either world. Entry conditions: the leaves do not move with the tail flags, the typing flag or a handed-down reading; in `clone`'s mode (or in place) the arm's read COPIES `n.0`, but the join drops `n` whole after it, so the leaves are the same. **`σ1` is world `S`'s fresh unknown only when world `Z`'s continuation names no unknown and issues no label**: a world's exit keeps the counters (`exitWorld`), so a `k` that makes an unknown in world `Z` moves world `S`'s to `σ2` (guarded). Hence the condition `KeepsAtZ k`.

**The form says a split as it stands; what is added is a wrapper for tail position** (`Steps/FormTail.lean`). `Out` and `Ext` are unchanged. A checker run in tail position returns nothing: it hands each path's value to its continuation at that path's leaf. So `TailOut K ρ φ π st s x k`: SOME leaf `stl` of the checker's run where `k` ran successfully on the leaf's value `v` at its type `T`, with `Out K ρ φ π st stl s x v (some T)`; and `TailSim` the statement from every related good reference state, the reference running the term in tail position at the goal's hint (as `fixBodyAt` runs a body). What `Ext`/`Out` say about the world, exactly: `Ext`'s `ρ'` agrees with `ρ` below the start state's unknowns and gives the fresh unknown its value (in world `S`, `σ1 ↦ a` where `ρ σ0 = a + 1`); `Ext`'s `Δ'` gives it its type (`σ1 : Nat`, a real extension: the start context's `Δ` is free there); the leaf's `Pre` asks the world's refinement of `ρ'` (`TypedAssignment.refs`: `ρ' σ0 = S(ρ' σ1)`), which is what makes the leaf the one `ρ` selects; the reference's run stays at the starting `ρ` (it reads no unknown). No change to the form was needed.

**Proved (standard axioms).** `Steps/ColumnPRun.lean`: the reference's run of the body from every state that reads like one related to `stP` (`RefP`: no live label, `n` at `(1, 1)` holding the number `ρ 0` at `Nat`, the top frame read `[.other, .pi .other]`), at a table deciding as the checker does (`ParDecP`: the body and the arm's read not erased, `n` and `n.0` moved, the join's moves `[n]` from an empty entry): on `0` the result `0 : Nat` and `n` moved; on `a + 1` the result `a : Nat`, the arm's write of `moved` at `n.0` (`setContent` at `[.0]`) then the join's at `n` (`runP`; the join's ends strict-OK, `joinP`; the entry set empty, `RefP.movedPlaces`). `Steps/ColumnPSplit.lean`: `refP_of_rel` (every reference state related to `stP` reads as `RefP`); `rel_transfer` (the relation across a change of the checker's state, the assignment and `Δ` that keeps the skeleton, the meaning, the counter, the labels and the sites: the refinement, under an assignment on its path condition); `rel_leafZ`, `rel_leafS` (the relation follows the checker through the refinement, the arm's move of `n.0` and the join's drop of `n`, by `botWrite_rel`, the second time at the path `[.0]`); `pre_lzT`, `pre_lsT` (`Pre` at each leaf with its value in flight, under the extended assignment and `Δ`: `Inv` by `inv_envT`, no checker fact); **`split_outP` / `split_simP`: `TailSim` for `P`'s body from every state with `stP`'s kernel in the plain mode, with any continuation that keeps the counters in world `Z`, in the context `KP` (any `Δ` meeting `Pre`). ASSUMES 1 fact about the checker, `SplitPInv` (stated in `Steps/SplitP.lean`, guarded in `Steps/SplitGuards.lean`: the leaves' kernels `leafZ`/`leafS` and the continuation's runs there), and NO model fact.** The general `RefP` route means the step holds from every related reference state, not only the start state read as one.

**The column, assembled** (`Steps/ColumnP.lean`, standard axioms; the template is column `I`). The decisions are one table per check: the fuel-free table at the body's start state (`checkerDecisionsFF st`), whose answers the reference's run of `P` reads are computed by kernel evaluation of the checker's decision procedures at `stP` (`parDecP`: the body and `n.0` not erased, `n.0` and `n` moved, the join's moves `[n]`; `fixDecP`: the `.fix` clause's readings at the empty scope). Start bundles at `stP` with no checker fact (`pre_stP`, `rel_stP`). `body_runP`: from the checker's ONE successful `checkTail` of the body (in which `σ0` is an unknown), for every number `a`, the reference's run of the body from the checker's start state read under `σ0 ↦ a` succeeds (through `split_outP`, the world `a` falls in) with `a - 1` at `Nat` and no live label (the result identified by computing the reference run, `runP`). `start_P` (`fixBodyAt`'s state is that start state, as `start_I`), `fixBodyAt_P`, `column_P_run` (the closure a member of `Π(n : Nat). Nat`, the `.fix` run succeeds). **`column_P_ff`** (for every successful `checkTail` of the body from a state with `stP`'s kernel in the plain mode, with a continuation keeping the counters in world `Z`): ASSUMES 1 fact about the checker, `SplitPInv`; no model fact. **`column_P_def`** (from `checkDef defP`'s acceptance): ASSUMES 2 facts about the checker, `FixRunP` (new: the acceptance inverted to the body's `checkTail` from `stP`'s kernel with a continuation keeping the counters in world `Z`; guarded by `checkDef defP`'s derivation: goal, world `Z`, its join and return, world `S` with fresh `σ1`, the read, the join, the return) and `SplitPInv`; no model fact. As with `I` and `J`, the reference's run of this closed body at a start state is also computable outright (`runP` needs only the decisions), so the column's conclusion would hold with no checker fact at all; what the simulation adds is the relation at the world's leaf (`Pre` and `Rel` there), which is what a body that continues after the match needs.

### D132. `J2`'s call: the relation at the callee state, and steps (3)+(4) with only what they use (sixteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead16`)

**Built (`Steps/CallJ2.lean`, standard axioms, no assumed fact).** Glue (c) of D128's plan, its relation part: `stCJ2`, the kernel of the checker's callee state at `J2`'s call (`[{x ↦ ⊥ : Nat}, {I ↦ .gfn I : Π(n : Nat). Nat` unmarked`, n ↦ S(Z)` lazily typed at `I`'s Π-type`}]`, `#guard`ed against the state the checker records at the body's entry); **`rel_calleeJ2`**: from every reference state `s₂` related to the caller after the argument's read (`stX`: `x` moved), `stCJ2` is related to `inPlaceJ2 s₂ f 1` (the callee frame pushed, as `CallRef.callFrame`), at the same renaming, at every context whose global `I` means `f` at `Π(n : Nat). Nat` and which reads `I`'s declared type and the deferred parameter type as the reference's frame stores them; the two readings discharged: `hpi_of_globals` (from `GlobalsOk.ty`, `I` not being checked) and `hint_nLazy` (`codeMeaning.paramTy` at `I`'s Π-type reads `Nat`, from the table's `paramDecls [] [.nat] = [.other]`, which `fixDecJ`/`fixDecI` give). **`closureRun_J2`**: `FrameFrag.closureRun_of_checker_free` without `FundCtx`, the in-place state's `SGood` and `FrameStart`, which the label-free frame property does not use (D129): so `J2`'s step needs none of those three at the callee state. Assumes `BodyLazyInv`, `InvNumA` (checker); no model fact.

**The one-table question, at the call (a refinement of D128's plan, not built).** The result agreement needs no second table: take `I`'s meaning as the closure `J2`'s OWN context forms (`GlobalsOk.fn`: `f` is `meanV K (.clo [] fixI [])`, the `.fix` clause run under `J2`'s context and table), not column `I`'s `fI` (formed under `I`'s table). Then `callOf f [1]` is the closure's body run from `fixBodyAt`'s state under `J2`'s context, which is `closureJ2 1` (as `start_I`, at the decisions `fixDecJ` gives at `stJ`), the very run `closureRun_J2` relates to the checker's untyped value. What `closure_apply'` asks besides (the function total at member arguments under `J2`'s table) is a reference computation at `J2`'s decisions for `S(#0)` at the scope `[.other, .pi .other]`, which kernel evaluation of the checker's procedures at `scopeState stJ2 RI` gives (as `parDecI` at `stI`): no checker fact. Only the membership of `I`'s meaning, used for the `SelfRel` clause of `frameRelS_start`, still meets two tables (D130's `fJ_mem` is at `I`'s); for `J2` it can instead come from the same totality under `J2`'s table (`closure_mem_of_total'`).

**Also built:** `pre_stCJ2` (`Pre` at the callee state, from the caller's `CtxS`; `Inv` with the lazily typed binding, `inv_stCJ2`; no unknown), `lazyNum_stCJ2` (`LazyNum` at every state with `stCJ2`'s kernel and no handed-down reading, at a table reading `Nat` as `.other` at the empty scope). **Found: the table at the callee body's end.** `closureRun_J2` (through `bodyLazy_out` and `BodyLazyInv`) asks a whole `DecTab K.D st'` at the END state of the callee body's untyped run. With one table per check (`J2`'s, aligned at `stJ2`), that does not follow by `DecTab.of_core`: the callee's states have a different top frame (`DecideScope.liveVal` reads the top frame's readings: `[]` at `J2`'s start, `[.other, .pi .other]` in the callee). Per the ruling, the agreement should be stated narrowly, as a fact about the checker's decisions at exactly the class used. Proposed (not done, a change to a stated fact, the lead's call): `BodyLazyInv` takes, instead of `DecTab D st'`, only the decisions its conclusion names (the copy decision of `#0` at the callee's readings, the flags of `S(#0)` there), which kernel evaluation gives at `J2`'s table as `parDecI` does at `I`'s. **Also built: the reference's side of the call** (no assumed fact): `runT_callJ2` (the strict run of `I(x)` from every state where `x` holds the number `a` at `Nat` and nothing is live or held is `callOf` of the global at `a` from the state with `x` moved, `moveX`; at a table deciding the call and the read as neither a statement nor a proof and `x` moved); `caller_of_rel` (every reference state related to the caller `stX1 = [{x ↦ S(Z)}]` meets those hypotheses with `a = 1`, and `moveX` of it is related to `stX`, by `botWrite_rel`). **Stated: `CallJ2Inv`** (a fact about the checker, for the machine lane; `Steps/CallJ2.lean`): every successful typed `eval` of `I(x)` from `stX1`'s kernel, in the plain mode, not in tail position, no reading handed down, outside any private copy, returns its value at `Nat` and ends with `stX`'s kernel, having run `I`'s body untyped with no hint from a state with `stCJ2`'s kernel (plain mode, no handed-down reading) to that same value; `#guard`ed at three hints, typed or not, either tail flag before (the value `S(S(Z))`, the end kernel, the body's entry kernel, the body's exit value). **Still to build for `J2`'s step** (in order): the table at the callee's end (ruling asked, above); `callOf` of `J2`'s own closure as above; `frameRelS_start`'s two hypotheses at `moveX s` (`f` a member; every lent site of `s₂` below the callee's frame, for EVERY label: `Rel` constrains only live labels' sites, so this needs `SGood` or a restatement of `frameRelS_start` to live labels); the `let` around the call (the fragment's `let` case reused with a non-fragment body).

### D133. `BodyLazyInv` narrowed to the one decision it reads; the other stated checker facts take no table (seventeenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead17`; the ruling on D132)

**Ruled (orchestrator), on D132's finding:** a stated fact about the checker takes only the decisions its conclusion names, never a whole `DecTab` at a state, and those decisions are got at the caller's one table by kernel evaluation. **Built.** `CallBody.CopyDec D st p` is the one decision a read of `p` makes, at the state it is made in: wherever the checker's `Machine.copyRead fuel p` answers at `st` (`declCopy` of `placeDecl`'s reading at the top frame's readings), `D.copy (D.placeDecl (topReadings st) p)` answers the same. `BodyLazyInv` takes `CopyDec D st (.var 0)` at the body's START state in place of `DecTab D st'` at its end; its conclusion is unchanged (it names only that decision). The body's flags are not in the fact (the checker reads its own); they stay the step's hypotheses on the reference side (`ParDecI`, `hfl`), as before. The narrowed fact is stronger as a statement and easier to prove: its proof reaches `copyRead` at the start state (up to `tailNext`, which no decision reads) and uses the hypothesis directly, where the old form needed `DecTab.back` first. Guarded: `copyRead` of `#0` at the callee states at `Z` and `S(Z)`, either tail flag, answers `false` (a move). Consumers: `bodyLazy_out`, `FrameJ2.closureRun_of_checker`, `FrameFrag.closureRun_of_checker_free`, `CallJ2.closureRun_J2` take `hcd : CopyDec K.D st (.var 0)` for `hD : DecTab K.D st'`; `bodyLazy_sim` keeps its statement (the form's `DecTab` at the end gives the decision at the start: `DecTab.of_rest`, then `copyDec_of_decTab`). **At `J2`'s one table** (aligned at `stJ2`), by kernel evaluation of the checker's procedures at `scopeState stJ2 [.other, .pi .other]` and at `stJ2`, no checker fact: `CallJ2.parDecJ2` (`ParDecI` for `I`'s body in place), `flags_bodyI_J2`, `copyDec_calleeJ2` (at every state with `stCJ2`'s kernel the read's decision is a move, on both sides; `declCopy_other`: `.other` is never a copy type). So the table question at the callee (D132) is closed: `closureRun_J2` now needs nothing of `J2`'s table that `J2`'s table does not give.

**The test applied to the other stated facts.** `CallJInv`, `ConstRunJ`, `CallJ2Inv`, `SplitPInv`, `FixRunP` take no table at all (each speaks of the checker's own run): nothing to narrow. `VarNumAInv` (column `I`, read and judged true, not in the list ruled on) has `BodyLazyInv`'s old pattern (`DecTab D st'` at the end, reading only the read's copy decision); it is left as it is, since the machine lane may be mid-proof on it, and the same narrowing (`CopyDec D st (.var i)`) is recommended when it is next touched.

### D134. The first recursive function, column `R`: the checker at the self-call pinned; the recursive call's step fits the form at the stage's context (seventeenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead17`)

**The program.** `def R (n : Nat) : Nat by n := match n { Z => Z, S m => R(m) }`, the smallest function the checker accepts that calls itself; core body `match #0 { Z => Z, S _ => #1(#0.0) }` (`ColumnR.bodyR`; `#1` is the function's binding of itself). `S(R(m))` adds only the successor around the call (the surface's `S(…)` is moreover a call of `succFn`, D124); taken next if wanted.

**What the checker does** (`Steps/RecGuards.lean`, every `#guard` runs the checker). `checkDef` accepts `R` with `by n` and rejects it without ("calls itself but declares no decreasing parameter"), and rejects `R(S(m))` and `R(n)` in the arm. The body's start state (recorded by the checker for the surface program) has `stP`'s kernel with `R`'s name and global (`stR`): the function's binding of itself `R ↦ .gfn R` at `Π(n : Nat). Nat` read `.pi .other`, MARKED (`recUid = some 0`), then `n ↦ σ0`; and one [Rec] context `{fn = .gfn R, entries = [σ0], cands = [0], uid = 0}`. The match splits as `P`'s (D131). World `Z`: leaf `leafZR` with `Z : Nat`. World `S`: `σ1 : Nat`, `n ↦ S(σ1)`, the record `0 := S(σ1)`; the arm is the call `R(n.0)`: its head is read in place (the binding of itself is not consumed); the argument's read moves `n.0`; [Call-type] gives `Nat`; **the recursion check** (`Machine.recCheck`, typed runs only, after [Call-type], before the callee runs) selects the [Rec] contexts the call belongs to by the head binding's mark (or the head value), keeps each decreasing candidate whose argument is a strict subterm of the entry unknown as the records expand it (`σ1` in `strictSubterms(S(σ1))`), and rejects when none was declared or none survives (guarded: the entry `σ0` itself, `S(σ1)`, and `σ1` before the refinement are rejected; with no [Rec] context for `R` nothing is checked; an untyped call does not check); **then the callee's body runs untyped on `σ1`, its match on the unknown is stuck, and the call closes off**: the value is the sealed program `⌈R(σ1)⌉` (`.sealed (.call (.val (.gfn "R")) [.val (.abs 1)] true)`) at `Nat`; no new unknown, no label, the [Rec] context kept; the state is the arm's start with `n.0` moved (`armSR'`). The join drops `n`; the leaf is `leafSR` with `⌈R(σ1)⌉ : Nat`. Entry conditions: the leaves do not move with the tail flags or a handed-down reading, nor without a [Rec] context; the run fails where the continuation fails in either world.

**What the reference does.** The `.fix` clause builds the closure by stages (`RunCongr.fixStage`): at an argument of rank `r` the closure is stage `r + 1`, whose body runs with `self` bound to stage `b`'s closure at the WEAKENED type (`Nat` at arguments of rank below `b`, `Unit` above; `stageFn`, `weakCod`). In world `S` its body reads `R` from the frame in place, reads `n.0` (moving it) to `m = a - 1`, and applies the closure (`callOf`). It never runs a sealed program; the checker's value `⌈R(σ1)⌉` means the run of its term from the empty state (`meanV`'s `.sealed` clause), which reads `R` through the context, `K.C.global "R"`, and applies it to `ρ σ1`.

**The step for the recursive call, in the form as it stands (no change of form).**
1. *Where the reference's value comes from*: the stage below at the smaller argument. The context of the body's check at stage `b + 1` is `K_b`: `cur = some ("R", b)`, `C.global "R"` = the stage-`b` closure at the weakened type. Then `Pre` already holds the outer induction hypothesis: `GlobalsOk.memBelow` asks `MemBelow K_b (K_b.C.global "R") gR b`, whose content is "stage `b` is defined and good at member arguments of rank below `b`" (the knot's step, `FixKnot.stages_below`, `knot_member`); and `FixKnot.memBelow_apply` gives the call's result at `m` from it. So the reference's call succeeds and its result is good from `Pre`, not from anything about the checker.
2. *What `Ext` gives the call's result*: nothing. The call makes no unknown: the checker's value is a sealed program, not an unknown, and its meaning is computed (the run of `R(σ1)` from the empty state under `K_b`: `callOf (K_b.C.global "R") [ρ' σ1]`), the same closure at the same argument as the reference's call, so `Out`'s value agreement is the same `memBelow_apply` result read twice. The one unknown in world `S` is the split's `σ1` (`ρ' σ1 = m`, `Δ' σ1 = Nat`, as in D131).
3. *What the relation asks at the function's own binding*: equality, as at `I` and `P` (D123's `refTy` reads the marked binding at the context's type). It holds because `K_b.C.global "R"` IS what `fixBodyAt` binds for `self` at stage `b + 1` (closure and weakened type), and the call reads it in place, so the binding is unchanged; `SGood` asks it to be a member of its weakened type, `memBelow_mem`. D127's `SelfRel` (the in-place frame of a call) is not involved: the body is checked at its own generic call, whose frame is the closure's frame.
4. *What "smaller" is, and where it comes from*: the rank of the decreasing argument (`decRank K gR [m] = semRank m`) against the bound `b`. The body's step at stage `b + 1` starts from `ρ σ0 = a` with `semRank a < b + 1` (an entry condition on the assignment, which the knot's step supplies); in world `S` the leaf's `Pre` gives the refinement `ρ' σ0 = S(ρ' σ1)` (`TypedAssignment.refs`) and `Ext.agree` keeps `ρ' σ0 = a`, so `m < a ≤ b`. The checker's recursion check is what makes this true in general (`FixKnot.recDecreases`, open: an argument that is a strict subterm of the expanded entry means a part of the entry's value); for `R` the refinement record alone gives it, so no fact about [Rec] is assumed.

What this costs the column: the context per stage (`K_b` instead of `P`'s fixed `KP`), the entry condition on `ρ σ0`, and two checker facts of `P`'s shape (`SplitRInv`: the leaves; `FixRunR`: the acceptance inverted). Built: `Steps/RecR.lean` (the states), `Steps/RecGuards.lean`.

**The split's step at stage `b + 1`, proved** (`Steps/ColumnRRun.lean`, `Steps/ColumnRSplit.lean`; standard axioms). The context is `KR dm D Δ G b` (`cur = some ("R", b)`, `C.global "R" = G`) with `G = GR dm.read φ b fn`, the stage-`b` closure of `fn` at the weakened type (exactly `fixStage`'s `self` at stage `b + 1`), under `GoodBelow dm.read b fn` (`fn` defined and good at every number below `b`). `ColumnRRun`: `runCallR` (the reference's call `R(n.0)`: the head read in place, a copy, at its stored type; the argument's read; `callOf`), `runR` (the match at both tags), `runT_sealR`/`mean_vR` (the meaning of `⌈R(σ1)⌉`: the run of its term from the empty state is `callOf` of the context's `R` at `ρ σ1`). `ColumnRSplit`: `memBelow_GR` (`GlobalsOk.memBelow`'s content from `GoodBelow`), `callGR` (the stage closure called below the bound gives `fn`'s result at `Nat` and keeps the state, from `ClosureStage.stage_apply`), `GR_mem`, `ctxS_R`, `tySelf_of_rel` (the relation reads the function's own binding at `G`'s type), the leaves' `Pre` with `⌈R(σ1)⌉` in flight, and **`split_outR` / `split_simR`: `TailSim` for `R`'s body at stage `b + 1` from every state with `stR`'s kernel, every assignment with `ρ σ0 ≤ b` meeting `Pre`, every related reference state. ASSUMES 1 fact about the checker, `SplitRInv`; no model fact** (the outer induction hypothesis `GoodBelow` is a hypothesis of the step, discharged by the induction on the bound in the column). In world `S` the value agreement is one result read twice: `callGR` at the reference's state after the argument's read and at the empty state of the sealed program's run. One change to a definition was needed, and is a generalisation: parking a value in flight asked it `InertA`, whose `anyAtom` clause rules out every atom but numbers and unknowns, and so every sealed program; its only use is `MState.Occurs`, so `Value.InertO` (that one predicate) is factored out of it (`ColumnTemp.rel_withTemp_inertO`, `rel_withTempT_inertO`; `rel_withTemp_inertA` keeps its statement, through `InertA.toO`).

**The column, assembled** (`Steps/ColumnR.lean`, standard axioms). Decisions by kernel evaluation at `stR` (`parDecR`, including the sealed call's flags at the empty scope; `fixDecR`); start bundles at `stR` in the stage's context (`pre_stR`, `rel_stR`). `start_R`: `fixBodyAt`'s state at stage `b + 1` is `SM'` of `stR` under `σ0 ↦ a` in the context `KR … (GR … b fn) b` (as `start_P`). `fixBodyAt_R`: at every `a ≤ b`, the body inside the `.fix` clause with `self` the stage-`b` closure of a function good below `b` returns a number and no final, in ANY context of `R`'s shape (the run is computed by `runR`; world `S` by `callGR`). **`goodBelow_stg` (the knot): every stage `b` of the reference's closure (`stgR`, `fixStage` under the `.fix` clause's context) is good below `b`**, by induction on `b`, the step being `fixStage`'s own definition and `fixBodyAt_R`. **`column_R_run`: the `.fix` run of `R` from the empty state succeeds and the closure is a member of `Π(n : Nat). Nat`; NO assumed fact**, checker or model (membership through `closure_mem_of_total'` at the guarded fixpoint). **`column_R_ff`** (from every successful `checkTail` of the body from `stR`'s kernel with a continuation keeping the counters in world `Z`, at the fuel-free table there): (1) `column_R_run`, and (2) at EVERY stage `b`, `TailSim` for the body in the context whose `R` is the reference's own stage-`b` closure, from every assignment with `ρ σ0 ≤ b`, the step's induction hypothesis discharged by the knot; ASSUMES 1 fact about the checker, `SplitRInv` (for (2)); no model fact. **`column_R_def`** (from `checkDef defR`'s acceptance): the same, ASSUMES 2, `FixRunR` and `SplitRInv`. As with `I`, `J` and `P`, the closure's membership needs no checker fact, because the reference's run of this body is computable from the decisions alone; what the simulation adds (and what a body that continued after the call would need) is the relation at each leaf, now at every stage of a recursive function.

### D135. The frame relation asks lent sites of held labels only; `frameRelS_start`'s misfit removed (seventeenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead17`)

**The misfit** (D132): `FrameJ2.frameRelS_start` asked every lent site of the caller state `s₂`, for EVERY label, to lie below the callee's frame, which a step in the form cannot supply: `Rel` relates live labels' sites only, and nothing in `Rel` or `SGood` constrains a dead label's sites (a label held nowhere keeps its sites: `SState.endLabel` does nothing to it). The requirement came from `FrameRel.lentTop`/`lentImg` (`FrameProp.lean`), which related the two sides' top-frame sites for every label, and from the label-free proof's record `FrameFrag.Recs.topLent`.

**What the proof uses** (checked clause by clause): the label-free frame property reads sites only through [Access] (`access_self`, `accessOk_self`: the rounds go over `liveLabels`), and otherwise re-establishes the clauses at the end (`lf_end`) from the record at the start (`lf_start`). So the weakening is what the proof uses. **Changed:** `FrameRel.lentTop` and `lentImg` are asked of the in-place state's HELD labels only (`∃ p, s.shape p = some l`); `Recs.topLent` likewise; `access_self`/`accessOk_self` take non-reach for live labels only; `lf_noReach` concludes it for live labels; `frameRelS_start`'s `hlent` is for held labels only. "Held" rather than "live" (a holder at a position of the state) because `Recs` compares the current state's holders with the call-time state's, whose positions differ; on states whose holders sit at real positions (`SState.RInv`) the two notions agree. `frame_J2`/`frameJ2_holds`, `run_frameS_lf`, `closureRun_J2`, `Regress/FrameCheck`, `Strict/FrameLocCE` rebuilt with their statements (the last two with a binder added where they build the clauses); standard axioms. At `J2` the caller after the argument's read has no holder at all (`Rel` with a borrow-free checker state, `liveLabels_of_rel`), so `hlent` holds vacuously.

### D136. `J2`'s own closure of `I` at `J2`'s one table: member, and its call computed, with no assumed fact (seventeenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead17`)

Items (4) and (5) of D132's list. `I`'s meaning in `J2`'s context is `GlobalsLocal.fJ` (the meaning of the closure value `.clo [] fixI []`, `GlobalsOk.fn`). At `J2`'s table (aligned with the checker at `J2`'s start, `stJ2`, which is `stJ`), every decision its body reads is computed by kernel evaluation (`parDecJ2`, `flags_bodyI_J2`, the read's copy decision; `fixDecJ2`), so (`Steps/CallJ2Fn.lean`, standard axioms, NO assumed fact): `runT_bodyI_ref` (`S(#0)` from any state reading like `I`'s frame, `ColumnP.RefP`: `a + 1`, `n` moved); `refP_closureJ2`; `fixBodyAt_J2` (the `.fix` clause's body run at stage 1 starts from `closureJ2 a`, the state `closureRun_J2` relates to the checker's untyped run, and returns `a + 1`); `gJ2_run`, `gJ2_tot`, `gJ2_mem`; `fJ_eq_J2` (`fJ` is the closure of `gJ2`: the `.fix` clause's strict test is that membership); **`fJ_mem_J2`** (`GlobalsOk.mem`'s content for `I` in `J2`'s context, at `J2`'s own table: D130's `fJ_mem` needed `I`'s table and column `I`'s checker facts, so the one-table question of D130 and D132 is closed) and **`callOf_fJ_J2`** (the call at any `a` gives `a + 1` and keeps the state). With D135 these discharge both hypotheses of `frameRelS_start` at `J2` (`f` a member; no holder in the caller). What is left of `J2`'s step: the step for the call `I(x)` in the form (the reference's side is `runT_callJ2` + `caller_of_rel` + `callOf_fJ_J2`; the checker's is `CallJ2Inv`; the value agreement either by `callOf_fJ_J2` and the checker value `S(S(Z))`, or, as the general call step will, by `closureRun_J2`, whose hypotheses are now all available: `pre_stCJ2`, `rel_calleeJ2`, `lazyNum_stCJ2`, `frameRelS_start`, `flags_bodyI_J2`, `copyDec_calleeJ2`); then the `let` around it.

### D137. The first block the checker closes off, column `Q`: pinned; the form can say it; the model reads every closed-off block that takes a place as `err` (eighteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead18`; RULING ASKED)

**The program.** `def Q (n : Nat) : Nat := S(match n { Z => Z, S m => m })` in core terms, `S(match #0 { Z => Z, S _ => #0.0 })` (`Steps/BlockQ.lean`: `bodyQ`; the match is `P`'s body, `matchQ`). The surface's `S(…)` is a call of `succFn` (D124); the core term former `S` is used. The match is the argument of `S`, so the checker cannot split there; nothing smaller puts a match on an unknown outside tail position.

**What the checker does** (`Steps/BlockGuards.lean`, every `#guard` runs it, from `Q`'s start state `stQ`, `stP` with `Q`'s name and global; `checkDef defQ` accepts `Q`). `checkTail`'s general case runs the body's typed `eval`; `S(…)` runs the match with no hint; the match on `σ0` in a typed run is [Split] for a non-tail match, `splitThenClose`, then [Block], `closeBlock`: (1) the captures, from syntax and declared types before anything runs (`blockCaptures`, K1; `ckBlockCaps` gives `([n], [(n, move)])`): `n` BY MOVE, because the arm `S _` moves `n.0` and the static join moves `n`; (2) [Entry] accesses them (a no-op here); (3) each arm is checked in its own world: world `Z` (`σ0 := Z`, the arm `Z : Nat`), world `S` (a fresh `σ1 : Nat`, `σ0 := S(σ1)`, the arm's read moves `n.0`, `σ1 : Nat`), each arm's return checked (`bodyReturns`); the worlds' refinements and moves are undone at their exits, but the counters are KEPT: after the block `σ1 : Nat` is still in the table of unknowns, named by nothing; (4) the block's type is the arms' common type, `Nat`; (5) the block is the anonymous closure `anonQ = .clo [] (fix _ (n : Nat) : Nat := matchQ) [(.other, Nat)]` (its body the match with the capture renumbered to its parameter, here `matchQ` itself), called UNTYPED on the place `n`: the argument's read moves `n` and gives `σ0`; the callee's body is stuck on `σ0`, so the call closes off: the value is the sealed program `⌈anonQ(σ0)⌉` (`vQ`) at `Nat`; (6) no other unknown, no label, no refinement; the state after is `endQ` (`n ↦ ⊥`, unknowns `[Nat, Nat]`). The body returns `S(⌈anonQ(σ0)⌉) : Nat` and ends at `endQ`. Entry conditions, guarded: the same at either tail flag, any hint, a handed-down reading, inside a private copy; in `clone`'s mode the block's argument read COPIES `n` (`n ↦ σ0` after), so the plain mode is assumed; an untyped run of the match fails (stuck: the enclosing call closes off); entered with the counter of unknowns at 2, world `S`'s unknown is `σ2` and the table ends at three. Pinned for the columns after `Q` (not in `Q`): a place an arm WRITES is captured by `&`, the block's call consumes that borrow and the place is left holding a sealed fill (`⌈let c1 = …; anon(σ0, &c1); c1⌉`); a write through a borrow `y` live across the block captures `*y` by `&` (a reborrow), and `y` stays live.

**What the reference does** (`Steps/ColumnQRun.lean`, `Steps/ColumnQMean.lean`, standard axioms, no assumed fact). It does not close off. `runT`'s `.matchNat` clause not in tail position: [Access] for the tag, [Entry] (`matchEntry`: the captures read as the decision `Decisions.blockCaps`, each accessed; `entryQ`: a no-op on a state with no live label, strict-OK, and the lending after the arm a no-op for a result with no label), the ONE arm the tag selects, the join's drop of `n`. `runMatchQ`: on `0` the result `0`, on `a + 1` the result `a`, and in both `n` moved (`dropN_setN0`: the arm's move of `n.0` is overwritten by the join's drop). `runBodyQ`: `S(ρ σ0 - 1)` at `Nat`. The decisions are the checker's at `stQ` by kernel evaluation (`parDecQ`), but for the block's captures: `DecTab` does not hold them (they read values) and the kernel does not evaluate `ckBlockCaps` (`blockCaptures` sorts with `Array.qsort`), so they are the decision `ParDecQ.caps`, guarded (compiled) against the checker.

**The step in the form, as it would be stated** (no change of form needed). `Out` for the match's typed `eval` from a state with `stQ`'s kernel: (a) the reference's run is `runMatchQ`'s; (b) `Ext`: `ρ'` agrees with `ρ` at `σ0` and gives world `S`'s leftover `σ1` ANY number (it occurs nowhere after the block; `ρS ρ 0`), `Δ'` gives it `Nat` (`ΔPS`); (c) the value agreement: the meaning of `⌈anonQ(σ0)⌉` at `ρ'` must be the reference's result; (d) the relation after: the checker's `endQ` (`n ↦ ⊥`) with `vQ` in flight against the reference's `dropN s` (`n` moved): one `botWrite_rel` at `n` after a `rel_transfer` that adds `σ1` to the table; (e) borrows live across the block: none in `Q`. What (c) should be, and is in the model's code path: the sealed program runs `anonQ(σ0)` from the empty state; the head is the closure value, whose `.fix` (no capture) forms the closure of `gA` (`runT_anonFix`; a member of `Π(n : Nat). Nat`, `gA_mem`); the call runs `matchQ` in tail position in the closure's frame `[{}, {_ ↦ stage 0, n ↦ ρ σ0}]`, which is `FrameJ2.closureJ2`, so `ColumnP.runP` computes it: `ρ σ0 - 1` (`gA_run`, `callOf_A`), the same arm of the same code on the same number as the reference's direct run, at every assignment.

**FINDING: the model reads the checker's block value as `err`, so (c) is false at every assignment.** The checker's closure value `.clo caps t rd` keeps in `rd`, by design, "the declared types of the frame slots its code does not declare: each capture's, and for a stuck block each PARAMETER's, the place it takes" (`Syntax.lean`, D80, D82; the checker reads the parameters' readings as `rd.drop caps.length`, `Machine.lean` at its two call sites). A block has no capture of its own and one `rd` entry per place it takes: `anonQ` is `.clo [] … [(.other, Nat)]`. The model's `meanV` `.clo` clause (and `.tPi`'s) reads `rd` as the captures' types only and asks `CapsOk (capsVals caps) (capsTys rd)`, which is `False` when the lengths differ. So `anonQ` means `err` (`ColumnQMean.meanV_anonQ_err`, kernel-checked), its strict `.val` fails, and `⌈anonQ(σ0)⌉` means `err` (`mean_vQ_err`), while the reference's result is a number. Every closed-off block that takes a place means `err` in the model; no step in which a block closes off can be proved. A difference no user could want. **Asked of the lead (options):** (A, minimal, recommended) `CapsOk` checks the captures against the first `caps.length` entries and ignores the rest (`| [], _ => True`; `capsState` already zips, so the frame is unchanged); with it `meanV anonQ` is `runT_anonFix`'s closure and `⌈anonQ(σ0)⌉` means `ρ σ0 - 1`. (B, the full mirror) the `.clo` clause also hands `rd.drop caps.length` to the `.fix` run as the parameters' declared readings, as the checker does, in place of the decision `paramDecls` on the domain terms; needed only where a place's declared reading differs from the reading of its type's value (not in `Q`). (C) the checker keeps a block's parameters' readings outside `rd` (a change on main). Until ruled, `Q`'s step is stated but not proved.

**Ruled (orchestrator): (A) now, (B) recorded as the endpoint, (C) refused** (the checker freezes a closed-off block's parameter readings on purpose: the block's later run decides from what was declared, not from what a run produced). **(A) built** (`Run.lean`'s `CapsOk`: `| [], _ => True`, the captures checked against the first `caps.length` readings, the rest not read). One proof read the two lengths as equal: `Strict/Typed.lean`'s `HWTCaps.nil` asked `HWTCaps [] []`; what it needed was only that the captures be typed, so `nil` now allows any trailing readings (`hwtCaps_of` loses its mismatched-length case). No run changes; every module of `OchrMeaning` builds (Witness and Regress included, no expected state changed); `Strict.fund_all`, `column_C_free`, `column_B_free`, `frag_run_free`, `column_I_def`, `column_J_def4`, `column_P_def`, `column_R_run`, `column_R_def`, `frameJ2_holds`, `run_frameS_lf` keep their statements and the three standard axioms. The two `err` lemmas are replaced by their corrected statements: `ColumnQMean.meanV_anonQ` (the block's closure value means the closure of `gA`), `runT_sealQ`, **`mean_vQ`: `⌈anonQ(σ0)⌉` means `ρ σ0 - 1` wherever `ρ σ0` is a number, the reference's direct result (`runMatchQ`); no assumed fact.** **(B), decided, not built**: the `.clo` clause hands `rd.drop caps.length` to the `.fix` run as the parameters' declared readings in place of the decision `paramDecls` on the domain terms. (A) alone is wrong exactly where a block takes a place whose DECLARED reading (`placeDecl`, frozen in `rd`) differs from the reading of its type's VALUE (`typeDecl` of `.val T`, which `paramDecls` gives): e.g. a place whose declared type is a stuck type function call at a proposition sort, read `.prop` from the declaration, while its value `⌈TP(σ0)⌉` reads otherwise. The candidate program is main's `FrozenReadings.KeepTP` (`(let r = match k { Z => h, S _ => h }; r)` with `h : TP(n)`, accepted); NOT YET pinned as an executable regression (the next lead's first item: assert the reading `closeBlock` stores for `h` and what `paramDecls [] [.val T]` gives, and mark it as the test (B) must pass; if they agree on every accepted program, (B) is dropped).

### D138. The first functions with a borrow parameter, columns `W` and `K`: pinned; the model's body state is related to the checker's by no relation, because the cell is not recorded as the label's site (eighteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead18`; RULED (A), BUILT by the nineteenth lead, D140)

**The programs.** `def W (r : &Nat) : Nat := clone(*r)` reads through its borrow parameter; `def K (r : &Nat) : &Nat := r` returns it (`Steps/BorrowPar.lean`; core bodies `clone(*#0)` and `#0`, domain `&Nat`). The smaller `W (r : &Nat) : Nat := *r` is REJECTED ("[D53] a borrow ends while its content is partly moved out": the read moves the content out and the parameter's borrow ends at the return over it), so `clone` is the smallest accepted read through the parameter.

**What the checker does** (`Steps/BorrowParGuards.lean`, every `#guard` runs it). The body's start state (recorded, frames of sizes `[1, 2]`) has `stW`'s / `stK`'s kernel: frame 0 the CELL `r° ↦ loan₀` at the content type `Nat`; frame 1 the function's binding of itself, marked (`K`'s read `.pi (.ref .other)`), then `r ↦ borrow₀ σ0` at `&Nat` read `.ref .other`; the label counter at 1, the unknowns `[σ0 : Nat]`; typed, plain mode. `W`'s body returns `σ0 : Nat` and leaves the state as it was (`clone` reads in place; `r` keeps the borrow); `K`'s returns `borrow₀ σ0 : &Nat` and leaves `r ↦ ⊥` (the read of a borrow variable moves it), the cell untouched; the same at every hint and tail flag and under a handed-down reading, and in tail position (`checkTail`: one leaf). In `clone`'s mode `K`'s read copies the borrow. Stated for the machine lane: `BodyWInv`, `BodyKInv` (each body's typed `eval` from the start kernel: value, type, end kernel).

**What the model does at the start** (`RunCongr.fixBodyAt`, `fixStep`, D126 stage 2): frame 0 the cell `r° ↦ ψ l` at the content type, the top frame `self` then `r` holding the argument `a`, shaped with the fresh label `l = 0`, `heldMem l = a` (so the typed future gives the label its argument: the record), `heldTy l = Nat`. It does NOT record the cell as `l`'s loan site: `lent l = []` (`fixStep` writes `env`, `shape`, `nextLoan`, `heldMem`, `heldTy` only; `BorrowParStart.startS_lent`).

**FINDING: no reference body state `fixBodyAt` builds for a borrow parameter is related to the checker's start state** (`BorrowParStart.rel_start_false`, kernel-checked, for every context, assignment, future and renaming): the checker's cell `loan₀` IS label `0`'s one site (`Env.sitesOf stW.env 0 = [⟨(0, 0), [], loan⟩]`, label `0` live), and `Rel.sites` asks the two sides' sites of a live label to be the same. So the step for `W`'s or `K`'s body cannot start, and the column's membership argument (the body's run from `fixBodyAt`'s state) has no simulation to rest on. The other clauses of `Rel` at that state were read, not proved: the cell means `ψ 0` on both sides, `r` means `a` = `ρ σ0`, the holder is label `0` on both sides (`π 0 = 0`), the record `a` is a member of `Nat`. **Asked of the lead (options):** (A, recommended) `fixStep` records the cell as the label's loan site (`setLent l [⟨cell, [], loan⟩]`), the checker's shape exactly; run-neutral inside a body (no place resolves into frame 0; [End] re-roots sites, it does not read them to write), but it touches `run_fix_def`'s pieces, the congruence lemmas over `fixStep`, and the frame property's `FrameRel` (whose cells are read from frame 0); (B) `Rel.sites` excuses sites in frame 0 below the top frame (an exception in the relation for one representation seam; not recommended).

**What the statements will say once the start is related** (not built). The body's step: `Out` for the body's typed `eval` from `stW`'s kernel, the reference's run of `clone(*r)` reading the holder's content in place (`σ0` means `ρ σ0`, the state unchanged; for `K`, the read moves the borrow: the result labelled `π 0`, the holder `r` moved, the label still live with the value in flight). Membership in `Π(r : &Nat). …` (`closure_mem_of_total'`) asks, at each member argument `a`, the body's run to succeed with a member of the codomain and exactly ONE final, the cell's content after the frame pop, a member of `Nat` (`OutGood`). The end of the body: the pop ends every label at or above the start but a returned one, each after its strict end test (`EndsOk`); for `W` label `0` ends and the cell reads the holder's content (`a`): the final is `a`; for `K` the codomain is a borrow, so the returned label `0` is kept, its holder is the caller's result, and the cell's final is the future at `0`, the content when the caller ends the borrow.

**Ruled (orchestrator): (A)**; (B) refused (an exception in the relation is a seam; the checker's cell really is the site). To be built on `spine-lead18` as its own commit, after D137's: `fixStep` records the parameter's cell as its label's one loan site; `Strict.fund_all` and the named columns keep their statements and the standard axioms; then `rel_start_false` becomes the positive statement (the checker's start state for `W` and for `K` related to `fixBodyAt`'s, every clause of `Rel` proved). NOT BUILT by the eighteenth lead (context exhausted after D137's change); the first item of the next lead after D137's regression. Recorded so nobody re-derives it: `W (r : &Nat) : Nat := *r` is REJECTED (a number cannot be moved out through a borrow, D53), and `clone(*r)` is the smallest read through the parameter.

### D139. `J2`'s call: the call's inversion restated as a disjunction; the reference's run and the value agreement proved in both branches (eighteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead18`; the orchestrator's ruling for lead18)

**Ruled (orchestrator):** where a call's checker fact would need "the callee's body never ends stuck" to pin the result, it is not stated; the call's inversion is a disjunction, the result being the body's value or the sealed call, because the sealed call means the same closure applied (column `R`). **Restated:** `CallJ2.CallJ2Inv`'s last conjunct is now "the body ran untyped from `stCJ2`'s kernel to the value, OR the value is `sealJ2 = ⌈I(S(Z))⌉`" (weaker than before; its guards hold, the first disjunct at every guarded run). **Built** (`Steps/CallJ2Step.lean`, standard axioms): `callDecJ2` (the caller's decisions at scope `[.other]` and the sealed call's at `[]`, by kernel evaluation at `stJ2`); `runT_callJ2_J2` (from every reference state related to `stX1`, the strict run of `I(x)` in `J2`'s context returns `2 : Nat` and leaves `x` moved; NO assumed fact); `mean_sealJ2` (`⌈I(S(Z))⌉` means `callOf` of `J2`'s closure of `I` at `1`, `2`; no assumed fact); `mean_callJ2` (the checker's value of the call means `2` at every assignment, future and `Δ`, in either branch: the body's value is `S(S(Z))` by `BodyLazyInv` at `copyDec_calleeJ2`, or the sealed call; ASSUMES 2 facts about the checker, `CallJ2Inv`, `BodyLazyInv`; no model fact). **Left of the step `callJ2_out`**: `Ext` (nothing new: `stX`'s counters are `stX1`'s), `StrAgree` at `Nat`, the label (none on both sides), and `Pre`/`Rel` at `stX`'s kernel with the value in flight, in each branch: the numeral by `rel_withTempT_inertA` with `Num` and `InvNumA`'s push (or an `Inv` proof for `stX` with a numeral temporary, as `inv_stCJ2`); the sealed call by `rel_withTempT_inertO` and a concrete `Inv` (as `ColumnRSplit.inv_envT`). Then the `let` around it and `column_J2_ff`/`_def`.

### D140. D138 built: a borrow parameter's cell is its label's loan site; the start states of `W` and `K` are related, every clause proved; the frame relation lets the closure lend at its cells (nineteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead19`)

**The model change (D138's ruling (A)).** `fixStep` (`Run.lean`'s `.fix` `bodyAt` step and `RunCongr.fixStep`, kept identical, `run_fix_def` still `rfl`) now writes `setLent l [⟨cell, [], false⟩]` right after pushing the cell: the cell is the label's one LOAN site, as the checker's `x° ↦ loan l` is. No run reads it inside a body (no place resolves into frame 0, so no [Access] reaches it); it matters at an [End] of the parameter's label inside the body, where `relend` re-roots a reborrow's site at the parameter to the cell (before, to nowhere), and in the relation. The ripple was small: `RunCongr`'s forming invariant does not read loan sites (`formInv_setLent`, `pushBindShaped_setLent`, `takeLabel_setLent`, `setAt_setLent`; `fixStep_inv` takes one more rewrite). Every module of `OchrMeaning` builds; `Strict.fund_all`, `column_C_free`, `column_B_free`, `frag_run_free`, `column_I_def`, `column_J_def4`, `column_P_def`, `column_R_run`, `column_R_def`, `frameJ2_holds`, `run_frameS_lf`, `mean_vQ` keep their statements and the three standard axioms.

**Expected states changed** (each the state a body starts or ends in, now with the cell's site): `Regress/CallInPlace`'s `SB` (`Inc`'s frame) and `SBs` (`Id1`'s) gain `lent 0 = [⟨.bind 0 0, [], loan⟩]`, and `SB2` (`Id1` after `&*x`) keeps it beside the reborrow's site; `reached_SB`, `access_SB`, `reached_SBs`, `access_SBs` are now stated at the position the bodies access (`.bind 1 1`, the parameter) instead of every position (they were false at the cell). No other Witness or Regress file changed.

**A restatement: the frame relation's `lentCl`** (`FrameProp.FrameRel`, the open frame property's relation between the in-place state and the closure's). It said every site the closure lends at is in its own frame; with the cells as sites that is false at every call with a borrow parameter (`Regress/FrameCheck`'s `Id1`, kernel-checked). Now: in its own frame, OR at one of its cells (`∃ i < ks.length, x.pos = .bind 0 i`). Nothing reads the clause (`lf_end` and the regression construct it; no proof consumes it); the label-free fragment has no cells, so `FrameRelS` and `run_frameS_lf` are unchanged. The sites of a parameter label in place (the caller's place) and in the closure (the cell) are related by no clause, as before: neither run reads them, since the body names neither the caller's places nor frame 0.

**The positive start (replaces `rel_start_false`)** (`Steps/BorrowParStart.lean`, standard axioms, no assumed fact). `startS_eq`: `fixBodyAt`'s state for one borrow parameter at argument `a`, for any `fb` reading the parameter at `Nat` with reading `d`, is `SBW sb d a`: frame 0 the cell `ψ 0` at `Nat`; frame 1 `self` then `r ↦ a` at `Nat` labelled `0`; label `0` held at `.bind 1 1`, lent at `.bind 0 0`, recorded `a` at `Nat`; counter 1. `rel_startB`: for every checker state whose environment is the cell, a marked `self` at a Π-type, and `r ↦ borrow₀ σ0` (`envB`), with label counter 1, `Rel` holds with the identity renaming against `SBW (self) (.ref .other) m` under `σ0 ↦ m`, in the context `KB` (the function's global its stage-0 closure, as `ColumnI.KI`), at every future. Every clause proved: `skel` (the cell `(Nat, other)`, `self` at the context's type read `.pi cd` both sides, `r` `(Nat, ref other)`), `fresh` (label `0` below 1 on both sides), `sim` (the cell `ψ 0` both sides, `self` the stage-0 closure, `r` the number `m`: a borrow means its content; one holder, `r`, of label `0` both sides; no temporaries), `inj`, `sites` (label `0`'s one site the cell on both sides: the clause D138 repaired), `holders`, `records` (`m` whole and a member of `Nat`, the holder's stored type). `rel_startW`, `rel_startK`: the instances at `stW`, `stK` (`K`'s codomain read `.ref .other`). No second clause failed.

### D141. The bodies of `W` and `K` run by the reference; `W`'s closure a member; `OutGood` is false for every function that returns its borrow parameter (nineteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead19`; RULED: the final clause at the futures typed for the result; BUILT by the twentieth lead, `spine-lead20`)

**The reference's runs** (`Steps/BorrowParRun.lean`, standard axioms, no assumed fact; at decisions that classify the reads as neither statement nor proof, and, for `K`, read `r` as not copied). `W`'s `clone(*r)` from `SBW` at a number `m`: `m` at `Nat`, the state unchanged (`runT_bodyW`; [Access] at `r` reaches nothing, the only site being the cell; the read is at the typed future, which gives label `0` its record `m`, so `*r` reads `m`). `K`'s `r`: `m` at `Nat` labelled `0`, `r ↦ moved`, the borrow parked in a temporary holding label `0` (`runT_bodyK`, the state `SBK`). Inside the `.fix` clause (stage 1, formed in the empty state): `fixBodyAt_W` gives `⟨m, [m], no label⟩` (the pop ends label `0` after its strict test, the holder `r` holding `m`, a `Nat`; the cell then reads `m`); `fixBodyAt_K` gives `⟨m, [ψ ↦ ψ 0], label 0⟩` (the codomain is a borrow, so the returned label `0` is kept and nothing ends; the cell's final is the content the caller leaves in the returned borrow). **`closure_mem_W`**: `W`'s closure is a member of `Π(r : &Nat). Nat` (`Witness.fixClo_mem_nonrec`).

**FINDING: `K`'s closure cannot be shown a member of `Π(r : &Nat). &Nat`, and the reason is a statement, not the model.** `OutGood` (`Steps/Statements.lean`) asks each borrow parameter's final content to be a member of its content type at EVERY future. For a function that returns its borrow parameter the final IS the returned borrow's final content, `ψ 0`, a member only at futures that give label `0` a member. `not_outGood_K` (kernel-checked): `K`'s output is not `OutGood` at any number; `closure_ref_hyp_false_K`: so the hypothesis of the closure lemma at a returned borrow (`closure_mem_of_total_ref'`, and `stage_mem_ref` through it) is false for `K`'s function. What the closure's construction actually asks (`ClosureRef.RefLeafOk`: finals good at `φ.upd k v` for each member `v` left in the returned borrow, and the backward function injective) `K` meets (`outGoodRet_K`). The same `OutGood` sits in `OutGood'`, hence in `MemBelow` (`GlobalsOk.memBelow`, part of every step's `Pre` through `CtxS`) and in `Consistency.lean`'s per-stage obligations and `FixKnot`: for any global that returns its borrow parameter, those are false as stated. **Options:** (A, recommended) restate `OutGood`'s final clause as "at every future that gives the returned label (if the codomain is a borrow) a member of the codomain": for a data codomain this is today's clause; producers get an easier obligation; consumers that instantiate a future (`refLeafFrom_start` at `φ.upd k v`, `closure_apply_ref'`) supply the premise. (B) a second notion used only by the closure lemma at a returned borrow, `OutGood` kept; `MemBelow` and the consistency chain stay false for such functions, so (B) only defers (A).

**Ruled (orchestrator), 2026-11-22:** restate the final clause to quantify over the futures that are TYPED for what the function returns: every borrow label the result holds is given a member of the type it is held at; not a special case for "the codomain is a borrow", but half (I)'s typed futures applied to the labels live in the result; a data codomain gives exactly the old clause, a borrow codomain the one-label statement, and a result holding two borrows needs no third statement.

**Built (twentieth lead, `spine-lead20`; standard axioms).** The notion is defined once, beside `TypedFut` (`Run.lean`): `HeldTyped R held ψ` (each label of a list `held` of labels and types is given a member of its type); `typedFut_iff_held`: `TypedFut`'s second clause is `HeldTyped` at the state's live labels and their recorded types. The labels a body's result holds past the body are `BodyOut.held out args o` (`Steps/Statements.lean`): the returned label at the codomain's content type, i.e. the label the frame pop keeps live (`Run.lean`'s `.fix`, `keep`; at a data codomain a labelled result is ill-typed and its label is ended with the rest, so a data codomain's result holds none). `OutGood`'s final clause is now `∀ ψ, HeldTyped R (o.held out args) ψ → f ψ ∈ₛ[R] d.dom …`. At a data codomain this is the old clause (`heldTyped_data`); at a returned borrow it is the one-label statement. **A result holding two labels does not exist** in the checker or the model: a borrow sits inside no data (core D8, D48: the census in core DECISIONS rejects a constructor, a pair and a box of borrows) and `&` is only ever the whole declared type, so `&&Nat` is rejected too (`[D48]`, `#guard` in `Steps/BorrowParGuards.lean`); the model's result type `TyS` and `BodyOut.retLabel` hold at most one label. So the general form costs nothing over the one-label form and no gap is recorded. Producers and consumers: `OutGood'`, `MemBelow`, `GlobalsOk.memBelow`, the per-stage obligations (`Consistency.lean`, `FixKnot`) change by definition only; the consumers that read a final instantiate a typed future: `Closure.goodFrom_start` (a data codomain, at `φ`; takes `hdata`), `ClosureRef.refLeafFrom_start` (a returned borrow, at `φ.upd k v` for a member `v`, `heldTyped_upd`; takes `href`); `ClosureStage.stageFn_good`/`stageFn_good_ref` read the weakened codomain's held labels as the true one's below the bound (`held_weakCod`), `fill_outGood`'s finals are constant. Nothing else needed a change: the other producers (`I`, `J`, `J2`, `P`, `Q`, `R`, `W`, `Regress/DefKnot`, `CodeClosures`, `Strict/WitnessFix`) have no borrow parameter or a data codomain. `Strict.fund_all`, `column_C_free`, `column_B_free`, `frag_run_free`, `column_I_def`, `column_J_def4`, `column_P_def`, `column_R_run`, `column_R_def`, `column_W_ff`, `frameJ2_holds`, `run_frameS_lf`, `mean_vQ` keep their statements and the standard axioms. **`K`** (`Steps/BorrowParRun.lean`): `not_outGood_K` now refutes the OLD clause, stated locally (`OutGoodEvery`); `outGood_K`, `retGood_K`, `outGood'_K` (the obligation `MemBelow` asks, met); **`closure_mem_K`: `K`'s closure is a member of `Π(r : &Nat). &Nat` (`closure_mem_of_total_ref'`), no assumed fact**; it replaces `closure_ref_hyp_false_K`.

### D142. `W`'s body step in the form, and the column `W` with no assumed fact; `K`'s decisions (nineteenth lead, 2026-11-21; `spine-lead19`)

**The step** (`Steps/BorrowParStep.lean`, `body_simW`, `Sim` of `Steps/Form.lean`, the form unchanged). For every checker run of `clone(*r)`'s typed `eval` from a state with `stW`'s kernel (plain mode, no handed-down reading, decisions classifying the body as neither statement nor proof), every related good reference state runs `clone(*r)` to `ρ σ0` at `Nat`, the state unchanged, related again with `σ0` in flight.
